PrismHR Help — Onboarding and Benefits Enrollment

Onboarding & Benefits Enrollment

This file is a combined export of PrismHR help topics in Onboarding & Benefits Enrollment. Source help system: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Default.htm Each topic is separated by a heading and includes its original URL.

========================================================================

Assigning Administrative Roles

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Assigning_Admin_Roles.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Assigning_Admin_Roles.htm

Assigning Administrative Roles

There are many places in PrismHR that require administrator actions. For more information, see the System Administration Guide, Human Resources User Guide, or Approvals Guide.

Requiring Approvals

When setting up workflows for enrollments, you have the option to require that an administrator approve all enrollments. If you choose to do this, you must have at least one user or worksite trusted advisor for each of these roles.

  • If the user is a service provider, assign the role Benefit Manager in the Users form.

  • If the user is a worksite manager or worksite trusted advisor, assign the role Benefit Approver in the Users form.

You must also set up approval policies. Each approval policy specifies who must approve an action before it is finalized.

  • Use the Global Approval Policies form to define approval policies at the system level.

  • Use the Approval Policies form to override global approval policies at the client level.

Setting Proxy Enrollments

You can set up the following proxy enrollments:

  • Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors who are set up with the Enrollment Proxy user role can act as an employee and execute the employee’s enrollment selections using the Proxy Login form.

  • Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors who are set up with the Onboarding Proxy user role can read employees’ full onboarding workflows. (Note: The system bypasses any field-level security when using this role.)

Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors who are set up with the Enrollment Proxy user role can act as an employee and execute the employee’s enrollment selections using the Proxy Login form.Note: If the user is a Worksite Manager or Worksite Trusted Advisor, assign the Enrollment Proxy and Onboarding Proxy roles in the Users form.

========================================================================

Assigning Dependents to Group Plans

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Assigning_Dependents_Grp_Plans.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Assigning_Dependents_Grp_Plans.htm

Assigning Dependents to Group Plans

You must add validation on group benefit plan types by assigning them to the relationship codes that represent employee dependent relationships. You must also define the age limit for dependents and when the dependent’s age limit takes effect.

These settings allow the correct cost for the offered benefit plans, based on the employee’s dependent selection, to display during enrollments.

  • In the Group Benefit Plan Types form, set up the Minimum Dependents and Maximum Dependents.

If the benefit plan type requires that the employee’s spouse be set as a dependent, select Spouse Required or Domestic Partner Req’d on each Type Code.

Indicate whether the plan covers the employee’s regular dependents, the domestic partner’s children, or both using the Dependent Min/Max Type list.

  • In the Group Benefit Plans form, define the Dependent’s Age Limit and select when the benefit plan imposes an age limit from the list.

  • In the Relationships form, assign group benefit Plan Types to each Relationship Code.

========================================================================

Assigning Offer Types

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Assigning_Offer_Types.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Assigning_Offer_Types.htm

Assigning Offer Types

You must assign an offer type to each benefit plan offered to employees during benefit enrollments. The offer type classifies the benefit plan according to type (for example, Medical, Dental, Vision) and assures that the benefit plan displays in the correct form during benefit enrollments.

If benefit plans are not assigned offer types, you cannot include them in benefits enrollment.

To assign offer types on group benefit plans:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Group Benefit Plans. The Group Benefit Plans form opens.

To assign offer types on flexible spending account (FSA), health saving account (HSA), and commuter (COM) benefit plans:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Flex Spend Account Types. The Group Benefit Plans form opens.

Benefit Plans Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Benefit%20Plans%20Report.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Benefit Plans Report.htm

Benefit Plans Report

Use this form to generate a report that is run at the system level of all group benefit plans in the system. The report includes important plan setup information with the option to include specific G/L account information.

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

To access the form, click Reports menu. Under System|Report, click Benefit Plans Report.

To generate the Benefit Plans Report:

  1. Complete the following:

Field Name — Description

Termination Date Above or Equal to — Enter the date from which you want to run this report.

G/L Template ID — If you want G/L account numbers on the report, enter a G/L template here or click the G/L Template ID link and select it from the Select G/L Template dialog box.

  1. Click Run to generate the report. The Benefit Plans Report shows up on screen.

  2. Click Clear to clear the form at any time

  3. Click Close to close this form.

This report shows the following columns:

  • Benefit Plan ID

  • Benefit Plan Description

  • Insurance Class

  • Reconciliation Group

  • Cost Basis

  • Auto Enroll

  • Section 125

  • COBRA Eligible

  • Report on W2

  • Deduction Code

  • 125 Deduction Code

  • Bill Code

  • Debit G/L Account ID

  • Credit G/L Account ID

  • Termination Date

  • Client Sponsored

  • Doesn’t Meet Participation Requirements

Benefit Reconciliation Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Benefit%20Reconciliation%20Report.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Benefit Reconciliation Report.htm

Benefit Reconciliation Report

Use this form to generate a report to show benefit collections per coverage period based on benefits registers.

Note: Note:

  • Worksite Managers, Worksite Trusted Advisors, and Service Providers can access the form.

  • Client Access Group Security and Data Entity security is enabled on this form.

To access the form, either:

  • Click Reports. Under Benefits|Report, click Benefit Reconciliation Report OR

  • Under Work Centers, under Onboarding & Benefits, click Benefit Imports & Reporting. Under Benefit Reporting, click Benefit Reconciliation Report.

To generate the Benefit Reconciliation Report:

  1. Complete the following:

Field Name — Description

Coverage Year — Select the coverage year to report on from the drop-down. The default is the current year with a 10 year look back option.

Coverage Month — Select the coverage month to report on from the drop-down. The default is the month of January.

Benefit Plan ID — This field is optional. Leave this field blank for this report to run for all benefit plans. If you want to restrict this report to run for selected benefit plans, enter the benefit plan IDs here or click the Benefit Plan ID link and select them from the Select Benefit Plan dialog box.

Client ID — This field is optional. Leave this field blank for this report to run for all clients. If you want to restrict this report to run for selected clients, enter the Client IDs here or click the Client ID link and select them from the Select Clients dialog box.

  1. Click Run to generate the report. The report displays on-screen.

  2. Click Clear to clear the form at any time.

This report displays the following columns:

  • Plan ID

  • Plan Name

  • Client ID

  • Client Name

  • Employee ID

  • Employee Name

  • Billed

  • Deducted

  • Arrearage

Benefit Summary Statement

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Benefit%20Summary%20Statement.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Benefit Summary Statement.htm

Benefit Summary Statement

Use this form to generate a customizable Benefit Confirmation Statement that can be run for a single client and includes details related to benefits including: benefit enrollments, dependents, coverage amounts, premium amounts. This report uses the information from the Benefit Summary Statement Setup form. The link to the Benefit Summary Statement Setup form is available on this form. This setup form needs to be completed for a client to run this report for the client.

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

To access the form, in the Work Centers panel, under Onboarding & Benefits section, click Benefit Imports & Reporting. Under Benefit Reporting click Benefit Summary Statement.

To generate the Benefit Confirmation Statement:

  1. Complete the following:

Field Name — Description

Benefit Summary Statement

Show all employees with coverages effective on this date — If you select this radio button, indicate the date

Show all employees whose coverage starts between — If you select this options, then provide a date range be providing a Start Date and an End Date. Start Date - Coverage for all employees that start from this date. End Date - Coverage for all employees that is up to this date.

Plan Year - Enter Plan Year Start and End date for display on the report only.

Start Date — Indicate the Plan Year start date here.

End Date — Indicate the Plan Year end date here.

  1. Click Generate Report to build the Benefit Confirmation Statements for the client. A job progress window shows the processing steps. Click Close to close the job progress window. The following links appears on the screen.

  2. Click the View Benefit Summary Report link to open the Benefit Confirmation Statement on screen. One employee record is displayed per page in the Benefit Confirmation Statement on screen. This link will not display unless you generate the report.

  3. Click the View Error Report link to review the error message if any. If there is no error then “No Errors Produced” is displayed before the View Benefit Summary Report link. This will not display unless you generate the report.

  4. Click Clear to clear the form at any time.

Following is a sample Benefit Confirmation Statement:

[Image: Benefit Confirmation Statement]

Setting Benefit Import Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Benefit-Import-Def.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Benefit-Import-Def.htm

Setting Benefit Import Parameters

Use the Benefit Import Maintenance form to set parameters for benefit imports. Benefit import definitions specify data file layout and provides a template to interpret the data. The import definition also has tables that translate data file codes into standard PrismHR codes.

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

To set benefit import parameters:

  1. From the Work Centers panel, click Benefit Imports and Reporting.

  2. From Benefit Imports, click Benefit Import Maintenance.

  3. Complete these fields as required:

Field Description
Import Definition ID / Import Definition Description Click the Import Definition ID link to select an existing import definition, or enter data to create a new import definition. For existing import definitions, the Import Definition Description fills in. For new import definitions, enter an import description.
File Format Options are Delimited or Fixed Length. Delimited is the default setting.
Delimiter Select Comma or Tab from the drop-down.
Header Row Set to Included to include a header row in your import definition file.
Terminate coverage from other plans in the same insurance class when a new enrollment is created in a medical, dental, or vision plan. Enable this to end coverage from other plans that are in the same insurance class when new enrollments are created in medical, dental, or vision plans.
Suppress billing adjustments for new enrollments and benefit terminations made during the benefit import. Enable this to suppress billing adjustments for new enrollments and benefit terminations made during the benefit import.
Suppress COBRA prompts when a benefit enrollment is terminated. Enable this to: - Suppress COBRA prompts when a benefit enrollment is terminated. - Prevent COBRA enrollee records from being created when benefits coverage is lost. > Note: Note: Do not enable if you need to administer COBRA for terminated benefits.
  • Suppress COBRA prompts when a benefit enrollment is terminated.
  • Prevent COBRA enrollee records from being created when benefits coverage is lost. | Special Program After Import | Enter a subroutine name to run immediately after the data file is sent from your PC to the host. Do not perform this without guidance from PrismHR Support. |

a. Click + to add an import field.

Note: Note: While importing data on the Benefit Import Maintenance form, you can identify employees by Employee ID, SSN, or Clock Number.

While importing data on the Benefit Import Maintenance form, you can now identify employees by Employee ID, SSN, or Clock Number.

  1. Set up translation tables.

  2. Click Save to update your import definition settings or click Save as New to reuse existing settings and save the definition under a new ID and description. The system displays a success message that all settings were saved.

You also have the option of deleting the import definition.

Translation Tables

Use translation tables to convert codes found in the data file to standard action codes that PrismHR software uses. External action codes are located in an external data file, and standard action codes correspond to external action codes.

Options include:

  • Action Translations: Enter action codes found in the external data file in this column. Enter the corresponding standard action codes in the next column.

  • Client Translations: Enter company IDs found in the external data file in this column. Enter the corresponding standard company IDs in the next column.

  • Plan Translations: Enter benefit plan codes found in the external data file in this column. Enter the corresponding standard benefit plan codes in the next column.

  • Plan Type Translations: Enter plan type (coverage levels) found in the external data file in this column Enter the corresponding standard plan types in the next column.

  • Network Translations: Enter network codes found in the external data file in this column. Enter the corresponding standard network IDs in the next column.

  1. In the Translation Tables dialog box, click Actions. The Benefit Import Actions Translation Table form opens.

  2. To indicate the action the system should take for each line of data, enter the External Action Codes found in the data file and the corresponding Standard Action Codes from the following table.

Code Name — Description

ADD — Creates a new benefit enrollment.

WAIVE — Waives coverage for an existing enrollment.

CHANGE — Changes the plan type/coverage of an existing enrollment.

TERM — Terminates an existing enrollment.

DEPEN+ — Adds a covered dependent to an existing enrollment. You can also use this to add covered dependents to a new enrollment, as long as the new enrollment occurs in the file before the line that adds the dependent.

BENEF+ — Adds a beneficiary to an existing enrollment. You can also use this to add a beneficiary to a new enrollment, as long as the new enrollment occurs in the file before the line that adds the beneficiary.

DEPEN- — Removes a covered dependent from an existing enrollment.

BENEF- — Removes a beneficiary from an existing enrollment.

ENROLLFSA — Creates a new flexible spending account (FSA) enrollment.

ENROLLHSA — Creates a new health savings account (HSA) enrollment.

TERMFSA — Terminates an existing flexible spending account (FSA) enrollment.

TERMHSA — Terminates an existing health savings account (HSA) enrollment.

CHANGEFSA — Changes the plan type/coverage in an existing flexible spending account (FSA) enrollment.

CHANGEHSA — Changes the plan type/coverage in an existing health savings account (HSA) enrollment.

Defining Enrollment Plans

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Benefits_Enrollment_Plan_Details.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Benefits_Enrollment_Plan_Details.htm

Defining Enrollment Plans

To set up information about available benefit plans for new hires and employees to view during their enrollments, upload required documents, and set up benefit plan dependencies, use the Benefits Enrollment Plan Details form.

To select what to do:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Benefits Enrollment Plan Details.

  3. In What would you like to do today? select an option:

  • Define Offer Type Level Setup. For more information, see Defining Offer Type Setup.

  • Define Plan Level Setup. For more information, see Defining Plan Level Setup.

The system opens the selected tab, but you can switch to another tab as needed. If you do this, the system prompts you to save or discard any unsaved changes.

Tracing Benefit Enrollment

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Benefits_Enrollment_Trace_Log.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Benefits_Enrollment_Trace_Log.htm

Tracing Benefit Enrollment

When an employee logs in and begins the enrollment process, the system determines the benefit plans to display and calculates the employee costs for each benefit plan by applying a series of checks on each form in the workflow. You can view a record of these checks in the Benefits Enrollment Trace form.

The Tracing tool is especially useful for troubleshooting and finding errors when you set up benefits and open enrollments. If a user made an error in setting up a workflow or a benefit plan, tracing provides a way to find the error. You can then make any needed corrections to enable employees to access the correct benefit plans and view correct calculations for the costs of the available plans.

The Benefit Enrollment Trace form displays data only for a form that is currently open and active in an enrollment. It can display details for only one form at a time.

Note: Note: You must be logged in to an enrollment using the correct employee credentials. Also, the form whose details you want to view must be open and active to use this tool.

Before you begin:

  • Verify that the correct client record is shown near the top of the form. If it is not, click it and then select the correct client. If the correct client is not selected, the system cannot access system checks for the client’s enrollments.

  • Log in to the enrollment using the employee’s login credentials, and open the form to view. The form opens in the enrollment.

To trace benefit enrollment:

  1. On the HR menu, select HR|View > Benefits Enrollment Trace.

  2. Enter the Employee ID and click Reload.

  3. The system checks (for the form that is open and active in the enrollment) are shown in the table.

You can click Download to save a text file of the trace log.

Defining Offer Type Setup

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/BEPD_Define_Offer_Type_Level_Setup.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/BEPD_Define_Offer_Type_Level_Setup.htm

Defining Offer Type Setup

In the Define Offer Type Level Setup panel, use the Comparison Fields and Offer Type Dependency tabs to set up benefit plan details at the offer type level. The system prompts you to select the offer type to set up and opens the selected tab. You can switch to another tab as needed. Before you switch to the other tab, the system prompts you to save or discard any unsaved settings.

Use this tab — To do this

Comparison Fields — Define the benefit plan information options employees can view for the offer type. Corresponding plan specific information is set up in Plan Details.

Offer Type Dependency — Set up dependencies at the offer type level. The system prompts you to select an offer type from the list. It applies the settings you define to all benefit plans that have that offer type selected.

Defining Employee Options

To define the benefit plan information options employees can view in all plans that have the selected offer type during enrollments, use the Comparison Fields tab. After you set up the options, you can set up the values in each individual benefit plan using the Details tab in the Define Plan Level Setup panel.

An employee can review this benefit plan information, including costs for employee and dependents, co-pays, deductibles, and so on. An employee can also compare information for up to three benefit plans within the offer type during enrollment.

To define the benefit options available to employees:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Benefits Enrollment Plan Details.

  3. In What would you like to do today? select Define Offer Type Level Setup > Comparison Fields.

  4. Select the Offer Type for the benefit plan to configure. The Comparison Fields tab displays.

A table is shown where you can enter the options for the information to include. You can enter as many options as needed. You can also import multiple fields using the Plan Offer Type Import form.

  1. Specify how to display the benefit plan information options.

Use this option — To define this

Display Comparison Fields — How to display the benefit plan information options.

…even if empty in all plans — All options entered are displayed, even if there is no corresponding value entered for them at the plan level in the Details tab in the Define Plan Level Setup panel. This is the default setting.

…only if filled in all plans — The only options displayed are those that have a corresponding value entered for them at the plan level in the Details tab in the Define Plan Level Setup panel.

Allow Waive in Benefit Enrollment — The option is allowed to waive coverage to display in all benefit plans that have the offer type selected. See Setting Up Employee Waive Options for more information about other system and client-level settings that affect employee waive options.

  1. Enter the following information for each benefit plan:
Use this option To define this
Description The field name to display during enrollments. Required.
Display Summary option Whether to include the field in the Display Summary during enrollments.
Display Comparison option Whether to include the field in the Display Summary during enrollments.

  • To modify the name of an option, click [Image: screenshot] (Edit).

  • To remove an option, click [Image: screenshot] (Delete).

  • To rearrange options, click and hold [Image: screenshot] (Rearrange) for an option and then drag the option higher or lower in the list. Note that when an option is moved, the values defined for the option on the Details tab of the Define Plan Level Setup panel do not move with the option and must be updated manually. For more information, refer to Defining Plan Level Setup.

  1. Select one of the following options:

  • To save settings, click Save.

  • To save settings and close the tab, click Save and Close.

  • To close the tab without saving settings, click Cancel.

  • To delete the record, click Delete.

Defining Dependencies

To set up dependencies at the offer type level, use the Offer Type Dependency tab. For example, if plans with the offer type Domestic Partner Medical (MDP) require the employee to enroll in a dependency plan with the offer type Medical (MED), the employee can select any available plan that has the MED offer type.

Tier-based dependencies further limit which benefit plans are available to employees during enrollments. The option to set up additional dependencies is available only in offer types that use tiers. It is not available in life insurance or disability benefit plans, which use coverage levels rather than tiers.

To set up dependencies for offer types:

  1. Open the Offer Type Dependency tab.

  2. Select the offer type to use as a dependency, and then click Add. The Offer Type Dependency is shown in the table.

  • You can add as many dependencies as needed. The type being viewed in the form requires enrollment in one of the specified dependencies.

  • To remove a dependency, click [Image: screenshot] (Delete).

  1. If necessary, specify an additional tier-based dependency.

Tier — Description

Exact Tier — Dependency plans must have a tier level that is an exact match for the tier or coverage level selected in the plan that requires the dependency.

Limited Tier — Dependency plans can have a tier level that includes the tier or coverage level selected in the plan that requires the dependency.

  1. Select one of the following options:

  • To save settings, click Save.

  • To save settings and close the tab, click Save and Close.

  • To close the tab without saving settings, click Cancel.

  • To delete the record, click Delete.

If you switch to another tab, the system prompts you to save any unsaved settings.

========================================================================

Defining Plan Level Setup

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/BEPD_Define_Plan_Level_Setup.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/BEPD_Define_Plan_Level_Setup.htm

Defining Plan Level Setup

In the Define Plan Level Setup panel, you can use the following tabs to set up benefit plan details for specific benefit plans offered during enrollments.

Use this tab — To do this

Plan Details — Enter plan specific information to go with the options set up in Comparison Fields for the benefit plans you offer to employees during enrollments.

Documents — Attach a Summary of Benefits Document and additional Supporting Documents to the benefit plan record.

Plan Dependency — Set up dependencies at the plan level.

Terms & Conditions — Enter content for terms and conditions that the user must acknowledge. This content displays for all confirmation forms

To set up plan details:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Benefits Enrollment Plan Details.

  3. In What would you like to do today? select Define Plan Level Setup > Plan Details.

  4. Select the Offer Type, Plan Description, and Effective Date for the benefit plan to configure. You can enter multiple effective dates as needed for the benefit plan selected in the Plan Description list. Each effective date creates a new record, allowing you to enter contribution information on the same benefit plan for different enrollment periods. You also have the following options:

  • To enter a new effective date for the plan, click New Date.

  • To copy all settings to a new effective date and create a duplicate record, click Copy Date.

Defining Plan Details

To enter plan specific information for the benefit plans offered to employees during enrollments, use the Details tab.

To enter specific information about a benefit plan:

  1. Select the Details tab.

For this option — Define this information

Field — The benefit plan information options for the offer type set up in the Comparison Fields tab in the Define Offer Type Level Setup panel.

Value — Corresponding information about the options.

Additional Plan Details displays the following options:

Option — Description

PCP Lists — If benefit plans require primary care physician (PCP) information, the URL where employees can look up their PCP. Typically, these are health maintenance organization (HMO) medical plans, but some dental or voluntary benefit plans might also require this information. This list is shown only for benefit plans that have the Collect PCP Information option enabled in the Web Enrollments Parameters panel on the Group Benefit Plans form.

Participation Requirement — How to track carrier plan participation requirements at the system level to ensure client and employee enrollments abide by carrier guidelines. Percentages must be from 1 to 100%.

Plan Application — At the system level, you can enable employees to complete a carrier plan application through Benefits Enrollment, enter a website URL to a carrier application. When defined, the Plan Application link displays in the Required forms section of the benefit plan tile. When an employee clicks the link, the application opens in a new browser window. > Note: Note: This feature is only for the Medical (MED), Dental (DEN), and Vision (VIS) offer types.

Description Text — Enter a description for the plan application link.

Use aggregate spouse maximum — Whether the maximum coverage for the employee’s spouse should not exceed the aggregate coverage amount of an employee’s basic and additional life insurance plan selections. You must select Require Basic Life Enrollment if you select Use Aggregate Spouse Maximum.

Attaching Documents

To attach a Summary of Benefits document and additional supporting documents to the benefit plan record, use the Documents tab.

Life insurance and disability plans display an additional option to attach an Evidence of Insurability (EOI) form. If you attach an EOI form, you must also enter an Evidence of Insurability Message to display during enrollments.

Before you begin:

  • Upload files you plan to attach to the benefit plan to the Document Repository.

To attach documents to a benefit plan:

  1. Select the Documents tab.

  2. In Summary of Benefits Document, click Add and select a document from the search list that opens.

  3. In Supporting Documents, click Add and select a document to attach from the search list that opens. You can repeat this step to include additional supporting documents.

The names of the selected documents display in Summary of Benefits Document and Supporting Documents.

  • To update an attached file, click [Image: screenshot] (Edit), make changes if necessary, and then click Upload New Version.

  • To remove an attached file, click [Image: screenshot] (Delete).

Setting Up Plan Dependencies

To set up dependencies at the plan level, use the Plan Dependency tab. For example, a domestic partner medical plan that you select might require the employee to enroll in a specific medical benefit plan as well.

If this dependency is set up at the plan level, you can specify which benefit plans are required. The employee can select only those benefit plans.

To set up plan dependencies:

  1. Select the Plan Dependency tab.

  2. In Offer Type, select the offer type.

  3. In Plan Description, select the benefit plan to use as a dependency.

  4. Click Add. The specified plan displays as a dependency in the table.

  • You can add as many dependencies as needed. The benefit plan being viewed in the form requires enrollments in one of the specified dependencies.

  • To remove a dependency, click [Image: screenshot] (Delete).

  1. You can specify an additional tier-based dependency as needed. Tier-based dependencies further limit which benefit plans are available to employees during enrollments:

The option to set up additional dependencies is available only in offer types that use tiers. It is not available in life insurance or disability benefit plans, which use coverage levels rather than tiers.

Tier — Description

Exact Tier — Dependency plans must have a tier level that is an exact match for the tier or coverage level selected in the plan that requires the dependency.

Limited Tier — Dependency plans can have a tier level that includes the tier or coverage level selected in the plan that requires the dependency.

Defining Terms and Conditions

In Benefits Enrollment, employees cannot submit their enrollment without affirming the terms and conditions. If they decline, a warning message displays indicating what they must do to proceed. After submitting their enrollment, the affirmation displays on the Benefit Confirmation Statement.

To enter content asking employees to affirm or decline the terms and conditions provided, use the Terms & Conditions tab. This content displays for all confirmation forms, based on the plan offerings.

  1. Click the Terms & Conditions tab. Complete the following:
Use this option To define this
Display Terms and Conditions The Terms and Conditions as defined by the carrier for the plan.
Acknowledgment Question The employee and proxy question to show with the acknowledgment check box. - Example employee question: Have you read and understand the Terms and Conditions? - Example proxy message: Have you reviewed the Terms and Conditions with the employee? You can edit the default text in the text boxes.
  • Example employee question: Have you read and understand the Terms and Conditions?
  • Example proxy message: Have you reviewed the Terms and Conditions with the employee? | Acknowledgment Response | The employee and proxy affirmation and declination messages. - Example employee affirmation: I have read and understand the terms and conditions. - Example proxy affirmation: I have reviewed the terms and conditions with the employee. - Example employee declination: I either do not understand or am unable to review the terms and conditions at this time. - Example proxy declination: The employee either did not understand the terms and conditions or I was not able to review the content with the employee at this time. |
  • Example employee affirmation: I have read and understand the terms and conditions.
  • Example proxy affirmation: I have reviewed the terms and conditions with the employee.
  • Example employee declination: I either do not understand or am unable to review the terms and conditions at this time.
  • Example proxy declination: The employee either did not understand the terms and conditions or I was not able to review the content with the employee at this time.
  1. Select one of the following options:

  • To save settings, click Save.

  • To save settings and close the tab, click Save and Close.

  • To close the tab without saving settings, click Cancel.

  • To delete the record, click Delete.

If you switch to another tab, the system prompts you to save any unsaved settings.

Managing Underwriting Questions

In some cases, insurance companies require employees to complete underwriting questions to enroll in benefit plans. Underwriting questions determine whether an employee can enroll in a benefit plan based on the response. For example, when enrolling in a benefit plan, the system could ask an employee, “Does this coverage replace or change any existing insurance?” Based on the response, the system could deny employee enrollment. To configure required questions for enrollment, use the Underwriting Questions option when defining plan level setup.

Underwriting Questions are only available for the following offer types:

- LTD - Long Term Disability - ACC - Accident Benefits
  • LTD - Long Term Disability
  • ACC - Accident Benefits | - STD - Short Term Disability | - HOS - Voluntary Hospitalization |
  • STD - Short Term Disability
  • HOS - Voluntary Hospitalization | - LDA - Additional LTD | - IDT - Identity Theft |
  • LDA - Additional LTD
  • IDT - Identity Theft | - SDA - Additional STD | - LGL - Legal |
  • SDA - Additional STD
  • LGL - Legal | - CRI - Critical Illness | - TEL - Telehealth |
  • CRI - Critical Illness
  • TEL - Telehealth | - CAN - Cancer Benefits | - WLI - Whole Life |
  • CAN - Cancer Benefits
  • WLI - Whole Life To add underwriting questions:

  1. On the Underwriting Questions tab, click Add. The Add/Edit Underwriting Questions dialog box displays.

  2. Complete the following:

Use this option To define this
Question The underwriting question. The character limit is 500 characters.
Response Type The type of response: - Yes/No: The employee selects Yes or No. - Multiple Choice: The employee selects a multiple choice option. To add multiple choice options, click [Image: screenshot] . To delete multiple choice options, click [Image: screenshot] .
  • Yes/No: The employee selects Yes or No.
  • Multiple Choice: The employee selects a multiple choice option. To add multiple choice options, click [Image: screenshot] . To delete multiple choice options, click [Image: screenshot] . | Disclaimer | A disclaimer statement. The character limit is 500 characters. |
  1. Click Save.

After adding underwriting questions, you can:

  • Click [Image: screenshot] to move the question in the order of the list.

  • Click [Image: screenshot] to delete the question.

  • Click [Image: screenshot] to edit the question.

After adding underwriting questions to a benefit plan in PrismHR, they display in a BE workflow when electing plan coverage. The BE benefit plan tiles now have an Underwriting tab where employees can answer any necessary questions. If a benefit plan requires employees to answer underwriting questions, they cannot elect coverage until they answer.

After an employee answers underwriting questions for an elected benefit plan and submits their enrollment, the questions and responses display on the Benefit Confirmation Statement. The questions and responses also display on the employee benefits enrollment record in PrismHR. You can view this information on the Employee Benefits Enrollment form.

Importing Benefits

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/BIP_Benefits_Import_Process.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/BIP_Benefits_Import_Process.htm

Importing Benefits

The Benefits Import Process enables you to enroll employees in group benefit plans using the information in an external data file. The process requires you to set up a data file and a benefit import definition.

Before you begin:

  • Set up company benefits and benefit rules. For more information, see the Benefits Administration User Guide.

Setting Up the Data File

The data file must be either tab-delimited or comma-delimited. It must include the following columns of information, in any order:

  • Company ID

  • Employee ID, Employee SSN, or Employee Clock Number

  • Offer Type

  • Benefit Plan ID

  • Benefit Plan Type

  • Effective Date

  • Action Codes

========================================================================

Defining the Data Layout

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/BIP_Creating_the_Benefit_Import_Definition.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/BIP_Creating_the_Benefit_Import_Definition.htm

Defining the Data Layout

The benefit import definition defines the layout of the data file and provides a template to interpret the data. It also contains tables that let you translate codes found in the data file into standard PrismHR codes.

To define the data layout:

  1. Go to Work Centers >File >Benefit Imports & Reporting> Benefit Import Maintenance. The Benefit Import Maintenance form opens.
Use this option To define this
Import Definition ID The type of plan to import. The following definition IDs are for Benefits Enrollment only. They are already set up in the system with the correct import fields and translation tables and cannot be changed. - NGBEPOST: Imports group benefit plans into Benefits Enrollment - NGBEFSPOST: Imports flexible spending plans into Benefits Enrollment
  • NGBEPOST: Imports group benefit plans into Benefits Enrollment
  • NGBEFSPOST: Imports flexible spending plans into Benefits Enrollment | Import Definition Description | Text that describes the import. | | File Format | Select delimited or fixed-length format, then select tab-delimited or comma-delimited. | | Header Row | Select an option to include or exclude a header row with the import. | | | This will also prevent COBRA enrollee records from being created when benefits coverage is lost. You should not enter Y if you need to administer COBRA for terminated benefits. | | Special Program After Import | The name of subroutine to run immediately after the data file is sent from your computer to PrismHR. Do not use this option before consulting PrismHR Support. |

  1. Answer the following questions with either Y for yes, or N for no.

  2. Unless the PrismHR Customer Support Team instructed you to do otherwise, leave Special Program After Import blank.

To enter the import fields:

  1. In the Benefit Import Definition Maintenance form, click F5-Import Fields. The Benefit Import Fields Maintenance form opens.

  2. Enter the Field Names to include in the data file. The fields you enter define the headers and their order in the data file, as shown in the following tables.

  1. Click F2-Save. The system displays the Benefit Import Fields Maintenance form.

Entering Translation Tables

The system uses translation tables to convert codes found in the data file to the standard action codes that PrismHR uses. Each line of the data file must be mapped to an action code (set up in the Benefit Import Action Translation Table form).

The definition IDs NGBEPOST and NGBEFSPOST are already set up in the system with the correct translation tables. You cannot change these settings. If you enter any other definition ID, use the following procedures.

  1. In the Benefit Import Fields Maintenance form, click F6-Translation Tables.

  2. In the Translation Tables dialog box, select the translation table to set up:

  • Actions

  • Company

  • Plan IDs

  • Plan Types

  • Networks

First, you must specify the action codes found in the data file and map them to the standard action codes used by PrismHR. This is required.

Actions

  1. In the Translation Tables dialog box, click Actions. The Benefit Import Actions Translation Table form opens.

  2. To indicate the action the system should take for each line of data, enter the External Action Codes found in the data file and the corresponding Standard Action Codes from the following table.

Code Name — Description

ADD — Creates a new benefit enrollment.

WAIVE — Waives coverage for an existing enrollment.

CHANGE — Changes the plan type/coverage of an existing enrollment.

TERM — Terminates an existing enrollment.

DEPEN+ — Adds a covered dependent to an existing enrollment. You can also use this to add covered dependents to a new enrollment, as long as the new enrollment occurs in the file before the line that adds the dependent.

BENEF+ — Adds a beneficiary to an existing enrollment. You can also use this to add a beneficiary to a new enrollment, as long as the new enrollment occurs in the file before the line that adds the beneficiary.

DEPEN- — Removes a covered dependent from an existing enrollment.

BENEF- — Removes a beneficiary from an existing enrollment.

ENROLLFSA — Creates a new flexible spending account (FSA) enrollment.

ENROLLHSA — Creates a new health savings account (HSA) enrollment.

TERMFSA — Terminates an existing flexible spending account (FSA) enrollment.

TERMHSA — Terminates an existing health savings account (HSA) enrollment.

CHANGEFSA — Changes the plan type/coverage in an existing flexible spending account (FSA) enrollment.

CHANGEHSA — Changes the plan type/coverage in an existing health savings account (HSA) enrollment.

  1. Press F2-Save. The system returns you to the Translation Tables dialog box.

Company

  1. In the Translation Tables dialog box, click Company. The Benefit Import Company Translation Table form opens.

  2. To denote the company, enter the External Company Code found in the data file and the corresponding Translated Company Code.

  3. Press F2-Save. The system returns you to the Translation Tables dialog box.

Plan IDs

  1. In the Translation Tables dialog box, click Plan IDs. The Benefit Import Plan Translation Table form opens.

  2. To denote the benefit plans to include, enter the External Plan ID found in the data file and the corresponding Translated Plan ID.

  3. Press F2-Save. The system returns you to the Translation Tables dialog box.

Plan Types

  1. In the Translation Tables dialog box, click Plan Types. The Benefit Import Plan Type Translation Table form opens.

  2. To denote the benefit plan types or coverage levels, enter the External Plan Type found in the data file and the corresponding Translated Plan Type.

  3. Press F2-Save. The system returns you to the Translation Tables dialog box.

Networks

  1. In the Translation Tables dialog box, click Networks. The Benefit Import Network Translation Table form opens.

  2. To denote the benefit networks to include, enter the External Network ID found in the data file and the corresponding Translated Network ID .

  3. Press F2-Save. The system returns you to the Translation Tables dialog box.

  4. Click Exit. The system returns you to the Benefit Import Definition Maintenance form.

  5. Click F2-Save.

========================================================================

Importing Benefit Rules

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/BIP_Using_the_Benefit_Rules_Import_Definition.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/BIP_Using_the_Benefit_Rules_Import_Definition.htm

Importing Benefit Rules

Use the following procedures to create the benefit rules import file and to import benefit rules information into PrismHR from a tab-delimited text file.

Note: Note: You can download data import template files for importing benefit rules information. Log on to the Customer Resource Center and search for import template file for more information.

Creating the Benefit Rules Import File

The following table shows the layout of the tab-delimited text file to use to import benefit rules.

XLS Field Name Field # Type Length Req? Description
A ACTION 1 A 6 R Enter the action code for this import (Add, Change, or Term only).
B COMPANY.ID 2 A 7 R A valid COMPANY.MASTER item.
C GROUP.ID 3 A 8 R Needs to be a valid BENEFIT.GROUPS item
D PLAN.ID 4 A 8 R Needs to be a valid BENEFIT.PLANS item that is also set up with a COMPANY.BENEFITS item.
E NETWORK.ID 5 A 8 O Must be a valid network ID for a Plan where Billing Rates vary by Network
F EFF.DATE 6 D 10 R MM-DD-YYYY format
G SEQ 7 N 5 R Numeric integer starting with 1 and incremented by 1 for each additional row. Columns 1 thru 6 need to be repeated for each sequence.
H CONTRIB.BY 8 A 1 R - E - Employee - C - Client - Null Value
- E - Employee
- C - Client
- Null Value
I TAKE.ON.XMPT 9 A 1 O Must be Y or N or Null value
J CONTRIB.PLAN.ID 10 A 8 O Must be a valid BENEFIT.PLANS item
K CONTRIB.RATE.GROUP 11 A 8 O Must be a valid Billing Rate Group RATE.GROUPS item
L PLAN.TYPE 12 A 4 O - E1 - Employee + 1 - EC - Employee + Child - EE - Employee - FAM - Family
- E1 - Employee + 1
- EC - Employee + Child
- EE - Employee
- FAM - Family
M CONTRIB.MTHD 13 A 2 O P - Percentage F - Fixed FP - Fixed (EE), Percent (DEP) PF - Family
N CONTRIB.AMT 14 N 8 R Must be a valid amount greater than zero.
O DEP.CONTRIB.AMT 15 N 8 R Must be a valid amount greater than zero.
P ELIG.STATUS 16 A 3 O Must be a valid Employment Status Class: A - Active L - On Leave T - Terminated
Q ELIG.AGE 17 N 2 O Must a numeric integer from 0 to 99
R ELIG.TYPE 18 A 3 O Must be a valid Employee Type Code EMPLOYEE.TYPE.CODES item
S ELIG.DAYS 19 N 3 O Must a numeric integer from 0 to 999
T ELIG.DAYS.FROM 20 A 1 O Must be ‘H’ or ‘S’ or a null value
U ELIG.FLSA.STATUS 21 A 1 O Must be ‘E’ or ‘N’ or a null value
V UPDATE.PLAN.TO.GROUP 22 A 1 0 Must be Y or N or a null value.
W DAYS.OR.MONTHS 23 A 1 0 Indicates days or months service until employees are eligible for plan (defines ELIG.DAYS): D - Days M - Months Null value - Plan does not base eligibility on service days/months..
X ELIG.DATE.USED 24 A 1 0 Eligibility date used: O - If calculated date is the first of the month, use that date. Otherwise, use the first day of the following month. F - First of month after calculated date. C - Use calculated eligibility date. Null value - No eligibility date calculation used for plan.
Y PLAN.APPLICATION.URL 25 The plan’s application URL.
Z PLAN.APPLICATION.DESC 26 The plan’s application description.
AA MULTIPLIER 27 The pay multiplier.
AB ROUND.METHOD.120 28 The rounding method.
AC ROUND.TIMING 29 Round before or after.
AD COVERAGE.MIN 30 Coverage minimum.
AE COVERAGE.MAX 31 Coverage maximum.
AF COVERAGE.AMT 32 Coverage amount.
AG ADD.TO.COV 33 Add to calculated coverage.
AH LIFE.YOS 34 Years of service for life.
AI LIFE.YOS.PAY.MULT 35 Pay multiplier for life.
AJ MULT.PAY.CODE 36 Multiplier pay basis.
AK PERCENT.BASE.115 37 Percentage base.
AL BENEFIT.AMT 38 The benefit amount.
AM LTD.YOS 39 Years of service for long term disability.
AN LTD.MAX.COVERAGE 40 Maximum coverage for long term disability.
AO LTD.BASE.CALC 41 Alternate base calculation for long term disability.
AP FROM.SALARY.D 42 From annual salary (for salary banded disability plans only).
AQ TO.SALARY.D 43 To annual salary (for salary banded disability plans only).
AR LTD.STD.COV 44 Coverage for short and long term disability (for salary banded disability plans only).
AS NETWORK.STOP.DATE 45 Network availability stop date.

See Importing Benefit Rules.

Importing Company Benefits

Importing Company Benefits

Bulk Launch Benefit Workflow

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Bulk-Benefit-Workflows.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Bulk-Benefit-Workflows.htm

Bulk Launch Benefit Workflow

This new form is a new bulk creation tool that can create enrollment records simultaneously, so information no longer needs to be added one record at a time. This is especially helpful for take-on clients. Service providers also now have the ability to launch life events in bulk. The form is called Bulk Launch Benefit Workflow and provides these options:

  • Employee record check boxes where you can select a single or multiple employees. You can also Select All employees with a check box.

  • Column sorting and filtering.

  • A record counter at the bottom of the table that displays the total number of items, the number of items displayed that are based on filtering, and the number of items selected or checked.

  • Data displayed in the table is pulled from the Employee Detail record.

  • Pre-filtering options for clients, employment type, and employment status.

  • Two action buttons exist on this form: Launch Life Event and Launch Benefit Enrollment.

  • View and download reports associated with benefit enrollment changes.

  • These columns of data are included in the table:

Column Name Description Filter Type
Client ID Name of the client. None
Employee ID ID for an employee. Type ahead search
Employee Name Name of an employee. Type ahead search
Hire Date The last hire date for this employee. Search range, from - through. Format is MM/DD/YYYY.
Employer Start Date The date the employee started with this employer. Search range, from - through. Format is MM/DD/YYYY.
Benefit Group Description of an employee’s benefit group. Drop-down search
Employment Status Status of an employee’s employment. Drop-down search
Employment Type Type of employment. Drop-down search
Active Workflow Displays the existing workflow ID. If there is no existing workflow, select None. Modal search
Enrollment Type Displays the type of enrollment, either Benefit Enrollment (BE), Anytime Enrollment (AE), Open Enrollment (OE), or Life Event (LE). Drop-down search

Note: Note: You can turn column display on or off by selecting the column name from the drop-down menu next to Enrollment Type.

Employee Considerations

For employees displayed in the table:

  • Employees are the same employees listed on the Web Enrollment Configuration form.

  • All eligible employees are listed, including employees without a workflow enrollment record and employees with a workflow enrollment record.

  • All employee statuses are displayed except Terminated.

Security

Security restrictions require enforcement on this form, and Service Providers, Worksite Managers, and Worksite Trusted Advisers need access. Security measures determine the companies that these users can view, as well as other data such as department, location, division, and so forth. It also determines what table data these users can see.

Bulk Launch Life Event

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Bulk-Launch-Life-Event.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Bulk-Launch-Life-Event.htm

Bulk Launch Life Event

Use the Bulk Launch Life Event form to make bulk life event changes for multiple employees and eliminate the administrative burden of launching individual life event workflows.

To perform bulk life event changes:

  1. Navigate to the Bulk Launch Benefit Workflow form.

  2. Pre-filter clients, employment types, and employment statuses, then click Continue. See Pre-Filtering Bulk Launch Benefit Workflowsfor more information.

  3. Select the desired client and employees (they must have an active workflow).

  4. Click the Launch Life Event button. .The Active Workflows(s) Detected form opens.

  5. Click Continue. The Launch Life Event form opens.

  6. Select a life event from the Select Life Event drop-down (all life event codes are available for selection). For example, changing from Part Time to Full Time.

  7. Select a Life Event Date from the calendar picker. For example, the date when employees become Full Time employees.

  8. Select a notification date from the Date Notified calendar picker.

  9. Click Continue to commit changes. The system runs a process that archives existing workflows (if any) and creates life event workflows. The Bulk Workflow Results form opens and displays a success message if the process completed correctly.

  10. Click Download Results to download the report as a CSV file (the report name is Launch Life Event Results). You cannot access the report after viewing it the first time.

========================================================================

Downloading Bulk Workflow Results

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Bulk_Workflow_Results.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Bulk_Workflow_Results.htm

Downloading Bulk Workflow Results

You can download these results as a CSV file by clicking Download Results. The downloaded report name is Launch Benefit Enrollment Results.

The Bulk Workflow Results form provides a way to validate and track bulk changes. You can download these results as a CSV file.

To access this form:

  1. From the Review Workflow Request form, click Continue. The Bulk Workflow Request form opens. The system displays this message:Launch request results are available as a CSV download. Download Results option will not be available after leaving this screen.

Note: Note: Benefit workflow launch results that are successful displays a Status of Success in the results table.

  1. Download the workflow results as a CSV file by clicking Download Results. The downloaded report name is Launch Benefit Enrollment Results.

Changing Benefit Workflows for Multiple Employees

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Changing-Benefit-Workflows-for-Multiple-Employees.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Changing-Benefit-Workflows-for-Multiple-Employees.htm

Changing Benefit Workflows for Multiple Employees

Use the Bulk Launch Benefit Workflow form to launch benefit workflows for multiple employees at a time.

To access this form and change benefit workflows for multiple employees:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From Operations|Action, select Bulk Launch Benefit Workflow. Or, from Work Centers, select Benefit Enrollment Maintenance|Bulk Change Tools|Bulk Launch Benefit Workflow.

  3. Select one or more pre-filter options (optional). You can specify Client(s), Employment Type(s), and Employment Status.

  4. Click Continue to complete pre-filtering. The main Bulk Launch Benefit Workflow form displays.

Note: Note: The form is also available in the Benefit Enrollment Maintenance work center under Bulk Change Tools > Bulk Launch Workflows.

  1. Select one or more employees from the list by clicking the check box next to their names. The employees must be in the same benefit group.

  2. You can narrow the displayed items by filtering or searching for specific items using these column headings:

Column — Description

Client ID — The unique identifier for the client. Click the link to open a multi-select search modal.

Employee ID — The unique identifier for the employee.

Employee Name — The name of the employee.

Hire Date — Date the employee was hired (from - through)

Employer Start Date — The date the employee started with this employer.

Benefit Group — The benefit group the employee is assigned to.

Employment Status — A text string describing an employment status. For example, A for active. This value is defined on the Employee Statuses form.

Employment Type — A text string describing the employment type. For example, FT for full-time. This value is defined on the Employee Types form.

Active Workflow — Displays an existing workflow ID. Search for an ID or select it from the drop-down. If there is no existing workflow, select None.

Enrollment Type — Displays the type of enrollment. Search for an enrollment type or select it from the drop-down. If left blank, it displays all enrollment types. Options are Benefit Enrollment (BE), Anytime Enrollment (AE), Life Event (LE), or OE (Open Enrollment).

  1. Click Launch Benefit Enrollment to open the Active Workflows(s) Detected form.

For life events, click Launch Life Event (see Bulk Launch Life Event

  1. Click Continue. The Review Workflows form opens.

  2. Review the results. You can change Workflow, Effective Date, Start Date, and End Date.

  3. Make changes as needed, then click Continue. The Bulk Workflow Results form displays.

  4. Click Download Results to download the report as a CSV file (see Downloading Bulk Workflow Results

Client Enrollment Status by Workflow

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Client_Enrollment_Status_by_Workflow.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Client_Enrollment_Status_by_Workflow.htm

Client Enrollment Status by Workflow

The Client Enrollment Status by Workflow Report shows how many employees have either completed or not yet begun the enrollment process. (PrismHR Benefits Enrollment only.)

You can run the report for a specific client or workflow as needed. You can also limit the report to enrollments with a specific starting, ending, or effective date.

If the start, end, or effective dates are missing from a record, one or more of the employees included either has an enrollment assigned that is missing this information or does not have an enrollment assigned. You must fix this by either creating a custom enrollment for employees who do not have an enrollment assigned or by updating their existing enrollments with the correct dates. You can run the Enrollment Status by Workflow/Client report to identify the employees who require this update.

To run the Client Enrollment Status by Workflow report:

  1. Click Reports.

  2. From Benefits|Report, select Client Enrollment Status by Workflow. The Client Enrollment Status by Workflow form opens.

Use this option To define this
Starting Date The start date for enrollments to include in the report.
Ending Date The ending date for enrollments to include in the report.
Effective Date The effective date for enrollments to include in the report.
Client ID The client to include in the report. Required.
Workflow Type The workflow type to include in the report. Required. - Enrollment: The workflow is for benefits enrollments (BE), open enrollments (OE), or life event enrollments. - Onboarding: For future development. Does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR.
  • Enrollment: The workflow is for benefits enrollments (BE), open enrollments (OE), or life event enrollments.
  • Onboarding: For future development. Does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR. | Workflow ID | The workflow to include in the report. The Version/Revision list populates. | | Version/Revision | The version or revision of the selected workflow to include in the report. If not specified, the report includes all versions and revisions of the workflow. |
  1. Click Run. The report displays information in the following columns.

Column — Description

Workflow — The workflow for the enrollment.

Client — The client for the enrollment.

Start Date — The start date for the enrollment.

End Date — The end date for the enrollment.

Effective Date — The effective date for the enrollment.

Not Started — The number and percentage of the client’s employees who have not started the enrollment process.

In Progress — The number and percentage of the client’s employees whose enrollments are currently in progress.

Completed — The number and percentage of the client’s employees whose enrollments are completed.

Total Employees — Total number of the employees whose enrollments are not started, in progress, or completed.

Client Enrollment Summary

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Client_Enrollment_Summary.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Client_Enrollment_Summary.htm

Client Enrollment Summary

The Client Enrollment Summary Report displays a summary of all enrollments for clients using Benefits Enrollment. It displays the percentage of employees who have not started, have enrollments in progress, or have completed their enrollments.

To run the Client Enrollment Summary report:

  1. Click Reports.

  2. From Operations|Report, select Client Enrollment Summary Report. The Client Enrollment Summary Report form opens. The report displays information in the following columns.

Column — Description

Client ID — The client’s ID number. If needed, you can view more detail by clicking the link to run the Client Enrollment Status by Workflow Report.

Client Name — The client’s name.

Enrollment Type — The enrollment type (New Hires, Open Enrollment, or both).

Not Started (%) — The percentage of employees whose enrollments are not yet started.

In Progress(%) — The percentage of employees whose enrollments are in progress.

Completed (%) — The percentage of employees whose enrollments are completed.

Assigning Workflows at the Client Level

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Client_Workflow_Grid.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Client_Workflow_Grid.htm

Assigning Workflows at the Client Level

Use the Client Workflow Grid form to assign, at the client level, the workflows to use during enrollments.

You create enrollment workflows in the Workflow Library. You can also assign workflows at the system level. Settings you define at the client level override system-level settings.

The Min # of Overlap days designates the limited amount of time an employee has to complete the open enrollment workflow when the workflow start date plus the Min # of Overlap days extends beyond the open enrollment window. This option is used only in an enrollment workflow overlap scenario.

  • If the start date is the date the first workflow is submitted, 4/25/19, the system checks the Min # of Overlap days on the grid and adds that number of days to the Start Date Window. It then calculates a date of 4/27/19. The system then checks the End Date Window on the grid.

  • If the calculated date is greater than the end date on the grid, the system uses the calculated date.

  • If the end date is in the past, 4/23/19, the system uses the calculated date of 4/27/19 for the workflow end date.

  • If the dates are equal, 4/27/19, the system uses the calculated date of 4/27/19 for the workflow end date.

  • If the end date is in the future, 5/3/19, the workflow end date is 5/3/19.

  • If the Min # of Overlap days is left blank, the system uses the current logic of same length as window dates; the length between start date window and end date window.

  • Use the Filter Records panel to filter system-level workflows by entering the OE Start Date and OE End Date, and then clicking Search.

  • Use First, Prev, Next, and Last to navigate through the workflow history.

  • View the total number of records, which displays in the lower right-hand corner.

  • Use the Filter Records panel to filter system-level workflows by entering the OE Start Date and/or OE End Date, then clicking Search.

  • Use First, Prev, Next, and Last to navigate through the workflow history in the records table.

  • View the total number of records, which displays in the lower right-hand corner.

Creating a Workflow Assignment

To create a new workflow assignment to use during enrollments:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Client Workflow Grid. The Client Workflow Grid form opens.

  3. Click Add Row. The Add a New Record panel opens.

Use this option To define this
Client ID The Client ID. Required. You can hover over a Client ID to view the corresponding Client Name.
Effective Date The date that the benefits that are offered in the workflow become effective for an employee. For example if the enrollment elections start coverage on 06/01/2018,enter that date. Required.
BE Workflow The BE Workflow to use for benefit enrollments. The list displays all active workflows in the Workflow Library.
BE Open Window Length The number of days from the Effective Date during which an employee can enroll in benefits.
Min # of Overlap days The Min # of Overlap days designates the limited amount of time an employee has to complete the open enrollment workflow when the workflow start date plus the Min # of Overlap days extends beyond the open enrollment window. This option is used only in an enrollment workflow overlap scenario.
AE Workflow The Anytime Enrollment workflow.
OE Workflow The OE Workflow to use for open enrollments. The list displays all active workflows in the Workflow Library.
OE Start Date and OE End Date The start and end dates to use for open enrollments. The OE Start Date is the first day that employees can review and make elections during the Open Enrollment period. The OE End Date is the last day that the employees can submit elections. The OE Start Date and OE End Date must fall before the workflow Effective Date.
WF End Date The last day that benefits are active and cover the employee. If the Effective Date for the benefits is 06/01/2018, the WF End Date is 05/31/2019, the End Effective Date. This option is required and cannot be earlier than the OE End Date.
Enable Notifications The Enable Notifications option to use for the record. You must set up notifications to enable notifications on workflows. - All OFF: Disable notifications for all enrollment types - BE Only: Turn on notifications only for benefit enrollments - OE Only: Turn on notifications only for open enrollments - AE Only: Turn on notifications only for anytime enrollments - All ON: Enable notifications for all enrollment types
  • All OFF: Disable notifications for all enrollment types
  • BE Only: Turn on notifications only for benefit enrollments
  • OE Only: Turn on notifications only for open enrollments
  • AE Only: Turn on notifications only for anytime enrollments
  • All ON: Enable notifications for all enrollment types

  1. Click Save. The record displays in the records table.

  2. If needed, assign additional workflows.

  3. Click Save.

Copying a Workflow Assignment

You use an existing workflow assignment as the basis of a new one by copying it and assigning a new effective date. For example, you can copy the information from a previous year to use for the current year.

To copy a workflow assignment:

  1. In the System Workflow grid, click Copy New. The Copy an Existing Record panel displays.

  2. Enter the Client ID for the workflow to copy.

  3. Enter the Effective Date for the record to copy.

  4. Enter the New Effective Date for the record to create.

  5. Click Save. The record maintains the settings from the original record and displays the new effective date in the Records panel.

Configuring Web Enrollment

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Configuring_Web_Enrollment.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Configuring_Web_Enrollment.htm

Configuring Web Enrollment

Use the following settings in the Group Benefit Plans Web Enrollment Parameters panel when setting up enrollments.

Some medical and dental benefit plans enable or require employees to enter information about their primary care provider (PCP) during enrollment.

  • To offer the benefit plan in open enrollments, select Include in Open Enrollments. If you do not select this option, the benefit plan is not included.

  • To give employees the option to enter PCP information, select Collect PCP Information. When you select this option, PCP Required becomes active.

  • To require employees to enter PCP information, select PCP Required.

You must also specify a URL where employees can look up their PCP in PCP Lists in the Details tab in the Benefits Enrollment Plan Details form.

Creating Benefits Enrollment Statements

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Create-Ben-Enroll-Statements.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Create-Ben-Enroll-Statements.htm

Creating Benefits Enrollment Statements

Benefits Enrollment Statements contain employee election data required to complete enrollment with an insurance carrier. To generate and download Benefits Enrollment Statements, use the Create Enrollment Statements form and the Download Enrollment Statements form. These statements can also be viewed, downloaded, and printed using the Manage Documents form if the user’s role has been granted access to the Benefits Enrollment Statements document category. Benefits Enrollment Statements are for Service Providers only.

Note: Note: You can only create and download enrollment statements for batches posted on June 13th, 2020, and later.

To add the document category to your user role:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select User Roles. The User Roles form opens.

  3. In Role ID, select your user role.

  4. ClickActions menu [Image: screenshot] and select Document Security. The Document Security form displays.

  5. Click Add. The Document Security Selection form displays.

  6. From the list, select Benefits Enrollment Statements and click Accept. The document category displays on the Document Category Security form.

  7. Click Save.

After adding the document category to your user role, you can create and download employee enrollment statements.

To create enrollment statements:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From Operations|Action, select Create Enrollment Statements. The Create Enrollment Statements form opens.

  3. Complete the following:

Use this option To do this
Start Date Select a start date. Required. Date cannot be in the future.
End Date Select an end date. Required. Date cannot be in the future.
Batch ID Select a posted batch number from the selected date range.

  1. In Batch ID, select the posted batch number to create enrollment statements. If the batch has documents to generate, the Generate button displays.

  2. Click Generate.

  3. Click Status. The panel shows information about the PDFs created and the PDFs that failed to generate. For any PDFs that fail to generate, click Generate to run the process again.

After creating enrollment statements, you can download them with the Download Enrollment Statements form.

To download enrollment statements:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From Operations|Action, select Download Enrollment Statements. The Create Enrollment Statements form opens.

  3. In Select Batch Id, select the posted batch number to download enrollment statements.

  4. Click Get Employees. The list shows employees with generated PDF enrollment statements.

  5. Click Download Statements. The statements download to your machine in a compressed zip file.

Creating a Custom Form

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Create_Custom_Form_BE.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Create_Custom_Form_BE.htm

Creating a Custom Form

You can create a custom form in the Form Library. If you use this option, you are building the form from scratch and must add and configure all of the fields you want to include.

To create a custom form:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Form Library. The Form Library opens.

  3. Click Create New Form. The Create New Custom Form opens.

For this option — Define this information

New Form ID — The ID for the new form. Required.

Form Name — A name for the form. Required. PrismHR recommends using spaces, not hyphens. Hyphenated words do not wrap correctly in Benefits Enrollment.

Description — A description of the form.

Workflow Type — Benefit Enrollment. Required.

Onboarding — For Onboarding only.

  1. Click Continue to proceed to Edit Custom Form.

General Tab

The New Form ID, Version, Revision, Status, Start Date, End Date, Workflow Type, and Offer Type are read-only. The system populates Form Name and Description with the information entered on the previous form, but you can edit it as needed.

Layout Tab

  1. In Fields, select the type of field to include (Text, Inline Image, Checkbox, and so on). Enter the number of fields to include, then click Add New Field. The form displays the new fields.

  2. Expand the field and click Field ID to configure content. The Field Details panel opens. The parameters vary, depending on the field type selected.

  3. For File Hyperlink and Inline Image, click Add File to attach documents, images, or other media to the field.

  4. Add and configure fields as needed until you have built the form.

  • To preview the form before saving, click Preview Form.

  • To return to the Form Library without saving changes, click Back to Forms Library.

  1. Click Save. The Submit Form dialog box prompts you to select an option.

Option — Description

Immediately — The system sets the form status immediately to Active, and sets the current date as the Start Date. Since there can be only one active version of the form, the system changes the status of any prior active version to Inactive and sets the current date as its End Date.

Future Date — You are prompted to enter a Start Date. The system sets the form status to Inactive, changing it to Active on the start date. Since there can be only one active version of the form, the system changes the status of any prior active version to Inactive and sets the start date for the new version as its End Date.

Save as Draft — The form status is set to Draft. The system does not set a Start Date nor does it set version or revision numbers for the form.

Creating a Confirmation Form

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Creating%20Confirmation%20Forms_BE.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Creating Confirmation Forms_BE.htm

Creating a Confirmation Form

The Confirmation form is where the employee submits benefit selections and completes the enrollment process during enrollments. It can include a message, documents, images, and other media.

The Confirmation Form template includes a header and a series of prebuilt fields that you can modify as needed.

The Confirmation form works in conjunction with the Benefit Summary form, the previous form in the workflow. The employee can access the Confirmation form only after clicking Next on the Benefit Summary form. This summarizes the employee’s benefit selections and lets the employee review and make changes to benefit selections.

The Benefit Summary and Confirmation forms are both required for benefits and open enrollments to process correctly.

When you submit a form, the system sets the form’s version and revision numbers.

To create a new Confirmation form:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Form Library. The Form Library opens.

  3. Click the Name on the Confirmation Form template. The Confirmation Form Template opens.

  4. Click the form ID to edit the form. The Edit Custom Form form opens.

General Tab

The Version, Revision, Status, Start Date, End Date, Workflow Type, and Offer Type are read-only.

For this option Define this information
Form ID A unique ID for the new form. Required.
Form Name The editable name of the form. Required.
Description The editable description of the form. Required.

Layout Tab

  1. Click Header and Footer to expand the Header/Footer Detail panel and customize the Field Default Value as needed. The Field ID and Field Type are read-only.

  2. Configure fields as needed until you have built the form. You can click any of the prebuilt paragraph fields to expand the Field Details panel and customize the Field Default as needed. The Field Type and Field ID are read-only.

  3. In Confirmation Verbiage, set up a confirmation statement to display above the e-signature field during enrollments. The Field Default Value displays the default verbiage but you can change it. The Confirmation Verbiage is required and you cannot delete it.

  4. Select one of the following options:

  • To preview the form before saving, click Preview Form.

  • To return to the Form Library without saving changes, click Back to Forms Library.

  1. Click Save. The Submit Form dialog box prompts you to select an option.

Option — Description

Immediately — The system sets the form status immediately to Active, and sets the current date as the Start Date. Since there can be only one active version of the form, the system changes the status of any prior active version to Inactive and sets the current date as its End Date.

Future Date — You are prompted to enter a Start Date. The system sets the form status to Inactive, changing it to Active on the start date. Since there can only be one active version of the form, the system changes the status of any prior active version to Inactive and sets the start date for the new version as its End Date.

Save as Draft — The form status is set to Draft. The system does not set a Start Date nor does it set version or revision numbers for the form.

  1. Click Submit Form.
