Creating a Workflow (Benefits Enrollment)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Creating_a_Workflow_BE.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Creating_a_Workflow_BE.htm

Creating a Workflow (Benefits Enrollment)

To create workflows for enrollments, use the Workflow Library form. You can also use this form to add notifications to send to employees or groups during enrollments. As you create the workflow, you are also building the workflow’s navigation menu structure that the employee views during the enrollment. The forms in the workflow display in the navigation menu in the same order in which you arrange them.

To create a custom workflow for enrollments:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Workflow Library. The Workflow Library opens.

  3. Click Create New Workflow. The Create New Workflow form displays.

For this option — Define this information

ID — A unique workflow ID. Required.

Name — A unique name for the workflow. Required.

Workflow Type — Enrollment. The type of enrollment. The Onboarding workflow type is used only to create workflows in Onboarding

Start Date, End Date — Inactive. You set these dates when you submit the workflow.

  1. Click Save. The Process, Forms, and Notifications tabs become active, and the name of the form changes to Edit Workflow.

To create a custom workflow for enrollments based on an existing form:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Workflow Library. The Workflow Library opens.

  3. Click Duplicate on the form to clone. The Duplicate Form form displays.

For this option Define this information
ID A unique workflow ID. Required.
Name A unique Name for the workflow. Required.
Version Select the version of the form to be duplicated. Required.
  1. Click Duplicate. The Process, Forms, and Notifications tabs become active, and the name of the form changes to Edit Workflow.

Defining the Process

The lists displayed in the Process tab are for the Welcome, Benefits Summary, and Confirmation forms.

You can click

[Image: screenshot]

(Edit) next to the list to edit the form’s display name.

To define the process:

  1. Select the Process tab.

  2. Select the form to use from each list. You can clear the I-9 option if you do not want to include the I-9 forms in the workflow.

  3. Select Approve Every Employee Enrollment if an administrator must approve all enrollments.

You must set up a user as a Benefit Approver (if the user is a worksite manager) or Benefit Manager (if the user is a service provider) for this option to be available. You must also set up an approval policy at the system level.

  1. Select HSA Request to inform employees that they must complete certain forms when selecting a high deductible health plan and provide a link to download the forms. Then, enter a message in the Title field and the Message field. Options are available for both English and Spanish. To add documents for employees to download, click Add to select a document to upload.

  2. Click Save.

Adding Forms

You can add forms to the workflow by dragging them from the Forms Available panel to the Forms Selected panel and organizing them in the appropriate order. Forms in bold are prebuilt forms.

You can organize forms into categories. This is especially helpful for workflows that include many forms. The category displays in the navigation menu, and the included forms for each category display as sub-menu items.

To add forms:

  1. Select the Forms tab.

  2. Click Add Category in the Forms Selected panel. The Category Name dialog box opens.

  3. Enter the Category Name and click Save. The category displays in the Forms Selected panel.

  4. Click and drag forms into the Form Selected panel and arrange in categories in the appropriate order. You can change the name of a category or form by clicking [Image: screenshot] (Edit). To remove a category or form from the Forms Selected panel, click x.

Creating Notifications and Submitting the Workflow

You must create notifications and set up recipients for them to be available to assign to workflows. See Notification Templates (Enrollments) for more information about creating notification templates. See Notification Recipients (Enrollments) for more information assigning notification recipients.

When you submit a workflow, the system sets the workflow’s version and revision numbers.

To create notifications and submit the workflow:

  1. Select the Notifications tab.

  2. Select Enabled for each notification to attach to the workflow.

  3. Click Save. The Submit Workflow dialog box opens.

  4. Select one of the following options:

Option — Description

Immediately — The system sets the workflow status immediately to Active, and sets the current date as the Start Date. Since there can be only one active version of the workflow, the system changes the status of any prior active version to Inactive and sets the current date as its End Date.

Future Date — You are prompted to enter a Start Date. The system sets the workflow status to Inactive, changing it to Active on the start date. Since there can be only one active version of the workflow, the system changes the status of any prior active version to Inactive and sets the start date for the new version as its End Date.

Save as Draft — The workflow status is set to Draft. The system does not set a Start Date nor does it set version or revision numbers for the workflow .

  1. Click Submit Workflow.

Using the Dashboard

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Dashboard_BE.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Dashboard_BE.htm

Using the Dashboard

The PrismHRDashboard provides quick access to company information and relevant features. The information shown in the Chart and List sections of the Perspective panel reflects the client selected.

To view onboarding tasks within Benefits Enrollment, in the Chart section of the Perspective panel, select Benefit Enrollments by Status from the list.

[Image: screenshot]

Displaying Tasks and Their Status

The chart in the Perspective panel displays enrollment tasks and their statuses. You can use the type list to filter the type of enrollment to view in the Dashboard (All Types, Benefit Enrollment Only, Open Enrollment Only, Life Event Only, Anytime Enrollment Only). When you select an enrollment type, the system displays the number of employee enrollments available.

Status Description
In Progress The employee has either not started, or has started but not yet completed the enrollment.
Waiting for Approval The employee has submitted the enrollment, and the enrollment is awaiting approval.
Ready to Review The employee’s enrollment is approved, and in the posting queue. At this point, the enrollment might be included or not yet included in a batch.
Ready to Post The employee’s enrollment is in a batch, has passed review, and is ready to post.
Post/Review Failure The employee’s enrollment is in a batch, but has either not passed review or failed to post.
OE Eligible Employees included in this status are eligible for open enrollment. OE eligibility is determined as follows: - Benefits Enrollment Enabled must be selected in the Global Configuration form. - If the client restricts eligible benefit plans (by Department, Benefit Group, Division, or Location) using the Eligible Benefits Determinant in Client Details, the system checks list of Eligible Benefits at the restriction level to see if the benefit plans are active and the employee is OE eligible. If no list of Eligible Benefits is entered at the restriction level, the system excludes the employee’s benefit group and the employee is not OE eligible. - If the client lists the employee’s status as eligible in the Benefits Enrollment Eligibility Criteria panel in the Client Details form, the employee is OE eligible. If the client has not listed eligible statuses in this panel, the system excludes terminated employees from being OE eligible. Employees who are not terminated are OE eligible. - If the client lists the employee’s type as eligible in the Benefits Enrollment Eligibility Criteria panel in the Client Details form, the employee is OE eligible. If the client has not listed eligible types in this panel, the employee’s type must have Eligible For On-line Benefit Enrollment selected at the system-level in Employee Types for the employee to be OE eligible.
  • Benefits Enrollment Enabled must be selected in the Global Configuration form.
  • If the client restricts eligible benefit plans (by Department, Benefit Group, Division, or Location) using the Eligible Benefits Determinant in Client Details, the system checks list of Eligible Benefits at the restriction level to see if the benefit plans are active and the employee is OE eligible. If no list of Eligible Benefits is entered at the restriction level, the system excludes the employee’s benefit group and the employee is not OE eligible.
  • If the client lists the employee’s status as eligible in the Benefits Enrollment Eligibility Criteria panel in the Client Details form, the employee is OE eligible. If the client has not listed eligible statuses in this panel, the system excludes terminated employees from being OE eligible. Employees who are not terminated are OE eligible.
  • If the client lists the employee’s type as eligible in the Benefits Enrollment Eligibility Criteria panel in the Client Details form, the employee is OE eligible. If the client has not listed eligible types in this panel, the employee’s type must have Eligible For On-line Benefit Enrollment selected at the system-level in Employee Types for the employee to be OE eligible. ## Displaying Benefit Enrollments by Type

The List section displays the individual benefit enrollment records for the type selected. You can further filter benefit enrollment records that display in the list by clicking a status in the pie chart. The list updates, and the colored dot that displays next to each item matches the color in the pie chart section.

The table displays the following columns.

Column Description
Employee Name The employee’s name.
Employee ID The employee’s ID.
Start Date The start date for the enrollment workflow.
End Date The end date for the enrollment workflow.
Effective Date The enrollment effective date.
Workflow The name of the enrollment workflow.
Status The status for the enrollment.
Actions An action to perform for a specific enrollment record. The following options are available: - Archive: Archives the enrollment record. The system displays an informative message and prompts you to confirm Yes or No. If you click Yes, the system archives the record and displays a confirmation message when complete. Click OK to close.Click OK to close. - Extend: Changes the enrollment End Date and extends the enrollment period for the employee. The system displays a dialog box where you can enter the new end date and click Extend to save. - Proxy: Performs a proxy enrollment in the Proxy Login form. - View Web Enrollment: Displays the enrollment record in the Web Enrollment Configuration form.
  • Archive: Archives the enrollment record. The system displays an informative message and prompts you to confirm Yes or No. If you click Yes, the system archives the record and displays a confirmation message when complete. Click OK to close.Click OK to close.
  • Extend: Changes the enrollment End Date and extends the enrollment period for the employee. The system displays a dialog box where you can enter the new end date and click Extend to save.
  • Proxy: Performs a proxy enrollment in the Proxy Login form.

  • View Web Enrollment: Displays the enrollment record in the Web Enrollment Configuration form.

Managing Documents

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Document_Repository.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Document_Repository.htm

Managing Documents

Use the Document Repository to upload and manage documents. You can upload pdf, jpg, jpeg, tif, tiff, png, and gif files. When uploaded, you can use the files to create forms for enrollments in the Form Library. Benefits Enrollment users can attach documents to benefit plan records in the Comparison Fields tab in the Benefits Enrollment Plan Details form.

Editing a Document

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/DR_Editing.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/DR_Editing.htm

Editing a Document

You can modify file settings to maintain and organize your files.

To edit a document:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Document Repository.

  3. Click the Name of the file. The Document Settings dialog box opens.

  4. Modify the File Label, Description, File Classification, File Tags, and File Version Number. The Security Level for the document is read-only and you cannot change it. The File Classification and at least one File Tag are required to save changes.

  5. Select one of the following options:

  • To update the document with a new version, click Upload New Version. The system prompts you to navigate to the location of the file to upload and select the file.

  • To save changes, click Save.

  • To delete the file, click Delete.

  • To view the file, click Preview.

  • To download the file, click Download.

  • To return to the Document Repository without saving changes, click Close.

Searching and Filtering Documents

PrismHR provides various ways to search and filter files in the Document Repository. The total number of documents displays in the lower left-hand corner of the Document Repository.

Search Option Description
Select documents to display Displays a specific set of documents. - Public and Client—The set contains all public and client documents. This is the default. - Employee for Client ID—The set contains all employee documents for the selected client ID. The set does not contain public and client documents.
  • Public and Client—The set contains all public and client documents. This is the default.
  • Employee for Client ID—The set contains all employee documents for the selected client ID. The set does not contain public and client documents. | Search | Searches for files in the Document Repository. Start typing to search, or narrow the search by Name, Description, Tags, and Label. | | Classification | Filters your view using the list. Only documents assigned the selected classification display. | | Tags | Filters your view by selecting one or more file tags. Only the documents assigned to the selected file tags display. | | [Image: screenshot] | Filters your view based on the columns you select or clear. Columns with the [Image: screenshot] icon display in the table. Uploading a Single Document

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/DR_Uploading.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/DR_Uploading.htm

Uploading a Single Document

You can upload a single document to the Document Repository. You can also upload a .ZIP file containing multiple documents.

You must assign uploaded documents unique names within their selected security level. The system displays an error message if you assign a document the same name and security level as an existing document in the Document Repository.

To upload a document:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Upload a Document. The Document Upload dialog box opens.

For this option Define this information
File Label The label to display when you include the document in forms (for example, Blue Cross Blue Shield PPO Benefit Summary). The file label is optional. If you do not enter a file label, the name of the file displays by default (for example, BCBS_PPO.pdf).
Description A description of the document. Required.
File Classification A classification for the form. Required.
File Tags One or more tags to categorize the file (Medical, Dental, Forms, Images, and so on). File tags are shown in the Document Repository and are used to organize files so that users can find them easily. At least one file tag is required.
File Version A version number for the file if you plan to maintain different versions of it. The system does not update file versions. If you upload a later version of the file, you must manually enter the new version number.
Select level for saving file A level for the file to determine who can access the document. - Public - All users can access the document. - Client - The system prompts you to enter the Client ID. All employees who work for the client you specify can access the document. - Employee - The system prompts you to enter both Client and Employee IDs. Only the employees that you specify will be able to access the document.
  • Public - All users can access the document.
  • Client - The system prompts you to enter the Client ID. All employees who work for the client you specify can access the document.
  • Employee - The system prompts you to enter both Client and Employee IDs. Only the employees that you specify will be able to access the document.

  1. Click Choose File, navigate to the location of the file to upload, and then select the file.

  2. Click Upload.

========================================================================

Uploading Multiple Documents

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/DR_Uploading_Multiple_Documents.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/DR_Uploading_Multiple_Documents.htm

Uploading Multiple Documents

Using .ZIP file format, you can upload multiple documents to the Document Repository. Documents can include logos and other images, PDF files, spreadsheets, and other types of files.

Creating the .ZIP File

For bulk uploads, construct .ZIP files exactly as follows. Any variation causes the upload to fail.

  • Use PKZIP or the Compressed (zipped) folder option in Windows Explorer to create a .ZIP file that contains the documents for bulk upload.

  • Do not use any implementation‑specific features or the file does not extract correctly.

  • Encryption, passwords, or other security measures are not supported, and cause the upload to fail.

  • Arrange the folders and files in the .zip file following the directory structure, with three sub-folders (client, employee, public) contained in the root folder.

Files placed in the root directory (that is, outside the sub-folders shown) and files placed in any other sub-folders are not supported and cause the upload to fail. Directory names must be in lowercase.

Sub-folder Description
client Files in this folder are restricted by Client ID after upload in the Document Repository. - The folder name and all sub-folder names must be in lowercase (client). - The client folder must contain one sub-folder per client, with the folder name set to match the Client ID (including any leading zeroes). For example, if the Client ID is 1908, the sub-folder for that client must be named 1908. - The client documents should be placed inside the client sub-folder. No additional sub-folders are permitted.
  • The folder name and all sub-folder names must be in lowercase (client).
  • The client folder must contain one sub-folder per client, with the folder name set to match the Client ID (including any leading zeroes). For example, if the Client ID is 1908, the sub-folder for that client must be named 1908.
  • The client documents should be placed inside the client sub-folder. No additional sub-folders are permitted. | employee | Files in this folder are restricted by Client ID and Employee ID after upload in the Document Repository. - The folder name and all sub-folder names must be in lowercase, for example (employee). - The employee folder must contain one sub-folder per client, with the folder name set to match the Client ID (including any leading zeroes). For example, if the Client ID is 1908, the sub-folder for that client must be named 1908. - Each client sub-folder must contain one sub-folder per Employee ID, with the folder name set to match the Employee ID. For example, if the Employee ID is 22386, the employee sub-folder must be named 22386. - The files should be placed inside the employee sub-folder. No additional sub-folders are permitted. |
  • The folder name and all sub-folder names must be in lowercase, for example (employee).
  • The employee folder must contain one sub-folder per client, with the folder name set to match the Client ID (including any leading zeroes). For example, if the Client ID is 1908, the sub-folder for that client must be named 1908.
  • Each client sub-folder must contain one sub-folder per Employee ID, with the folder name set to match the Employee ID. For example, if the Employee ID is 22386, the employee sub-folder must be named 22386.
  • The files should be placed inside the employee sub-folder. No additional sub-folders are permitted. | public | Files in this folder are imported with no security restrictions, and are viewable by everyone after upload in the Document Repository. - The folder name must be in lowercase (public). - All files must be placed within the public sub-folder. No additional sub-folders are permitted. |
  • The folder name must be in lowercase (public).
  • All files must be placed within the public sub-folder. No additional sub-folders are permitted. ## Uploading the .ZIP File

To upload the .ZIP file:

  1. Click [Image: screenshot] (Back Office).

  2. From System|Change, select Document Repository.

  3. Click Bulk Upload (Zip). The Upload window opens.

  4. Click Choose File and select the .ZIP file to upload.

  5. Click Submit. After the upload completes, the system automatically extracts the documents, stores them in the Document Repository, and generates a metadata.csv file. You can review for errors and edit as needed, with the following restrictions:

  • Do not change the value in the first column (uuid).. The system uses this value to locate the files in the document repository. Do not remove the first row containing the column headings.

  • The columns userClassification, label, description, and version are strings and are always treated as a single value.

  • Classification labels must match the classifications in the Document Repository.

  • To avoid processing errors, do not use quotation marks inside any of the column values.

  • In the userTags column, specify multiple tags by separating them with a comma.

  1. Import the metadata.csv file back into PrismHR using the Metadata Import Entry Form to complete the upload process.

Importing the metadata.csv File

To import the metadata.csv file into PrismHR, use the Metadata Import Entry form, located in the Back office menu under System > Action. The metadata.csv file is generated when you upload multiple files to the Document Repository using the bulk upload.

This is the final step in uploading multiple files to the Document Repository. You must complete it for the files the files to display correctly.

To import the metadata.csv file into PrismHR:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Action, select Metadata Import Entry.

  3. Click Start Import. The Upload window opens.

  4. Click Choose File, and select the file to upload.

  5. Click Submit. The imported data displays. If the file contains invalid information, the system displays an error.

  • To view the data, click Done with Errors

  • To scroll through the report, click Next Page and Previous Page.

  • To discard the data, click Cancel.

  1. Click Post Data to complete the import.

EDI History Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/EDI%20History%20Report.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/EDI History Report.htm

EDI History Report

more of an audit report to compare the employee bill rate to the actual premium rate being paid to the carrier and see the variance if any. This is not a billing reconciliation report based on actual payroll data and generates the billed vs. premium amounts from the Group Benefit Plan Billing and Premium rates.

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

To access the form, click Reports. Under Operations|Report, click EDI History Report.

To generate the EDI History Report:

  1. Complete the following:

Field Name — Description

EDI File ID — This field is optional. Leave it blank for the report to run for all EDI files. If you want to run it for a particular EDI file, enter it here.

Original Client ID — This field is optional. This is the original billed Client ID. This would normally be blank for a test download. Leave this field blank for this report to run for all clients. If you want to restrict this report to run for a selected client, enter the Client ID here or click the Original Client ID link and select it from the Select Client dialog box.

Process Date On or After — This is the date the invoice was processed. This field is optional. Leave this field blank for the report to run for all EDI process dates. If you want to restrict this report to run from a particular EDI process date, enter it here.

Process Date On or Before — This is the date the invoice was processed. This field is optional. Leave this field blank for the report to run for all EDI process dates. If you want to restrict this report to run up to a particular EDI process date, enter it here.

Invoice Date On or After — This is the invoice date for the item being submitted through EDI. This field is optional. Leave this field blank for the report to run for all invoice dates. If you want to restrict this report to run from a particular invoice date, enter it here.

Invoice Date On or Before — This is the invoice date for the item being submitted through EDI. This field is optional. Leave this field blank for the report to run for all invoice dates. If you want to restrict this report to run up to a particular invoice date, enter it here.

EDI Agent ID — Enter the EDI Agent ID here.

  1. Click Run to generate the report. The report shows up on screen.

  2. Click Clear to clear the form at any time.

  3. Click Close to close this form.

This Detail report shows the following columns:

  • EDI File ID

  • Invoice Date

  • Original Client Name

  • Cill to Client ID

  • EDI Amount

  • Invoice Number

  • Location Code

  • Agent Type

  • Agent ID

Employee Enrollment Progress

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Employee_Enrollment_Progress_Report.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Employee_Enrollment_Progress_Report.htm

Employee Enrollment Progress

The Employee Enrollment Progress Report lists the enrollment progress for employees participating in an enrollment. This enables you to review which workflow steps in an employee’s enrollment are complete, and which are still pending. (PrismHR Benefits Enrollment only.)

You can run this report for a specific client, employee, or workflow as needed. You can also limit the report to enrollments with a specific starting, ending, or effective date.

If the start, end, or effective dates are missing from a record, one or more of the employees included either has an enrollment assigned that is missing this information or does not have an enrollment assigned. You must fix this by either creating a custom enrollment for employees who do not have an enrollment assigned or by updating their existing enrollments with the correct dates. You can run the Enrollment Status by Workflow/Client report to identify the employees who require this update.

To run the Employee Enrollment Progress report:

  1. Click Reports.

  2. From Benefits|Report, select Employee Enrollment Progress Report. The Employee Enrollment Progress Report form opens.

Use this option To define this
Starting Date The start date for enrollments to include in the report.
Ending Date The ending date for enrollments to include in the report.
Effective Date The effective date for enrollments to include in the report.
Client ID The client to include in the report. Required.
Workflow Type The workflow type to include in the report. Required. - Enrollment: Tthe workflow is for benefits enrollments (BE), open enrollments (OE), and life event enrollments. - Onboarding: For future development. Does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR.
  • Enrollment: Tthe workflow is for benefits enrollments (BE), open enrollments (OE), and life event enrollments.
  • Onboarding: For future development. Does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR. | Workflow ID | The workflow to include in the report. The Version/Revision list populates. | | Version/Revision | The version or revision of the selected workflow to include in the report. If not specified, the report includes all versions and revisions of the workflow. | | Employee ID | The employee to include in the report. If blank, the report includes all employees. |
  1. Click Run. The report displays information in the following columns.

Column — Description

Workflow — The workflow ID.

Client — The client’s name and ID.

Employee ID — The employee’s name and ID.

Start Date — The start date for the enrollment.

End Date — The end date for the enrollment.

Effective Date — The effective date for the enrollment.

Progress Step — The step number and name in the workflow.

Status — The employee’s enrollment status (Eligible, Not Eligible, Pending, Waived, Completed) for the specified step.

Adding Field Options

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Enrollment_Form_Library_Fields.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Enrollment_Form_Library_Fields.htm

Adding Field Options

The Form Library is where you create and manage forms by adding field options to them. You use forms to create workflows in the Workflow Library. Some options use the images or documents uploaded in the Documents Repository form.

To add fields to a custom form:

  1. Click [Image: screenshot] (Back Office).

  2. From System|Change, select Form Library. The Form Library opens.

  3. Using the Layout tab on the Edit Custom form, drag the preferred Field Types from the Field Type list to the designated section on the right. Users of the custom form can change Field Types noted as editable. For example, they can select or clear a Check box.

  4. Select a field type and click Configure. The Field Details panel opens. Field parameters vary, depending on the type of field selected, and they are required.

  5. Click Save.

All fields require the following parameters in the Field Details panel.

Parameter — Description

Field ID — Enter a new Field ID. It must begin with an alphabetic character, contain at least two characters, and have no spaces.

Field Type — Select the field type from the list.

Field Label — Enter the field label to display in the form to indicate what the user should enter in the field (for example, Last Name for a Text field).

Field Group — For future development. Does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR.

Configuring Client Settings

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Front_Matter/BE_UG_Chapter_Config_Client_Settings.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Front_Matter/BE_UG_Chapter_Config_Client_Settings.htm

Configuring Client Settings

The following client-level settings are specific to Benefits Enrollment. You must define them for the benefit plan information to display correctly during enrollments. Client-level settings override settings established at the system level.

The client-level settings for Benefits Enrollment are in the Benefits and Control tabs in Client Details form. Refer to the Benefits Administration User Guide for more information.

To set up group benefits, workers’ compensation, and retirement plan information:

  1. Select the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change select Client Details. The Client Details form opens.

  3. On the Benefits tab, in the Group Benefits panel, select a Benefit Plan ID.

Use this option — To define this

Section 125 Plan — The Section 125 Plan in which the client participates.

HSA Plan — The HSA Plan in which the client participates.

Maintains Own HDHP — Whether the client maintains a high deductible health plan. This suppresses the system’s validation when setting up an employee HSA.

Eligible Benefits Determinant — Whether to make benefits available to an employee if the client makes different sets of benefits available to their employees based on department, division, location, or benefit group. The system determines the benefits employees are eligible for based on the determinant.

Suppress Automatic Benefit Adjustment — Whether the client wants to suppress automatic benefit adjustment calculations.

Control Group Client — The control group client that acts as a master company for the control group, if any. Use control groups to aggregate data when multiple clients are treated as related entities for Affordable Care Act (ACA) or retirement plan reporting purposes. For example, if an employee works 20 hours at company A and 20 hours at company B, the system treats that employee as having worked 40 hours for the purposes of the ACA, and reports that for both companies A and B.

Auto Create Open Enrollments — Whether to create employee web enrollment forms automatically.

Auto Post Open Enrollments — Whether to post employee web enrollment forms automatically.

Include Employer Cost in Employee Self-Service — Whether to include employer cost in employee self-service. (PrismHR Benefits Enrollment only.)

Employee Calculation Based On — The method (Pay Period, Per Month, or System Default) for calculating employee costs during enrollments. (PrismHR Benefits Enrollment only.)

  1. Click Save.

To set up new hires and employees:

  1. Select Client in the menu bar.

  2. From Client|Change select Client Details. The Client Details form opens.

  3. Click the Control tab.

  4. Access the New Hire/Employee Detail Controls and complete these fields as required:

Use this option To define this
Employee Numbers Used A unique number that identifies the employee.
Auto Employee Number Whether to automatically assign an employee number to new hires.
Next Employee Number The next employee number to use.
Allow First Period Hours Whether users of the New Hire form can enter prorated hours for the new employee for the client if their start date is in the middle of a pay period. This is typically used for salaried employees who have auto time sheet hours. This list displays if your organization selected Enable New Hire First Period Hours in System Parameters. - System Default: The New Hire form displays the First Pay Period Hours only if Enable New Hire First Period Hours is selected in System Parameters. - Enabled: The First Pay Period Hours is available in the New Hire form, regardless of the setting in System Parameters. - Disabled: The New Hire form does not display the First Pay Period Hours, regardless of the setting in System Parameters.
  • System Default: The New Hire form displays the First Pay Period Hours only if Enable New Hire First Period Hours is selected in System Parameters.
  • Enabled: The First Pay Period Hours is available in the New Hire form, regardless of the setting in System Parameters.
  • Disabled: The New Hire form does not display the First Pay Period Hours, regardless of the setting in System Parameters. | Department Required | The department required for time sheets and labor allocation. | | Shift Required | The shift required for time sheets and labor allocation. | | Work Group Required | The work group required for the employee. | | Division Required | The division required for time sheets and labor allocation. |
  1. Click Save.

  1. Select Client in the menu bar.

  2. From Client|Change select Client Details. The Client Details form opens.

  3. Click the Control tab.

  4. Access the Enrollment/Onboarding Controls and complete these fields as required:

Use this option To define this
Electronic Enrollment Enable this field to complete enrollment in Benefits Enrollment.

Instead, administrators enter employee information in the New Hire form. - Prompt: The New Hire form prompts the user to indicate whether the employee will complete the onboarding process online, or whether an administrator will enter the information in the New Hire form.
  • Always: Employees always do their own onboarding online.

Instead, administrators enter employee information in the New Hire form. - Prompt: The New Hire form prompts the user to indicate whether the employee will complete the onboarding process online, or whether an administrator will enter the information in the New Hire form. |
  • Always: Employees always do their own onboarding online.
  • Never: Employees never do their own onboarding. Instead, administrators enter employee information in the New Hire form.
  • Prompt: The New Hire form prompts the user to indicate whether the employee will complete the onboarding process online, or whether an administrator will enter the information in the New Hire form. | I-9 Business Address | Whether the system should use the Employer address or the Client (DBA) address with the doing-business-as name. This list displays if the If Onboarding Module is set to PrismHR. | | Complete All Forms to Activate Payroll | How employees are added to payroll: - System Default: The system defaults to adding employees to payroll after they submit their Form I‑9 Section 1 (and the manager completes the Form I‑9 Section 2) and Employee Information form - Enabled: Prevents employees from being added to payroll until all of their onboarding forms are complete - Disabled: Does not prevent employees from being added to payroll until all of their onboarding forms are complete |
  • System Default: The system defaults to adding employees to payroll after they submit their Form I‑9 Section 1 (and the manager completes the Form I‑9 Section 2) and Employee Information form
  • Enabled: Prevents employees from being added to payroll until all of their onboarding forms are complete
  • Disabled: Does not prevent employees from being added to payroll until all of their onboarding forms are complete | Enable Benefit Enrollment After Final Submit | How employees access their benefit enrollment workflow: - System Default: Uses the configuration of the Enable Benefit Enrollment After Final Submit option on the Onboarding Options form. For more information, see the System Administration User Guide. - Enabled: Enable employees to access their benefit enrollment workflow through Onboarding after submitting all onboarding related forms. - Disabled: Disable employees from accessing their benefit enrollment workflow through Onboarding after submitting all onboarding related forms |

  • Enabled: Enable employees to access their benefit enrollment workflow through Onboarding after submitting all onboarding related forms.
  • Disabled: Disable employees from accessing their benefit enrollment workflow through Onboarding after submitting all onboarding related forms

Maintaining Web Enrollments

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Front_Matter/BE_UG_Chapter_Maintaining_Web_Enrollments.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Front_Matter/BE_UG_Chapter_Maintaining_Web_Enrollments.htm

Maintaining Web Enrollments

You maintain web enrollments using the following forms:

  • To view an employee’s enrollment activity during enrollments, use the Web Enrollments History form.

  • To extend the end date in an enrollment for an employee who needs more time to enroll, use the Web Enrollment Configuration form.

  • To create a custom enrollment period for an employee, use the Web Enrollment Configuration form.

  • To assign a new workflow to active enrollments, use the Web Enrollments Bulk Update form.

  • To modify the start and end dates on active enrollment workflows, use the Web Enrollments Date Update form.

  • To create enrollment batches and post benefit enrollments, use the Post Benefit Enrollments form.

Generating Reports

Setting Up Benefit Plan Information

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Front_Matter/BE_UG_Chapter_Setting_BP_Information.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Front_Matter/BE_UG_Chapter_Setting_BP_Information.htm

Setting Up Benefit Plan Information

To set up information about available benefit plans, use the Benefits Enrollment Plan Details form. For example, you can set up costs for employee contributions, co-payments, deductibles), attach detailed benefit plan summaries and other supporting documents, and set up benefit plan dependencies.

You can enter benefit plan information fields manually in the Comparison Fields tab in the Benefits Enrollment Plan Details form. You can also use the Plan Offer Type Import form to import multiple benefit plan information fields.

Setting Up Forms and Workflows

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Front_Matter/BE_UG_Chapter_Setting_Forms_Workflows.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Front_Matter/BE_UG_Chapter_Setting_Forms_Workflows.htm

Setting Up Forms and Workflows

A workflow is essentially a wizard—a script that presents employees with a series of forms to guide them through well-defined and highly customizable steps during onboarding and benefit enrollments.

In the Form Library, you can create custom versions of the forms or use the prebuilt versions provided with their default language and settings. Prebuilt forms are shown in bold.

If you plan to include any media items (such as documents or images) in forms or workflows, upload them to the Document Repository before you create the forms in the Form Library.

After you create the forms, you can use them as components to create workflows in the Workflow Library.

[Image: screenshot]

Planning Enrollment Workflow Components

A typical enrollment workflow might begin with an optional Welcome form and then present forms for medical, dental, and vision plans; long-term and short-term disability plans; and so on. You can organize forms into categories for easier navigation.

The final forms in a workflow include the following required forms:

  • A Benefits Summary form that summarizes the employee’s benefit selections and provides the opportunity to make changes.

  • A Confirmation form where the employee submits benefit selections and completes the enrollment process.

A Welcome form is recommended for workflows, but optional. The Benefit Summary and Confirmation forms are required for enrollments to process correctly.

Before you begin:

  • Plan the organization of the information blocks to present during enrollments so that the process flows well and makes sense.

Managing Documents, Forms, and Workflows

Use the following libraries to manage documents, forms, and workflows for enrollments.

Library — Description

Document Repository — Upload and maintain documents to use when creating forms (graphic files for logos, PDF files containing benefit information, and so on).

Form Library (Enrollments) — Build the forms, typically using documents from the Document Repository. PrismHR provides prebuilt forms that you can use as templates to create new forms, as well as templates for the Welcome and Confirmation forms. You can also build your own custom forms.

Workflow Library (Enrollments) — Create workflows using forms from the Forms Library as components.

========================================================================

Setting Up Notifications

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Front_Matter/BE_UG_Chapter_Setting_Up_Notifications.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Front_Matter/BE_UG_Chapter_Setting_Up_Notifications.htm

Setting Up Notifications

To set up notifications to send during enrollments, use the Notification Templates and Notification Recipients forms.

Before you begin:

  • Set up Email Services.

To access the Notification Templates form:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Notification Templates. The Notification Templates form opens.

To access the Notification Recipients form:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Notification Recipients. The Notification Recipients form opens.

To access the Notification Control Panel:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From Operations|Action, select Notification Control Panel. The Notification Control Panel opens.

Setting Up an Enrollment User

You must set up an enrollment user in PrismHR with the User ID called Enrollment. .

The Enrollment user serves as the administrator and sender for all notifications. The email address set up on the user account displays in From for all notifications sent to employees and new hires. If this user account is not set up, the notifications engine does not send notifications.

The User ID must be as follows, spelled correctly, and with no punctuation: Enrollment

To test the Enrollment user:

  1. In the Notification Templates form, set up a test notification template recipient.

  2. In the Notification Recipients form, set up a test recipient.

  3. In the Notification Control Panel, turn on the notification engine using a test client.

  4. Send a test message from the Enrollment user to confirm that it is set up correctly.

If the Enrollment user is set up correctly, the test recipient receives the notification.

Setting Up Templates and Recipients

After you set up the Enrollment user, you can set up content for notifications in the Notification Templates form.

You can designate recipients for notifications in the Notification Recipients form.

The notifications that you create are available in the Workflow Library where you can add them to workflows.

Managing Notifications

To specify how often the system should send the notifications and to start the notifications engine, use the Notification Control Panel.

You can run the Notification Audit Report to review notifications sent to all recipient types (for example, employees, generic recipients, message groups).

Messages are also stored in the Messages center in PrismHR. You can also set up your SMTP server to retain a copy of every email sent.

Handling Undelivered Notifications

There are two possible circumstances that can result in notifications not reaching their recipient.

  • The email address is not entered correctly. For example, it is entered as @​example.com​ instead of jsmith@example.com.​ PrismHR logs this error but it is not accessible to the user. Users should contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team.

  • The email address is entered correctly, but is otherwise not deliverable (for example, the email address is no longer active). In this case, PrismHR simply sends the email. There is no error log, and in most cases there is no way to know that it was not received. Many email servers are configured not to send notifications of undeliverable emails.

Tracking Notifications

To track notifications, set up the Notification Recipients form to send copies of all notifications to an administrator in your organization. This also enables you to forward notifications as needed.

Configuring System Settings

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Front_Matter/BE_UG_Chapter_SystemLevelSetup.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Front_Matter/BE_UG_Chapter_SystemLevelSetup.htm

Configuring System Settings

The following sections describe system-level settings that are specific to setting up benefits and open enrollments. You must define these settings for benefit plan information to display correctly during enrollments.

========================================================================

Setting Up Anytime Enrollment

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Front_Matter/BE_UG_Setting-Up-Anytime-Enrollments.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Front_Matter/BE_UG_Setting-Up-Anytime-Enrollments.htm

Setting Up Anytime Enrollment

Anytime Enrollment enables employees to enroll in Anytime Benefits at any point during a plan year. A date or event does not trigger the Anytime Enrollment process — you can enroll in Anytime Benefits at any time. One example of an Anytime Benefit plan is pet insurance. If you acquire a pet and want to enroll in a pet insurance benefit plan, you can do so at any time outside of annual enrollment restrictions. To set up Anytime Enrollment, you must:

  1. Configure a benefit plan to include in an Anytime Enrollment workflow

  2. Set up a notification template

  3. Set up notification recipients

  4. Create an Anytime Enrollment workflow

  5. Add the workflow to the system or client workflow grids

To begin, you must configure an Anytime Benefit plan to include in an Anytime Enrollment workflow.

Including a Benefit Plan in an Anytime Enrollment Workflow

To include a benefit plan in an Anytime Enrollment workflow, use the Group Benefit Plans form.

To include a benefit plan in an Anytime Enrollment workflow:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Group Benefit Plans. The Group Benefit Plans form opens.

  3. Configure a benefit plan for Anytime Enrollment or use the Benefit Plan ID link to select a plan from the list.

  4. In the Web Enrollment Parameters panel, enable Include in Anytime Enrollment Workflow.

  5. Click Save.

After configuring a benefit plan to include in an Anytime Enrollment workflow, you must set up a notification template.

Setting Up an Anytime Enrollment Notification Template

To set up a notification template for an Anytime Enrollment workflow, use the Notification Templates form.

To set up a notification template for an Anytime Enrollment workflow:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Notification Templates. The Notification Templates form opens.

  3. Configure a notification template for Anytime Enrollment or use the Template Id link to select a template from the list.

  4. In the Workflow Type panel, enable Anytime Enrollment. You can combine with other workflow types except for Onboarding.

  5. Click Save.

After configuring a notification template for an Anytime Enrollment workflow, you must set up notification recipients.

Setting Up Anytime Enrollment Notification Recipients

To set up notification recipients for an Anytime Enrollment workflow, use the Notification Recipients form.

To set up notification recipients for an Anytime Enrollment workflow:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Notification Recipients. The Notification Recipients form opens.

  3. To configure notification recipients for Anytime Enrollment, click Setup a new Notification. Or, to select a recipient group from the list, use the Notification Id link.

  4. In the Notification Template panel, to assign the recipient group to a template configured for Anytime Enrollment, click Template Id. For more information, see Setting Up an Anytime Enrollment Notification Template.

If you select a notification template configured for Anytime Enrollment, the Workflow Type and Message Delivery Method panels are disabled.

  1. Click Save.

After configuring notification recipients for an Anytime Enrollment workflow, you must create the workflow.

Creating an Anytime Enrollment Workflow

To create an Anytime Enrollment workflow, use the Workflow Library form.

To create an Anytime Enrollment workflow:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Workflow Library. The Workflow Library form opens.

  3. In the Type list, select Enrollment.

  4. To edit an existing workflow for Anytime Enrollment, select a workflow from the list. To create a new workflow, click Create New Workflow.

  5. On the Process tab, enable Anytime Enrollment.

  6. On the Forms tab, select forms to include in the workflow.

  7. On the Notifications tab, enable the notification template configured on the Notification Template form. For more information, see Setting Up an Anytime Enrollment Notification Template.

  8. Click Save.

After creating the workflow, you must add it to the system or client workflow grids.

Adding an Anytime Enrollment Workflow to the System Workflow Grid

To add an Anytime Enrollment workflow to the system workflow grid, use the System Workflow Grid form.

To add an Anytime Enrollment workflow to the system workflow grid:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select System Workflow Grid. The System Workflow Grid form opens.

  3. In the Anytime Enrollment column, select a workflow from the list to add it to the workflow grid.

You can also click Add Row to add another row to the workflow grid. In the Add a New Record section, you can select an Anytime Enrollment workflow from the AE Workflow list.

  1. Click Submit Changes.

After submitting your changes, the workflow is now available at the system level. You can also assign the workflow at the client level using the Client Workflow Grid form. Client‑level settings override system‑level settings.

Adding an Anytime Enrollment Workflow to the Client Workflow Grid

To add an Anytime Enrollment workflow to the client workflow grid, use the Client Workflow Grid form.

To add an Anytime Enrollment workflow to the client workflow grid:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Client Workflow Grid. The Client Workflow Grid form opens.

  3. In the Anytime Enrollment column, select a workflow from the list to add it to the workflow grid.

You can also click Add Row to add another row to the workflow grid. In the Add a New Record section, you can select an Anytime Enrollment workflow from the AE Workflow list.

  1. In Enable Notifications, you can select the following specific to Anytime Enrollment workflows:

  • All OFF: Disable notifications for all enrollment types. Applies only if you combine multiple workflow types on the notification template.

  • AE Only: Enable notifications only for Anytime Enrollment

  • All ON: Enable notifications for all enrollment types. Applies only if you combine multiple workflow types on the notification template.

  1. Click Save.

After submitting your changes, the workflow is now available at the client level.

Adding Anytime Benefits to Employee Portal

In Employee Portal (EP), you can add a widget displaying available Anytime Benefits enabling employees to enroll directly from EP. A link from EP opens directly to an Anytime Enrollment workflow (through SSO) where they can enroll in any of the available benefit plans. For more information on creating the widget, see Adding Anytime Benefits to Employee Portal (EP) on the Customer Resource Center.

Global Dependent Information Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Global%20Dependent%20Information%20Report.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Global Dependent Information Report.htm

Global Dependent Information Report

Use the Global Dependent Information Report form to generate a report for one or more clients to produce a detailed output of all employees who have a listed dependent.

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

To access this form, clickReports menu. Under Benefits|Report, click Global Dependent Information Report.

To generate the Global Dependent Information Report:

  1. Complete the following field:

Field Name — Description

Client ID — This field is not mandatory. Leave this field blank to select allclients. If you want to run this report for specific clients, enter the Client IDs here or click the Client ID link and select them from the Select Clients list. The corresponding Client Name displays.

  1. Click Run to generate the report on-screen.

  2. Click Clear to clear this form at any time.

  3. Click Close to close this form at any time.

Report Output

The Global Dependent Information Report is sorted by Client ID and displays the following columns:

  • Client ID

  • Client Name

  • Employee Last Name

  • Employee First Name

  • Dependent Last Name

  • Dependent First Name

  • Relation

  • Soc-Sec-Num

Global Employee Deduction Setup Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Global%20Employee%20Deduction%20Setup%20Report.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Global Employee Deduction Setup Report.htm

Global Employee Deduction Setup Report

Use the Global Employee Deduction Setup Report form to generate a global report on employee deductions set up for all employees. This report provides a global list of deductions set up by employee. The report can be customized by Client, Deduction Code, and employee Status Class.

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

To access this form, clickReports menu. Under Operations|Report, click Global Employee Deduction Setup Report.

To generate the Global Employee Deduction Setup Report:

  1. Complete the following fields:
Field Name Description
Client ID This field is optional. Leave this field blank to include all clients for your report. If you want to run this report for a specific clients, enter the Client IDs here or click the Client ID link and select them from the Select Clients dialog box.
Deduction Code This field is optional. Leave this field blank to include all deduction codes in PrismHR for your report. If you want to run this report for a specific deduction codes, enter them here or click the Deduction Code link and select them from the Select Deduction Codes dialog box.
Status Class This field is optional. Select from the following options: - All - This is the default form option. Select this option to include all employee statuses in PrismHR in your report. - Active - Select this option to include only active employees in PrismHR in your report. - Terminated - Select this option to include only terminated employees in PrismHR in your report. - Leave of Absence - Select this option to include only employees who are leave of absence in PrismHR in your report.
  • All - This is the default form option. Select this option to include all employee statuses in PrismHR in your report.
  • Active - Select this option to include only active employees in PrismHR in your report.
  • Terminated - Select this option to include only terminated employees in PrismHR in your report.
  • Leave of Absence - Select this option to include only employees who are leave of absence in PrismHR in your report.

  1. Click Run to generate the report on screen.

  2. Click Clear to clear this form at any time.

  3. Click Close to close this form at any time.

Report Output

The Global Employee Deduction Setup Report displays the following columns:

Columns
- Employee ID - Employee Name - Deduction Code - Type - Method - Description - Amount - Periods
  • Employee ID
  • Employee Name
  • Deduction Code
  • Type
  • Method
  • Description
  • Amount

  • Periods

Global Employee Garnishment by Date Report

Use the Global Employee Garnishment by Date Report form to generate a report for one or more employers and a specified pay date range of all garnishments during the period including the pay date, garnishment type, docket information, and amount.

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

To access this form, clickReports menu. Under Operations|Report, click Global Employee Garnishment by Date Report.

To generate the Global Employee Garnishment by Date Report:

  1. Complete the following fields:

Field Name — Description

Starting Pay Date — Enter the starting pay date of the reporting period.

Ending Pay Date — Enter the ending pay date of the reporting period.

Employer ID — This field is not mandatory. Leave this field blank to select all employers. If you want to run this report for specific employers, enter the Employer IDs here or click the Employer ID link and select them from the Select Employer list. The corresponding Employer Name displays.

  1. Click Run to generate the report on-screen.

  2. Click Clear to clear this form at any time.

  3. Click Close to close this form at any time.

Report Output

The Global Employee Garnishment by Date Report is sorted by Employer ID and displays the following columns:

  • Employer ID

  • Employee Name

  • Soc-Sec-Num

  • Pay Date

  • Voucher

  • Check No.

  • Type

  • Issue Authority

  • Docket ID

  • Amount Garnished

Include Inactive Garnishments — This field is optional. By default this box is unchecked, and will report garnishments that are active in PrismHR on the day this report is run. Check this box if you also want to include inactive garnishments in your report.

Global Payroll Deduction by Date Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Global%20Payroll%20Deduction%20by%20Date%20Report.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Global Payroll Deduction by Date Report.htm

Global Payroll Deduction by Date Report

Use the Global Payroll Deduction by Date Report form to generate a report on employee deductions for all or selected clients by a pay date range.

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

To access this form, clickReports menu. Under Operations|Report, click Global Payroll Deduction by Date Report.

To generate the Global Payroll Deduction by Date Report:

  1. Complete the following fields:
Field Name Description
Pay Date From Enter the first date of the pay date range for your report.
Pay Date To Enter the first date of the pay date range for your report.
Include Cost Center This field is optional. By default this field is unchecked. Check this field is you want to your report to show cost centers.
Cost Center This field is optional. Leave this field blank to include all cost centers for your report. If you want to run this report for a specific cost center, enter it here or click the Cost Center link and select it from the Select Cost Center dialog box.
Employer ID This field is optional. Leave this field blank to include all employers for your report. If you want to run this report for a specific employer, enter the Employer ID here or click the Employer ID link and select it from the Select Employer dialog box.
Client ID This field is optional. Leave this field blank to include all clients for your report. If you want to run this report for a specific client, enter the Client ID here or click the Client ID link and select it from the Select Client dialog box.
Deduction Code This field is optional. Leave this field blank to include all deduction codes for your report. If you want to run this report for a specific deduction codes, enter them here or click the Deduction Code link and select them from the Select Deduction Codes dialog box.
Report Type Select the report type that you want to generate from the following options: - Summary - This is the default option. This report provides an employee wise total amount for all deduction codes for an employee. - Detail - This report provides a further breakdown of the deduction amount of each employee by individual deduction codes.
  • Summary - This is the default option. This report provides an employee wise total amount for all deduction codes for an employee.
  • Detail - This report provides a further breakdown of the deduction amount of each employee by individual deduction codes.

  1. Click Run to generate the report on screen.

Global FSA/HSA Deduction Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Global_FSA_HSA_Deduction_Report.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Global_FSA_HSA_Deduction_Report.htm

Global FSA/HSA Deduction Report

Use this form to generate a report that displays the liability for FSA and HSA deductions and contributions.

Note: Note:

  • Only service providers can access the form.

  • Uses Client Access Group security.

To access the form, click Reports. Under Benefits|Report, click Global FSA/HSA Deduction Report.

To generate the Global FSA/HSA Deduction Report:

  1. Complete the following:

Field Name — Description

Starting Pay Date — Enter or select the Starting Pay Date (required) for the pay date range for which you want to run this report.

Ending Pay Date — Enter or select the Ending Pay Date (required) for the pay date range for which you want to run this report.

Employer ID — This field is optional. Leave this field blank for this report to run for all employers. If you want to restrict this report to run for selected employers, enter the employer IDs here or click the Employer ID link and select them from the Select Employer list.

Client ID — This field is optional. Leave this field blank for this report to run for all clients. If you want to restrict this report to run for selected clients, enter the client IDs here or click the Client ID link and select them from the Select Clients list.

Section 125 Plan ID — This field is optional. Enter or select the Section 125 Plan ID for the benefit plan that falls under Section 125.

HSA Plan ID — This field is optional. Enter or select the HSA Plan ID for the health savings account (HSA).

  1. Click Run to generate the report. The report displays on-screen.

  2. Click Clear to clear the form at any time.

  3. Click Close to close the form at any time.

This report displays the following columns:

  • Employer ID

  • Client ID

  • Client Name

  • Employee ID

  • Employee SSN

  • Employee Name

  • Plan Type

  • Deduction

  • Match

  • PEO Match

Creating a New Life Event

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/LEM_Creating_a_New_Life_Event.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/LEM_Creating_a_New_Life_Event.htm

Creating a New Life Event

You can create a new life event workflow code, modify a predefined code, or modify a code that you created.

After you save the life event workflow code, the Code is read-only and you cannot modify it. If you are editing a predefined life event code, you cannot change the value in the Code field.

To create a life event workflow code:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Life Event Maintenance. The Life Event Maintenance form opens.

  3. Select the Codes tab and click Add Life Event. The Add Life Event form opens.

For this option Define this information
Life Event Name The name of the life event workflow code. Required
Code The code for the life event workflow. Required

To modify a life event workflow code:

  • Select the life event and click Edit in the Action column.

To remove a life event workflow code:

  • Select the life event and click Remove in the Action column.

Specifying General Settings

To specify general settings for the workflow, use the General tab in the Add Life Event form. For example, you can specify the required documents, effective date, and the window length for the enrollment.

  • In the Add Life Event form, select the General tab.
For this option Define this information
Doc Required Whether supporting documents are required for the life event. For example, if the life event code is for the birth or adoption of a child, the employee might need to upload a birth certificate or adoption papers during the life event enrollment. If you select Yes, the Specify Document(s) list displays. Document options that display in the list are set up in the Enrollment Options form. Select the document to use and click [Image: screenshot] . The document name displays below the list. You can specify as many documents as necessary. You can click [Image: screenshot] to delete a selection.
Window Length The number of days from the Effective Date during which an employee can complete the life event enrollment.
Days to Notify The number of days from the date of the life event itself that the employee must notify the employer of the life event to be eligible for the life event enrollment.
Initiated By Who can initiate or assign the life event (Sponsor/Client, Employee, Either). If you select Sponsor/Client, an administrator assigns the life event in the Life Event form only. If you select Employee or Either, you must also set up the components the employee can access in EP in the Employee Portal Components tab. - If you select Employee, the employee assigns the life event in EP only. - If you select Either, an administrator can assign the life event in the Life Event form or the employee can assign it in EP.
  • If you select Employee, the employee assigns the life event in EP only.
  • If you select Either, an administrator can assign the life event in the Life Event form or the employee can assign it in EP. | Source of Effective Date | How the system determines the effective date for the life event when coverage changes (Date Notified, Life Event Date, Use Benefit Rule). - Coverage for the employee might be affected by the rules in the benefit plan itself. For example, if a selected benefit plan does not allow mid-month enrollment, coverage might be delayed, even if you specify Life Event Date. - If a benefit rule should determine the effective date for the life event, you must set up the benefit rule for the clients. When Use Benefit Rule is selected in this field, the Eligibility Date Used From Override field displays. This field can be used a selected date in place of the benefit rule’s eligibility date. |
  • Coverage for the employee might be affected by the rules in the benefit plan itself. For example, if a selected benefit plan does not allow mid-month enrollment, coverage might be delayed, even if you specify Life Event Date.
  • If a benefit rule should determine the effective date for the life event, you must set up the benefit rule for the clients. | ACA Workflow | Optional. Select this option to indicate that this life event is used for an ACA workflow. | | Calculate End Date from Life Event | Optional. Select this option to calculate the end date for coverage from the date of the life event. | | Override Offer Type Rules by Payee ID | Optional. This option only displays if Use Benefit Rule is selected in the Source of Effective Date field. When the option is selected, eligibility is overridden from benefit rules or group benefit plans at the Offer Type level for selected Payees. Select which date is used in the Eligibility Date Used From Override field. | ## Selecting Offer Type Rules

To select and view the offer types included in the life events workflow, use the OFFER TYPE RULES tab. You can also view what the employee can do during the enrollment in the Actions Allowed panel. The actions vary depending on the offer type.

To view offer rules:

  1. In the Add Life Event form, select the Offer Rules tab.

  2. Under Codes Assigned, select an assigned code.

  3. Select the OFFER TYPE RULES tab.

  4. Select an Offer Type. The offer type displays below the list, and the offer type’s settings display in the Actions Allowed panel. These settings show the enrollment options that are allowed for the employee for the offer type in the life event enrollment.

Selecting Forms

To select the Welcome, Summary, and Confirmation forms to use in the life event workflow, use the Process tab in the Add Life Event form.

The forms you select in the Welcome, Summary, and Confirmation lists override the forms that are set up in the benefit enrollment workflow that is being used as the basis for the life event workflow. For example, if you do not select a Welcome form, no welcome form displays in the life event workflow, even if one is set up in the original benefit enrollment workflow.

To select the forms to use in the workflow:

  1. In the Add Life Event form, select the Process tab.

  2. Select the Welcome form to use. This form is optional.

  3. Select the benefit Summary form to use. This form works in conjunction with the Confirmation form and is required.

  4. Select the Confirmation form to use. This form is required.

  5. If needed, select Approve every employee enrollment for this Life Event. To use this option, you must set up a global approvals policy. .

Selecting Notifications

To select the notifications to use for the life event workflow, use the Notifications tab in the Add Life Event form.

The notifications you select override the notifications that are set up in the benefit enrollment workflow that is being used as the basis for the life event workflow. If you do not enable a notification for the life event workflow in this tab, it is not sent, even if it is set up in the original benefit plan workflow.

You must set up notifications before they can be assigned to a life event workflow. The notification’s template must have the Workflow Type Life Event selected, and must be set up with recipients to display in the table and be available for life events.

To select notifications:

  1. In the Add Life Event form, select the Notifications tab.

  2. In the Enabled column, select each notification to use for the life event workflow. You can select as many as needed.

Option — Description

Notification Template — The name of the notification template.

Type — The Trigger Type specified in the notification template.

Condition — The Trigger Condition specified in the notification template.

Days — If the Type is Time-based, the number of days applied to the Trigger Condition in the notification template displays. Otherwise, the table displays N/A.

Recipients — The Notification ID for the notification recipient record attached to the notification.

Selecting Clients for Notifications

To apply notifications to clients, use the Client List tab in the Add Life Event form.

To make notifications available to clients:

  1. In the Add Life Event form, select the Client List tab.

For this option — Define this information

Apply to All Clients — Whether the life event available in the Life Event form is available to all clients at the system level.

Apply to Selected Client(s) — Whether the life event is available to a specific client or a selected list of clients. If selected, the client selection settings display. Select a client from the Available Clients panel and use the arrows to move it to the Selected Client(s) panel. You can add as many clients as needed. Selected clients can access the life event in the Life Event form. You can use the Search box to search for a specific client.

Override Existing Code — Whether the life event code is meant to replace an existing code. For example, you might create an alternate Birth life event code with different settings that are intended for a specific client. In such a case, select Override Existing Code and select the existing Birth code to override for the client selected from the list.

  1. Click Create Code to save the life event. The life event displays in the table and is available for administrators to select when initiating a life event record in the Life Event form.

Initiating a Life Event in the Employee Portal

If you want the employee to be able to initiate the life event in EP, select the components the employee can access in the Employee Portal Components tab in the Add Life Event form.

Note: Note: The Initiated By option in the General tab must be set to either Employee or Either for the life event available in EP.

You can add as many components as needed and use Search to find a specific component in the list. The available components are:

  • Address

  • Contact Info

  • Direct Deposit

  • Emergency Contacts

  • Personal Info

  • Tax Withholding

To enable employees to initiate a life event in the employee portal:

  • Select the components to include from the Available Components panel and use the arrows to move them to the Selected Components panel.

Editing a Workflow Code

After you create and save a life event workflow code it displays in the table, where you can click Edit to modify it.

The Audit tab displays in the Edit Life Event form if you edit the workflow code. This tab displays any changes that were made.
