Viewing Life Event Codes and Clients

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/LEM_Viewing_Life_Events.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/LEM_Viewing_Life_Events.htm

Viewing Life Event Codes and Clients

The Life Event Maintenance form is divided into two tabs, Codes and Clients. Existing life event work codes display in the main table in the Codes tab, and client information displays in the main table in the Clients tab.

You can:

  • Sort your view of the tables in the Codes and Clients tabs by clicking the column headers and selecting Sort Ascending, Sort Descending, or Hide Column.

  • Use the Search box to search for a specific record as needed.

  • Use the View list to select the number of records to view in either table (25, 50, 100).

  • Use the navigation arrows to scroll through records.

If you edit settings, the settings change for all clients, not just the client you are viewing. If you want to change settings for just one client, create an alternate life event workflow code.

To access the Life Event Maintenance form:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Life Event Maintenance. The Life Event Maintenance form opens. The Codes tab is displayed by default.

Viewing Codes

The Codes tab displays information about the event workflow codes. The main table list all codes you created, as well as the pre-defined life event codes provided in Benefits Enrollment.

Changing Predefined Life Event Codes

The system provides the following predefined codes. You can modify them as needed.

Predefined codes have default settings, but you need to reconfigure them base on the settings your organization requires. Review and modify the settings as needed before you use them for life events.

Code Life Event
MRG Marriage
DP Domestic partnership
BTH Birth or adoption
ADPT Placement for adoption
DIV Dissolution of marriage
SDTH Death of spouse
DDTH Dependent’s Death
DLE Dependent Ceases to Satisfy Eligibility
DDIS Dependent becomes disabled
SGC Spouse becomes employed
LCOV Loss of existing coverage
PF Part Time to Full-Time
LFT Loss of full-time status (20-29 hours per week)
BLOA Begin unpaid leave (30+ days)
RLOA Return from unpaid leave (30+ days)
COR Change of Residence

To customize the pre-defined codes:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Life Event Maintenance. The Life Event Maintenance form opens.

  3. Select the Codes tab.

For this option — Define this information

Life Event — The name of the life event workflow code.

Code — The code for the life event workflow.

Override Code — In some instances, you might create a version of a life event workflow code to override another existing code for a specific client or group of clients. The overridden code displays in this column.

Client List — The clients who are assigned the life event code.

Offer Type — The offer types included in the life event workflow code. You can click Show All to display the offer type icons, or click Show Less to display only the number of offer types.

Action — Click Remove to delete the life event workflow code. Click Edit to modify the life event workflow code’s settings.

Displaying Client Code Asssignments

The Clients tab displays information about clients and lists the codes assigned to them. You can also view details on individual clients as needed.

To view clients and the codes assigned to them:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Life Event Maintenance. The Life Event Maintenance form opens.

  3. Select the Clients tab. The following information is shown about each client.

Column Description
Client ID The client’s ID.
Client Name The name of the client.
Codes Assigned The life event workflow codes assigned to the client. You can click Show All to display the code icons, or click Show Less to display only the number of codes.
Action You can click View Details for a detailed view of the codes assigned to the client. The View Details form displays. - You can select any code and view its settings as needed. The code settings display in read-only format. - All code information is read-only with the exception of the Omit Code option. You can select this option to remove a life event workflow code for the client. - If you need to edit settings for the code, you can click Go To [Code] Life Event Details. The Edit Life Event form opens, where you can edit settings.
  • You can select any code and view its settings as needed. The code settings display in read-only format.
  • All code information is read-only with the exception of the Omit Code option. You can select this option to remove a life event workflow code for the client.

  • If you need to edit settings for the code, you can click Go To [Code] Life Event Details. The Edit Life Event form opens, where you can edit settings.

Assigning Life Event Workflows

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Life_Event.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Life_Event.htm

Assigning Life Event Workflows

Use the Life Event form to assign life event workflows to employees. When you assign the workflow, the system creates a web enrollment record for the employee, and the employee can complete the enrollment in EP. You create life events in Life Event Maintenance.

To assign life event workflows to employees:

  1. Click the HR menu.

  2. From HR|Action, select Life Event. The Life Event form opens.

  3. Complete these fields as required:

Field — Description

Employee — Click Employee to open an Employee selector. Select from the displayed list, or type in a search term to refine the search results.

Life Events — Select a life event to apply to the employee. Life events include items such as Marriage or Change in Spouse Employment.

Life Event Date — Select a date when the life event occurred from the calendar picker.

Date Notified — Select a notification date for the life event from the calendar picker.

  1. Click Save.

Maintaining Life Event Codes

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Life_Event_Maintenance.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Life_Event_Maintenance.htm

Maintaining Life Event Codes

To set up workflow codes for employee life events such as marriage, divorce, and birth, use the Life Event Maintenance form. Benefits Enrollment provides predefined life event codes that your organization can configure as needed, but you can also create your own as needed.

After you set up the life event workflow, administrators can assign the life events to employees in the Life Event form.

After you save the life event assignment, the system generates an enrollment workflow and the employee can complete the life event workflow in EP.

Configuring Required System Settings

You must set up the following settings before creating a new life event record:

  • Set up all benefit plans to include in the life event workflow. Assign them the correct Offer Type to include in the offer type forms assigned to the workflow.

  • In the Client Workflow Grid, assign a benefit enrollment workflow that corresponds to the effective date to use for the life event workflow. The life event workflow is based on this pre-existing benefit enrollment workflow, and the life event code settings serve as overrides.

  • Set up Benefit Summary and Confirmation forms to use in the life event workflow in the Forms Library. You can also set up an optional Welcome form. You can customize these forms as needed for the life event.

  • Set up the notifications to send for each life event. The notification templates must have the Workflow Type Life Event selected, and must be set up with recipients for the notifications to be available.

  • If you want the system to automatically approve every employee enrollment for the life event, set up a global approvals policy. See the System Adminstration Guide or Approvals Guide.

Logging in as an Employee

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Logging%20in%20as%20an%20Employee.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Logging in as an Employee.htm

Logging in as an Employee

Service providers, worksite managers, and worksite trusted advisors who are set up with the appropriate user role can use the Proxy Login form to access Benefits Enrollment and Onboarding on behalf of an employee.

Access to an employee’s Benefits Enrollment requires the Enrollment Proxy user role, and access to an employee’s Onboarding requires the Onboarding Proxy user role.

Note: Note: User roles are assigned using the User Roles form. By acting as a proxy for the employee, users acknowledge that they have received authorization to view the employee’s data and, for Benefits Enrollment, to make elections on the employee’s behalf.

To log in as an employee:

  1. On the HR menu, select HR|Action > Proxy Login. Alternatively, access the Employee Details form, click the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] , and select Proxy Login. The Proxy Login form displays.

Note: You can also proxy login as a worksite manager/worksite trusted advisor by selecting Worksite Manager Proxy on the Actions menu

[Image: screenshot]

. When the Proxy Login form displays, the system automatically displays the Worksite Manager/Trusted Advisor option in the Activity field and the selected employee’s username displays in the User ID field.

  1. Complete the following fields as appropriate:

Field — Description

Activity — Select whether to access Benefits Enrollment or Onboarding as an employee (Benefits Enrollment) or a user (Worksite Manager/Trusted Advisor).

Employee ID/User ID — Enter the ID number of the employee or user as whom to log in. Alternatively, click the link to select an employee or user from a list. > Note: Note: The Employee ID field automatically changes to the User ID field if you select the Worksite Manager/Trusted Advisor option in the Activity field.

Date — Enter the date the proxy login is being performed. This must be the current date or a previous date.

Proxy approval received by — Select the method used to obtain the employee’s approval for the proxy login.

Proxy Reason — Enter the reason for the proxy login.

  1. Click Start. Based on the selection in the Activity field, the selected employee’s Benefits Enrollment or Onboarding display in a new browser window.

Note: Note: When Onboarding is accessed using the Proxy Login form, changes to the forms included in the workflow can be made, but changes cannot be saved and forms cannot be submitted.

Managing the Workflow Library (Benefits Enrollment)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Managing%20the%20Workflow%20Library%20(BE).htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Managing the Workflow Library (BE).htm

Managing the Workflow Library (Benefits Enrollment)

Use the Workflow Library to create workflows for enrollments. A typical enrollment workflow might begin with an optional Welcome form, then present forms for medical, dental, vision plans, long-term and short-term disability plans, and so on. You can organize forms into categories for easier navigation.

The final forms in a workflow include a Benefits Summary form (which summarizes the employee’s benefit selections and allows the opportunity to make changes), and a Confirmation form (where the employee submits benefit selections and completes the enrollment process). These are required.

Before you begin:

  • Plan the organization of the information to present during enrollments so that the process flows efficiently and makes sense.

To view workflows used for Benefits Enrollment:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change,select Workflow Library. The Workflow Library opens.

  3. In Workflow Type, select Enrollment. You can sort the table view by clicking the column headings.

Managing Forms (Benefits Enrollment)

To create and maintain forms, use the Form Library.

These forms include Benefit Summary forms and Confirmation forms, which are required for enrollments to process correctly, as well as optional Welcome forms. PrismHR provides prebuilt forms that can be used with minimal customization and the ability to create custom forms for additional enrollment options.

Managing Versions and Revisions

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Managing_Versions_Revisions.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Managing_Versions_Revisions.htm

Managing Versions and Revisions

The Form Library and Workflow Library both maintain multiple versions and revisions for forms and workflows. Revisions are set automatically when you submit a new form or workflow or modify an existing one.

  • When you create a new form or workflow, the Version automatically sets to 1.0.

  • If you change a form or workflow and select the option to have the changes go into effect immediately, the system adds a revision number after the decimal point in the version number (1.1, 1.2, and so on).

The system also immediately sets the Status to Active, and sets the current date as the Start Date. Since there can be only one active version of the form, the system changes the status of any previous active version to Inactive and sets the current date as its end date.

  • If you change a form or workflow and select the option to have the changes go into effect at a future date, the version number automatically sets to the next number in the sequence, and the revision number resets to zero (2.0, 3.0, and so on).

The system sets the new version’s status to Active on the start date, which you assign when you submit the form or workflow. Since there can only be one active version, the system changes the status of any previous active version to Inactive and sets the current date as its end date.

You can view and compare different versions and revisions in both libraries by clicking the form ID or workflow ID in the table. The Versions form opens, displaying all existing versions and revisions.

  • To modify a form or workflow version or revision, click the ID.

  • To compare two versions or revisions of a form or workflow, select the two you want to compare and click Compare. The Comparison panel opens below the main table in the form (you must scroll down to view data) and displays settings for both.

Suspending Benefit Enrollments

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/New_Hire_Suspension_Process.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/New_Hire_Suspension_Process.htm

Suspending Benefit Enrollments

The new hire suspension process lets organizations suspend benefit enrollments for new hires if the benefits setup is not yet finalized for the client. The purpose is to ensure that new hires are not offered benefits based on obsolete settings. (PrismHR Benefits Enrollment only.)

The new hire suspension process works as follows:

  • On the System Parameters form, the organization enters the SUSPENDENH custom feature code. This code activates the new hire suspension process at the system level.

  • On the Client Details form Benefits tab, administrators setting up benefits for a client define the Benefits Rules Good Through Date to indicate the date up to which the current enrollment offerings for new hires is valid.

  • When the validity date passes, the system initiates the new hire suspension process to prevent benefits from being offered online to new hires.

  • The system runs the new hire suspension process automatically once a day when the first administrator logs on to a SCORPEO or HRP.ADMIN account and applies the suspension to any new hires. (PrismHR customers must log on to their HRP.ADMIN account every day for this process to run). Administrators can also manually run the process once a day (at any time) using the Suspended Employee Enrollment Process form.

  • A benefits administrator then reviews and updates the benefits offered, and adjusts the Benefits Rules Good Through Date when the benefit offerings are ready for new enrollments. As long as the enrollment workflow has the notification enabled, employees receive a notification that they can now enroll online.

  • Users can run the Suspended Enrollment Employee Report to view employees whose benefits enrollments are suspended or on hold.

  • The Benefits Good Thru Date cannot be blank if the client had a Good Thru Date at any time in their record.

To enable the new hire suspension process at the system level:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select System Parameters. The System Parameters form opens.

  3. Click the Custom Feature Codes link. From the list, select SUSPENDENH.

  4. Click Save.

To indicate the latest validity date for enrollment offerings for new hires:

  1. Click the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change, select Client Details. The Client Details form opens.

  3. On the Benefits tab, enter a date in Benefits Rules Good Through Date. When this date passes, the system initiates the new hire suspension process. If the system finds that an employee’s earliest potential eligibility date exceeds the Benefits Rules Good Through Date, it suspends the employee’s enrollment.

Viewing the Enrollment Suspended Until Date

On the Employee Details form Other tab, you can view records for employees whose enrollments are suspended. They display a date in the Enrollment Suspended Until field. This date indicates the next date that the new hire suspension process will review the record again. The date automatically advances by one day each time the process determines that the record should remain suspended. For more information, see the Human Resources User Guide.

Manually Running the Suspended Employee Enrollment Process

The system runs the New Hire Suspension Process automatically once a day and applies the suspension to any new hires. If needed, administrators can use the Suspended Employee Enrollment Process form to manually suspend benefit enrollments for new hires at any time.

Viewing the Suspended Enrollment Employee Report

You can also run the Suspended Enrollment Employee Report to view employees whose benefits enrollments are suspended. For more information, see Suspended Employee Enrollment.

Notification Audit

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Notification_Audit_Report.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Notification_Audit_Report.htm

Notification Audit

The Notification Audit Report provides a listing of notifications sent to all recipient types (for example, employees, generic recipients, message groups). You can run the report for a specific client and employee or for all clients and employees.

Note: Note: The Notification Audit Report form contains client and employee‑level security. Only clients and employees you have access to display in the report.

To run the Notification Audit report:

  1. Click Reports.

  2. From Benefits|Report, select Notification Audit Report. The Notification Audit Report form opens.

  3. Enter the Client ID for the report. If you leave the Client ID blank, the report runs for all clients. The Employee ID field displays.

  4. If you want to run the report for a specific employee, enter an Employee ID. If you leave Employee ID blank, the report runs for all employes.

  5. Select the Start Date and the End Date from the respective date pickers. Please ensure that the date range you provide in these fields must be a range within a year. These two fields are mandatory.

  6. Click Run. The report displays information in the following columns.

Column — Description

Client ID — The client ID.

Employee ID — The employee ID.

Notification Type — The notification trigger type specified in the template.

Notification Date — The date the notification was sent.

Notification Time — The time the notification was sent.

Triggering Workflow — The ID that is created when new messages are triggered.

Notification Template ID — The Notification template sent, including information on language (for example, en_US) and the template ID number.

Notification template name — The name of the notification template for you to understand the nature of the notification sent to users.

Recipients — Recipients for the notification.

Recipient Email — Email of the recipients where the notification is to be sent.

Message ID — Message ID generated by the engine that identifies the notification sent.

Using the Notification Control Panel (Benefits Enrollment)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Notification_Control_Panel_BE.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Notification_Control_Panel_BE.htm

Using the Notification Control Panel (Benefits Enrollment)

Use the BE Notify section in the Notification Control Panel to:

  • Start or stop the notifications engine during enrollments

  • Determine the frequency at which the notifications engine sends notifications

Note: Note: Settings are at the system level. You can also access the New Message form in the Actions menu

[Image: screenshot]

.

Notifications are added to workflows in the Workflow Library. During enrollments, the system sends out event and time-based notifications attached to the workflows to the notification recipients specified.

Note: Note: The system sends notifications only for organizations that have the Enabled Benefits Enrollment option selected in Global Configuration. If the option is not checked, the Notification Control Panel does not send notifications. You can turn notifications off at the client-level in the Client Workflow Grid.

  • Open enrollment notifications are sent once a day (typically at midnight).

  • Benefits enrollment notifications are sent at the interval specified.

  • Approval notifications are sent immediately.

Note: Note: The background process automatically runs BE Notify, Payday Notify, ACH Processing, and Payroll Reminders. However, if BE Notify is turned off, the other processes continue to run.

Setting Up Benefits Enrollment Notification Controls

The PrismHR Customer Support Team recommends that you set up open enrollment workflows at least one day before the window opens.

To set up benefits enrollment notification controls:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From Operations|Action, select Notification Control Panel. The Notification Control Panel opens.

For this option — Define this information

BE Notify Interval (mins) — The number of minutes to use for the current benefits enrollment interval. This interval determines how often the system checks for new hires and sends the notifications specified in the benefits enrollment workflow. You can enter a number from 5 to 1440 minutes. > Note: Note: The OB Notify Interval (mins) and OB Notify Date options in the OB Notify section are used only in Onboarding and do not affect Benefits Enrollment.

OE Notify Date — The most recent date when the system sent notifications for open enrollment. It populates automatically. By default, the notifications engine sends notifications for open enrollment every day. If you need to manually resend a notification for an enrollment (for example, if the notification was not received by employees, or if there is a change to the notification template), delete the displayed date, and then click Submit. The system resends notifications using the current date. When the process completes, the current date displays. > Note: Note: The OB Notify Interval (mins) and OB Notify Date options in the OB Notify section are used only in Onboarding and do not affect Benefits Enrollment.

Client — Whether to restrict notifications to one specified client. This is typically done only for testing notifications using a test client (for example, when you test to confirm that the enrollment user works correctly). Leave this field blank to apply settings at the system level.

  1. To submit your settings and start the notifications engine, click Save.

Click this in the BE Notify section — To do this

Status — Check whether the notifications engine is running or not running. The status displays the process ID (PID) number.

Stop — Stop the system from sending notifications. The Stop option changes to Start and the status changes to not running. The request to stop notifications takes effect immediately. However, it can take a few minutes for the updated status to display correctly. > Note: Note: The Stop button in the OB Notify section is used only in Onboarding and does not affect Benefits Enrollment.

Start — Start the notifications engine without changing settings. The Start option changes to Stop and the status changes to running. If you stop and then restart the notifications engine, the PID number resets and a new PID displays.

Cancel — Close without changing settings.

Setting Up Notification Recipients (Benefits Enrollment)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Notification_Recipients_BE.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Notification_Recipients_BE.htm

Setting Up Notification Recipients (Benefits Enrollment)

To set up recipients for enrollment notifications created using the Notification Templates form, use the Notification Recipients form.

This form can also be used to modify or delete existing notification recipient records. However, recipient records that are currently associated with a notification template cannot be deleted.

[Image: screenshot]

To set up notification recipients:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Notification Recipients. The Notification Recipients form opens.

  3. Complete the following fields:

Field — Action

Notification ID — Enter an existing notification ID, or click Setup a new Notificationcreate a new notification.

Name — Enter a name for the notification recipient group. The name displays on the Workflow Library form in the list of notification users you can add to workflows.

Description — (Optional) Enter a description for the notification.

  1. Select recipients for the notification using the following panels as appropriate:

Panel — Description

To Group Recipients — Primary recipients for the notification, organized by group (for example, Internal Employees, Managers, and so on). To set up groups, use the Message Center Groups form. > Note: Note: Message groups that include client records cannot be used in notification recipient records.

To Generic — Primary recipients for the notification, organized by role: Employee, Client Benefit Rep, Human Resources, Client Manager, I-9 Section 2 Approver. The Client Benefit Rep, Human Resources, and Client Manager roles can be assigned to users using the Users form. The I-9 Section 2 Approver role is specific to Onboarding and is not used in Benefits Enrollment.

CC Group Recipients — Additional recipients to copy publicly, organized by group.

CC Generic — Additional recipients to copy publicly, organized by role.

BCC Group Recipients — Additional recipients to copy privately, organized by group.

BCC Generic — Additional recipients to copy privately, organized by role.

  1. In the Notification Template panel, enter the ID number of the template to use in the Template ID field. The name and description associated with the template are automatically entered in the appropriate fields, and the appropriate check boxes are selected for the associated workflow type and message delivery method.

  2. Click Save.

Setting Up Notification Templates (Benefits Enrollment)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Notification_Templates_BE.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Notification_Templates_BE.htm

Setting Up Notification Templates (Benefits Enrollment)

The Notification Templates form is used to create templates for the notifications that are sent to employees or groups during benefit enrollments, open enrollments, anytime enrollments, or life event enrollments. Each template specifies the content that is included in the notification, such as the subject and message body, as well as the criteria for sending the notification and the communication method used to send the notification.

When defining notification content, substitution variables can be used to replace generic information with specific information from PrismHR.

Once the template has been created, the Notification Recipients form can be used to define the recipients who receive the notification. Refer to Setting Up Notification Recipients (Benefits Enrollment) for more information. In addition, the notifications can be added to a workflow using the Workflow Library. Refer to Creating a Workflow (Benefits Enrollment) for more information.

Note: Note: Notification templates that have been attached to a workflow or a notification recipient record cannot be deleted.

To set up a notification template:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Notification Templates. The Notification Templates form displays.

  3. In the Notification Templates panel, complete the following fields:

Field — Action

Template Id — To modify an existing template, enter a template ID or click Template Id to display a list of existing templates. To create a new template, click Create a New Template. An available template ID is entered in the Template Id field.

Name — Enter a name for the template. This name identifies the template and displays in the list of available templates when assigning notification recipients.

Description — Enter a description of the template.

Language Default — For future development. Does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR.

Subject — Enter a subject for notifications sent using the template. > Note: Note: Do not use square brackets ([ or ]) in this field as doing so will prevent recipients from receiving notifications sent using this template.

Message Body — Enter the body text for the notifications sent using the template.

SMS Body — For future development. Does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR.

  1. In the Workflow Type panel, select the type of enrollment during which to send notifications using this template.

  2. In the Notification Triggers panel, complete the following fields:

Field Action
Trigger Type Select a notification trigger type. - Time Based: Notifications are sent a specific number of days before the workflow’s start date or due date. If the notification engine is set up to check in the morning, the notifications are sent in the morning. If the notification engine is set up to check every other day, the notifications are sent every other day. Notification engine settings are defined using the Notification Control Panel. Refer to Using the Notification Control Panel (Benefits Enrollment) for more information. - Event Based: Notifications are sent when a specific event occurs, such as when a benefits enrollment workflow is completed. The notification engine runs in the background and periodically checks to see whether there are notifications to send and sends event-based notifications when they turn up during the check.
  • Time Based: Notifications are sent a specific number of days before the workflow’s start date or due date.
  • Event Based: Notifications are sent when a specific event occurs, such as when a benefits enrollment workflow is completed. | Trigger Condition | If Time Based is selected in the Trigger Type drop-down, the following options are available: - X Days before start date For a new hire, the start date is when the new hire is entered into PrismHR. The Benefits Enrollment workflow is launched when entering the employee (unless suspended ENH occurs). For open enrollment, the start date is when the open enrollment period begins based on the workflow grid. For more information, refer to Assigning Workflows at the Client Level or Assigning Workflows at the System Level. For a life event, the start date is when the sponsor or employee launches the life event. - X Days before due date For a new hire, the due date is calculated by adding the new hire’s start date plus the entry in the BE Open Window Length field defined on the workflow grid. For open enrollment, the due date is when the open enrollment window ends based on the workflow grid. For a life event, the due date is calculated by adding the new hire’s start date plus the entry defined for the life event code using the Window Length field on the Life Event Maintenance form. - X Days before hire date For a new hire, the hire date is the date the employee is registered in the system as a hired employee. This date may be different from the start date. - X Days after hire date For a new hire, the hire date is the date the employee is registered in the system as a hired employee. This date may be different from the start date. If Event Based is selected in the Trigger Type drop-down, the following options are available: - Employee submits workflow, no approval - Employee submits workflow, approval required - Employee submits form - Approver confirms - Approver rejects - Workflow open window overlap This trigger type is used to send notifications to employees who have an overlap in a benefits enrollment and open enrollment. To be triggered correctly, this notification must be added to the overlapping enrollment workflow that would be completed first by the employee. - Overlap Posting Failure This trigger type is only used to send a notification when Auto post BE/OE enrollments when overlap with OE is activated in the Enrollment Options form. |
  • X Days before start date
  • X Days before due date
  • X Days before hire date
  • X Days after hire date
  • Employee submits workflow, no approval
  • Employee submits workflow, approval required
  • Employee submits form
  • Approver confirms
  • Approver rejects
  • Workflow open window overlap
  • Overlap Posting Failure | Number of Days | Enter the number of days before the workflow’s start date or due date at which to send the notification. > Note: Note: This field is only available if Time Based is selected in the Trigger Type drop-down. |
  1. In the Message Delivery Method panel, select Email to send the notification using email.

Note: Note: The Send SMS option is for future development and does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR.

  1. Click Save.

Creating a New Batch

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/PBE_Creating_a_New_Batch.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/PBE_Creating_a_New_Batch.htm

Creating a New Batch

To create a new batch for posting, use the Post Benefit Enrollment form. Once created, the new batch displays in the table. You can then review the batch. After all enrollments pass review and their status display as Ready to Post, you can post the batch.

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From Operations|Action, select Post Benefit Enrollments. The Post Benefit Enrollments form opens.

  3. Click Start New Batch. The New Batch View displays, where you can filter and select the enrollments to include.

  4. Use Batch Filters to filter the list and narrow the view to only those enrollments you want to include. The Batch Filters panel includes:

Column — Description

Client ID — The Client ID for the client whose enrollments to display. You can also click a Client ID listed in the table to populate this field. Additionally, you can select multiple Client IDs using the selection modal.

Effective On or After — A date to view enrollments that are on or after that date.

Effective On or Before — A date to view enrollments that are on or before that date.

Name Starting — Letters from the start of an employee’s name to filter enrollments and locate a specific employee.

Name Ending — Letters from the end of an employee’s name to filter enrollments and locate a specific employee.

Enrollment Type — Whether to filter enrollments by (OE) Open Enrollments or (BE) Benefit Enrollments.

Status Class — The status of the employee. You can select from Active, On Leave, or Terminated.

Life Event — The life event code and description.

Benefit Group — A benefit group by which to filter enrollments.

Pay Group — A pay group by which to filter enrollments.

  1. When the enrollments to include in the batch display in the filtered view, click Create Batch. The Batch Name box opens.

  2. Enter the Batch Name.

  3. Click Create. The system prompts for confirmation.

  4. Click Yes. The system creates the batches and automatically generates the Batch Number. The new batch displays in the Batch Detail View, listing the selected enrollments.

========================================================================

Posting a Batch

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/PBE_Posting_a_Batch.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/PBE_Posting_a_Batch.htm

Posting a Batch

When all enrollments in the batch are reviewed and display the Ready to Post enrollment status, you can post the batch by clicking Post.

The system runs a final check on the enrollments included in the batch.

  • If any enrollments contain errors, the posting process stops. You must fix the errors or remove the failed enrollments from the batch before you can post the batch. The system prompts you to specify whether to save the failed enrollments to a new batch.

  • If posting is successful, the Batch Detail View of the form displays with the Enrollment Status Post Complete.

Posting Enrollments Automatically

When your organization selects Auto post BE/OE enrollments when overlap with OE in the Enrollment Options form, the system automatically posts any new enrollment records created for employees during an active open enrollment period. If the enrollment posts successfully, a new enrollment record is automatically created for the employee for the active open enrollment.

If posting for this new enrollment record fails, the posting batch that includes the new enrollment does not post but remains in the posting queue in the Post Benefit Enrollments form. The enrollment record displays the status Review Failed. In such a case, you must manually fix all errors in the enrollment before it can post.

========================================================================

Reviewing a Batch

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/PBE_Reviewing_a_Batch.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/PBE_Reviewing_a_Batch.htm

Reviewing a Batch

Click Review to review a batch when its status is Ready to Review. When the review is complete, the Batch Detail View displays. If the review process finds no errors in an enrollment, it returns the enrollment status Ready to Post. The enrollment can now be posted.

Note: Note: All enrollments included in the batch must have the Ready to Post enrollment status to successfully post.

If the review process finds errors in any of the enrollment records:

The system displays the following alert: Would you like to move the enrollments that failed review to a new batch so you can continue to post this batch? and prompts you to click Yes or No.

  • If No, the system displays the following message Please review the Error Report, fix any errors, and review this batch again before attempting to post. The system returns you to the batch and displays the enrollment status Review Failed.

Records that failed to post are not removed from the batch. The Error Report and Change Report options display at the bottom of the batch table:

  • Click Error Report to run the Benefit Enrollment Review Error Report to review specific enrollment errors in the batch.

  • Click Change Report to run the Benefit Enrollment Review Change Report to review the changes and fixes made to the enrollments.

  • If Yes, the system prompts you to enter a batch name and to confirm that you want to proceed. The system displays the following message: Failing enrollments have been moved to a new batch [Batch Name] [Batch Number].

The system moves the Benefit Enrollment Review Error Report and the Benefit Enrollment Review Change Report for these enrollments to the new batch where you can view them without doing another review. The system allows you to remain in the original batch where you can then post the batch with the remaining enrollment records.

You must fix all enrollment errors before the enrollment can be posted with a batch. You can remove an enrollment from the batch at any time by clicking selecting one or more enrollments and clicking Remove. When you remove an enrollment, it returns to the list of available enrollments.

After you fix the enrollment errors, review the batch again until the errors are fixed. Each time you review, the Benefit Enrollment Review Error Report and the Benefit Enrollment Review Change Report are updated. You can review the batch as many times as needed before posting.

Reviewing Errors that Can Occur When Posting a Batch

Use the Benefit Enrollment Review Error Report to review errors in the enrollment records that can occur when posting a batch in the Post Benefit Enrollments form. You must fix all errors for the system to successfully post the enrollment record.

The following information is shown for each error.

  • Company: The client ID.

  • Employee: The employee ID.

  • Employee Name: The employee name.

  • Offer Type: The offer type for the benefit plan that generated the error message.

  • Error Message: The error message.

Reviewing Changes Made When Fixing Errors

After posting a batch on the Post Benefit Enrollments form and correcting any errors, you can use the Benefit Enrollment Review Change Report to review changes made to the enrollment records during that process.

Change Report Output

The following information is displayed on the report:

  • Company: The client ID.

  • Employee: The employee ID.

  • Employee Name: The employee name.

  • Offer Type: The offer type for the benefit plan that generated the error message.

  • Plan ID: The plan ID for the benefit plan.

  • Plan Name: The name of the benefit plan.

  • Type: The plan type or tier selected for the benefit plan.

  • Action: A description of the change that was made to the enrollment.

Viewing Unposted Batches

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/PBE_Viewing_Batches.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/PBE_Viewing_Batches.htm

Viewing Unposted Batches

Before you can post benefit enrollments, you must organize the enrollment records to include into batches. By default, the Post Benefit Enrollments form displays a list of batches that are not yet posted.

  • The number of enrollments that are not included in a batch displays in parentheses next to the Start New Batch link. You can click Start New Batch to create a new batch using these available enrollments.

  • You can view batches that were successfully posted by selecting Display Posted Batches Only.

The table lists information about each batch in the following columns.

Column — Description

Batch Name — The batch’s name. Click to open the Batch Detail View.

Batch Number — The batch’s number. Click to open the Batch Detail View.

Status — The batch status.

Date Created — The date the batch was created.

Last Updated — The date of the latest change made to the batch.

Enrollments — The number of enrollments included in the batch.

Needs Review — The number of enrollments in the batch that need to be reviewed before posting. You can view the enrollments in the Batch Detail View.

Viewing Batch Details

To view a specific batch and the enrollments it includes, enter the Batch Number and press Enter. You can also access the Batch Detail View by clicking the Batch Name or Batch Number of a batch displayed in the batch list.

The Batch Detail View displays information about the batch selected and lists the included enrollments.

Column Description
Confirm # The confirmation number for the enrollment record. You can click this link to view the enrollment details in the Employee Enrollment Summary in the Benefit Enrollment Detail Inquiry form.
Client Name The client name.
Client ID The client ID.
Employee Name The employee name.
Employee ID The employee ID.
Enrollment Type The enrollment type (Open Enrollment or New Hire Enrollment).
Start Date The enrollment start date.
End Date The enrollment end date.
Effective Date The enrollment effective date.
Enrollment Status The enrollment status. - If the status is Ready to Post for all enrollments in the batch, you can post the batch. - If the status for any included enrollment is Ready to Review, the batch was not reviewed for posting. You must review the batch before you can post. - If the status for any included enrollment is Review Failed, the review process found a problem in the enrollment record. You must fix the enrollment record and run the review process again, or remove the enrollment from the batch before you can post. - You can remove an enrollment from the batch by clicking [Image: screenshot] (Remove). Once removed, the enrollment is returned to the list of available enrollments.
  • If the status is Ready to Post for all enrollments in the batch, you can post the batch.
  • If the status for any included enrollment is Ready to Review, the batch was not reviewed for posting. You must review the batch before you can post.
  • If the status for any included enrollment is Review Failed, the review process found a problem in the enrollment record. You must fix the enrollment record and run the review process again, or remove the enrollment from the batch before you can post.
  • You can remove an enrollment from the batch by clicking [Image: screenshot] (Remove). Once removed, the enrollment is returned to the list of available enrollments. In batches where they are available, you can view the Benefit Enrollment Review Error Report and the Benefit Enrollment Review Change Report for the batch by clicking Error Report and Change Report.

Post Status The posting status for the enrollment.
Status Date The date the post status was updated.
Effective Date The enrollment effective date.
Start Date The start date for the enrollment.
End Date The end date for the enrollment.

The form displays the following information in the Offer Type panel.

Column Description
Offer Type The Offer Type and the Post Status display at the top of the panel.
Offered Plans All plans offered in the enrollment for the selected offer type.
Description The plan description.
Effective Date The plan’s effective date
Selected The plan selected by the employee displays in bold with a checkmark in this column.

The following additional information can display depending on the type of benefit plan selected and its settings.

Column — Description

Plan Type Enrolled In — The plan type used for the enrollment in the plan.

Employee Contribution Rate — The employee’s contribution.

PCP ID — The ID for the employee’s primary care physician.

PCP Name — The name of the employee’s primary care physician.

Plan Guaranteed Issue Amount — The guaranteed issue (GI) amount for a life insurance or disability plan.

Coverage — The amount of coverage provided for the employee up to the GI limit in a life insurance or disability plan. Coverage amounts for spouse and dependents display depending on plan type.

Premium — Premium amounts for spouse and dependents display depending on plan type.

GI Overage — The amount of coverage that is over the GI limit.

The form displays the following information in the Covered Dependents panel.

Column Description
ID The dependent’s ID.
Name The dependent’s name.
Relation The dependent’s relationship to the employee.
DOB The dependent’s date of birth.
Age The dependent’s age.
Disabled Whether the dependent is disabled.
Tobacco User Whether the dependent is a tobacco user.
Student Whether the dependent is a student.
PCP ID The ID for the dependent’s primary care physician.
PCP Name The name of the dependent’s PCP.

The form displays the following information in the Beneficiaries panel.

Column Description
ID The beneficiary’s ID.
Name The name of the beneficiary.
Relation The beneficiary’s relationship to the employee.
Primary The percentage allocated by the employee for primary beneficiaries.
Contingent The percentage allocated by the employee for contingent beneficiaries.

Pre-Filtering Bulk Launch Benefit Workflows

Before launching the Bulk Launch Benefit Workflow form, pre-filtering options display that let you select specific criteria (clients, employhment type, employment status) to narrow results, which improves response time. None of these filters are required.

To set a pre-filter:

  1. Select one or more pre-filter options. You can specify Client(s), Employment Type(s), and Employment Status.

  2. Click Continue to complete pre-filtering. The main Bulk Launch Benefit Workflow form displays.

Creating Forms from Prebuilt Forms

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Prebuilt_Form_BE.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Prebuilt_Form_BE.htm

Creating Forms from Prebuilt Forms

You can create a new form in the Form Library using one of the prebuilt forms that Benefits Enrollment provides. Using this option, you can edit the default text in the headers and footers. The rest of the content is prebuilt for you. It is best practice to use a consistent convention for naming forms.

When you submit a form, the system sets its version and revision numbers.

To create a new form based on a prebuilt form:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Form Library. The Form Library opens.

  3. In the row of the prebuilt form to use (medical, dental, vision, life insurance, and so on), select Duplicate. The Duplicate Form displays.

  4. Enter a New ID and New Name for the form.

  5. Click Duplicate. The Customize Prebuilt Form form opens.

  6. On the General tab Version, Revision, Status, Start Date, End Date, Workflow Type, and Offer Type are read-only.

Use this option To define this
Form ID A unique ID for the new form.
Form Name The name of the form. Editable and Required.
Description A description of the form. Editable and Required.
  1. On the Layout tab, click Header and Footer to expand the Header/Footer Detail panel. The Field ID and Field Type are read-only.
Use this option To define this
Field Default Value The default value for the option.
Insert Link For future development. Does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR.
  1. If necessary, select one of the following options:

  • To preview the form before saving, click Preview Form.

  • To return to the Form Library without saving changes, click Back to Forms Library.

  1. Click Save. The Submit Form dialog box prompts you to select one of the following options:

Option — Description

Immediately — The system sets the form status immediately to Active, and sets the current date as the Start Date. Since there can be only one active version of the form, the system changes the status of any prior active version to Inactive and sets the current date as its End Date.

Future Date — You are prompted to enter a Start Date. The system sets the form status to Inactive, changing it to Active on the start date. Since there can be only one active version of the form, the system changes the status of any prior active version to Inactive and sets the start date for the new version as its End Date.

Save as Draft — The form status is set to Draft. The system does not set a Start Date nor does it set version or revision numbers for the form.

  1. Click Submit Form.

Assigning Documentation Rulesets

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Required_Document_Rulesets.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Required_Document_Rulesets.htm

Assigning Documentation Rulesets

To assign one or more required documentation rulesets to a benefit plan, use the Required Documentation Rulesets panel in the Plan Enrollment Details form.

Benefits Enrollment provides the following rulesets:

  • Domestic Partner

  • Dependents with Different Last Names

  • Disabled Dependents

  • Disabled Over-Age Dependents (Florida residents)

  • Disabled Over-Age Dependents (New York residents)

  • Florida Contract Benefit Plans

Setting Up Documentation Rulesets

To set up required documentation and dependent eligibility rulesets, use the Required Documentation Rulesets panel in the Plan Enrollment Details form. This panel displays only for offer types that might require special documents.

To set up required document and dependent eligibility rulesets:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Group Benefit Plans. The Group Benefit Plans form opens.

  3. Click the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] and select Enrollment Details.

  4. In the Required Documentation Rulesets panel, select the effective date for the benefit plan to modify.

A benefit plan can have multiple effective dates with different settings, which are then used for different enrollments. You can click New Effective Date to create a new effective date.

  1. Enter the Domain and Ruleset for the ruleset to attach to the benefit plan. You must manually enter these exactly as follows:
Rule Domain Ruleset
Domestic Partner PRISMHR-BE DOCUMENT-PARTNER
Dependents with Different Last Names PRISMHR-BE DOCUMENT-LASTNAME
Disabled Dependents PRISMHR-BE DOCUMENT-DIS
Disabled Over-Age Dependents (Florida residents) PRISMHR-BE DOCUMENT-FLO
Disabled Over-Age Dependents (New York residents) PRISMHR-BE DOCUMENT-NYO
Florida Contract Benefit Plans PRISMHR-BE DOCUMENT-FLCT
  1. Enter the Domain and Ruleset for the Dependent Eligibility rule. You must manually enter these exactly as follows:
Rule Domain Ruleset
Dependent Eligibility PRISMHR-BE DEPEN-ELIGIBILITY

This rule is required. Before evaluating an employee’s dependents for the benefit plan’s rulesets, the system checks the employee’s dependents’ eligibility for the benefit plan. This check runs only if the Dependent Eligibility rule is set up.

  1. PCP Required: For future development. Does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR.

  2. Click Save.

Evaluating Documentation Rulesets

The following sections explain how the system evaluates and applies documentation rulesets.

Evaluating Dependent Eligibility

Before evaluating an employee’s dependents for the benefit plan’s rulesets, the system checks the employee’s dependents’ eligibility for the benefit plan.

This check runs only if the domain and ruleset are set up for the Dependent Eligibility rule. If the domain and ruleset are not set up for the Dependent Eligibility rule, the benefit plan does not display in the enrollment, and the system does not apply any of the documentation rulesets.

Evaluating Documentation Ruleset Eligibility

When the Dependent Eligibility rule determines that the employee’s dependents are eligible for a benefit plan, the benefit plan displays in the employee’s enrollment.

The system then evaluates the employee’s dependents to see whether they satisfy the conditions for the required documentation rulesets that are set up in the benefit plan. If a required documentation ruleset applies to the dependent (for example, if the employee includes a domestic partner as a dependent in the enrollment), the system triggers the required document for the employee to complete.

Uploading and Tagging Documents

You must upload required documents to the Document Repository and tag them correctly.

Documents attached to a benefit plan display in the employee’s Benefit Summary when the enrollment is complete.

Evaluating Dependent Eligibility

If the Dependent Eligibility rule is set up, the system evaluates dependent eligibility for the benefit plan before determining whether the employee’s dependents satisfy the conditions for the required documentation rulesets.

If the dependent is a domestic partner or has a different last name, the system proceeds to the next step and determines whether the dependent satisfies the required documentation ruleset eligibility conditions. If the dependent does not satisfy these conditions, one of the following sets of conditions must apply for the dependent to be determined to be eligible for the benefit plan.

Condition Set Conditions
Condition Set #1 - The dependent is set up with the Relationship Type Dependent in the Employee Dependents form. - The dependent is a Florida resident, as determined by the dependent’s state (if the dependent has no state entered, the system defaults to the employee’s state). - The benefit plans is either a medical (MED) or gap (GAP) plan, and the dependent’s age is less than 30.
  • The dependent is set up with the Relationship Type Dependent in the Employee Dependents form.
  • The dependent is a Florida resident, as determined by the dependent’s state (if the dependent has no state entered, the system defaults to the employee’s state).
  • The benefit plans is either a medical (MED) or gap (GAP) plan, and the dependent’s age is less than 30. | Condition Set #2 | - The dependent is set up with the Relationship Type Dependent in the Employee Dependents form. - The benefit plan is a Florida-contracted national plan, indicated by the National Plan in the Carrier Code Maintenance panel in Group Benefit Plans. - The benefit plan is either a medical (MED) or gap (GAP) plan, and the dependent’s age is less than 30. |
  • The dependent is set up with the Relationship Type Dependent in the Employee Dependents form.
  • The benefit plan is a Florida-contracted national plan, indicated by the National Plan in the Carrier Code Maintenance panel in Group Benefit Plans.
  • The benefit plan is either a medical (MED) or gap (GAP) plan, and the dependent’s age is less than 30. | Condition Set #3 | - The dependent is set up with the Relationship Type Dependent in the Employee Dependents form, and the dependent is disabled. |
  • The dependent is set up with the Relationship Type Dependent in the Employee Dependents form, and the dependent is disabled. ## Evaluating Documentation Ruleset Eligibility

After the system determines dependent eligibility, it evaluates the employee’s dependents to see whether they satisfy the conditions for the required documentation rulesets. If a dependent satisfies the conditions, the system triggers the required document for the employee to complete.

Restarting the Notification Control Panel

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Restart_NCP.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Restart_NCP.htm

Restarting the Notification Control Panel

If you are re-sending a time-based notification, the trigger condition must match today’s date for the system to send the notification. For example, to re-send the open enrollment (OE) welcome notification to employees, the start date of the OE enrollment period on the Workflow Grid must be today’s date.

Before you restart the notification control panel:

  • Verify that the grid date is the current date.

To restart the Notification Control Panel:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From Operations|Action, select Notification Control Panel. The Notification Control Panel opens.

  3. Click BE Stop. The message SHUTDOWN REQUESTED displays.

  4. Click Status. The status displays NOT RUNNING.

  5. To restart notifications, select BE Start.

  6. Click Save. The current date populates the OE Notify Date.

  7. Click Status. The status displays RUNNING.

Retirement Auto Enrollment Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Retirement%20Auto%20Enrollment%20Report.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Retirement Auto Enrollment Report.htm

Retirement Auto Enrollment Report

Use this form to generate a report to show the list of employees auto enrolled for a retirement plan based on their following auto enrollment statuses:

  • Active – This report shows retirement enrollments for active employees who were automatically enrolled in the system. Their status on the Retirement Plan Enrollment form is Active Enrolled.

  • Eligible – This report shows new hire employees who were automatically enrolled into a retirement plan upon hire, but their status on the Retirement Plan Enrollment form is still Eligible Unenrolled. This is the status of all new hire employees retirement enrollment until they are calculated in a payroll. The payroll calculation will automatically apply the Benefit Plan Setup Auto Enrollment Percent, and change the status to Active Enrolled. These employees will appear on this report until the status is changed to Active Enrolled.

Note: Note: If the Auto Enrollment Percent on the Benefit Plan Setup form is not set up, the employee does not change to Active Enrolled upon payroll calculation.

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

To access the form, under Work Centers, under Onboarding & Benefits, click Benefit Imports & Reporting. Under Benefit Reporting click Retirement Auto Enrollment Report.

To generate the Retirement Auto Enrollment Report:

  1. Complete the Report Type using the following:
Field Name Description
Auto Enrolled Status Select the auto enrollment status of the employee for whom you want to run this report from the following options: - Active - this is the default option - Eligible
  • Active - this is the default option
  • Eligible
  1. Complete the Report Period:
Field Name Description
Updated on or After Enter the date from which you want to report on.
Updated on or Before Enter the date up to which you want to report on.

  1. Click Run to generate the report. The report shows up on screen. Click XLS to download the report.

  2. Click Clear to clear the form at any time.

This report shows the following fields:

  • Client ID

  • Client Name

  • Employee ID

  • Employee Name

  • Start Date

Setting Up Waive Options

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Setting_Up_Waive_Options%20_on_Benefit_Plans.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Setting_Up_Waive_Options _on_Benefit_Plans.htm

Setting Up Waive Options

Organizations can establish whether employees have the option to waive coverage for benefit plans during enrollment using settings at both the system and client level in PrismHR. (PrismHR Benefits Enrollment only.)

The system runs the following checks to determine whether to display the employee option to waive coverage during enrollments.

  • When you set up benefit plan information for the offer type for the benefit plan in the Comparison Fields tab in the Benefits Enrollment Plan Details form, you can select Allow Waive in Benefit Enrollment to establish the waive setting at the system level. This setting overrides the default waive logic of the system and allows the waive option to always display for the offer type.

When the option is … — Then …

Selected — The option to waive coverage displays in all benefit plans assigned the offer type. This setting overrides all other settings.

Not Selected — The option to waive coverage is not set up at the system level for the offer type. The system checks the employee’s contribution to determine employee waive options.

  • The system determines the employee’s contribution for the benefit plan, as defined in Group Benefit Plans. Typically, benefit plans that are 100% paid for by the employer do not include the option to waive.

When the employee’s contribution is … — Then …

Greater than $0 — The employee has the option to waive coverage in the benefit plan.

$0 — The option to waive coverage is not set up for the benefit plan. The system checks the Auto Enroll setting on the benefit plan to determine employee waive options.

  • The system checks the Auto Enroll setting for the benefit plan in Group Benefit Plans.

When the option is set to … — Then …

N for No — The employee has the option to waive coverage in the benefit plan.

Y for Yes — The employee auto enrolls in the benefit plan and does not have the option to waive coverage.

Company Decision — The system checks the Auto-Enrollment setting on the benefit plan at the client level to determine employee waive options.

  • The system checks whether Auto Enrollment is selected for the benefit plan in Benefit Plan Setup. This sets up auto-enrollment in the benefit plan at the client level.

When the option is … — Then …

Selected — The employee auto enrolls in the benefit plan and does not have the option to waive coverage.

Not selected — The employee has the option to waive coverage in the benefit plan.

The employee waive option displays in the form for the benefit plan offer type during enrollment. If settings on even one benefit plan included in an offer type establish that employees can waive coverage, the option to waive displays in the form for the offer type and employees can waive coverage.

Setting Enrollment Options

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/SP_Enrollment_Options.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/SP_Enrollment_Options.htm

Setting Enrollment Options

Enter medical waive reasons and employee cost calculations and determine whether to display employer costs for benefit plans during enrollments with the Enrollment Options form.

Click Close to close this form at any time. All unsaved changes will be lost.

To enter enrollment options:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select System Parameters.

  3. Click the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] and select Enrollment Options. The Enrollment Options form opens.

Use this option — To define this

Medical Waive Reasons — Reasons for waiving medical insurance (such as other group medical coverage or covered by Medicare) that display in a list when the employee waives healthcare benefits for a spouse.

Medical Waive Reasons (Spanish) — Reasons for waiving medical insurance in Spanish.

Valid Waiver Reason — Indicates whether the reason is valid (Yes or No (default). When activating pending enrollments, all waiver reasons (valid or invalid) are included in the total waived employee count.

Life Events - Documents Required — The documents to use for life event enrollments. Documents entered in this panel are shown in the Specify Document(s) list in Life Event Maintenance where they can be selected to include in life event workflow codes.

Life Events - Documents Required (Spanish) — The documents to use for life event enrollments in Spanish.

Include Employer Costs in Employee Self-Service — Enables viewing of employer benefit plan contributions by employees during enrollment periods. You can override this setting at the client level in the Client DetailsBenefits tab.

Employee Calculation Based on — Basis for calculating employee costs (Per Month, Per Period). This setting determines how the system calculates employee costs to display for the benefit plan. You can override this setting at the client level in the Client Details>Benefits tab.

Require Approval for Proxy Enrollments — Enables the requirement for administrative approval on proxy enrollments in Benefits Enrollment (PrismHR Benefits Enrollment only.)

Auto post BE/OE enrollments when overlap with OE — Allows the system to automatically create open enrollment records for employees who already have a benefit enrollment record that overlaps with open enrollment.

Allow Actions on Dashboard for Service Provider Users Only — Restrict access to actions on the dashboard to only allow service providers to access the Actions menu.

Allow Edit Dependents in Enrollment Workflow — Allows employees to make changes to their dependents during the Onboarding process and enrollment workflow.

Archive unsubmitted enrollment workflows X days after worfklow end date — Allows clients to automatically archive enrollment workflows after the designated time period. When this option is enabled, records from employees in all employment statuses are archived automatically.

  1. Click Save to save the record.

Assigning Workflows at the System Level

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/System_Workflow_Grid.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/System_Workflow_Grid.htm

Assigning Workflows at the System Level

To assign workflows to use for enrollments at the system level, use the System Workflow Grid form. You can also assign workflows at the client level. Settings defined at the client level override global settings. For more information, see Assigning Workflows at the Client Level.

The Min # of Overlap days designates the limited amount of time an employee has to complete the open enrollment workflow when the workflow start date plus the Min # of Overlap days extends beyond the open enrollment window. This option is used only in an enrollment workflow overlap scenario.

  • If the start date is the date the first workflow is submitted, 4/25/19, the system checks the Min # of Overlap days on the grid and adds that number of days to the Start Date Window. It then calculates a date of 4/27/19. The system then checks the End Date Window on the grid.

  • If the calculated date is greater than the end date on the grid, the system uses the calculated date.

  • If the end date is in the past, 4/23/19, the system uses the calculated date of 4/27/19 for the workflow end date.

  • If the dates are equal, 4/27/19, the system uses the calculated date of 4/27/19 for the workflow end date.

  • If the end date is in the future, 5/3/19, the workflow end date is 5/3/19.

  • If the Min # of Overlap days is left blank, the system uses the current logic of same length as window dates - the length between start date window and end date window.

  • Use the Filter Records panel to filter system-level workflows by entering the OE Start Date and OE End Date, then clicking Search.

  • Use First, Prev, Next, and Last to navigate through the workflow history.

  • View the total number of records, shown in the lower right-hand corner.

Creating a Workflow Assignment

To create a new workflow assignment to use during enrollments:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select System Workflow Grid. The System Workflow Grid form opens.

  3. Click Add Row. The Add a New Record panel opens.

Use this option — To define this

Effective Date — The date that the benefits that are offered in the workflow become effective for an employee. For example if the enrollment elections start coverage on 06/01/2018,enter that date. Required.

BE Workflow — The BE Workflow to use for benefit enrollments. The list displays all active workflows in the Workflow Library.System Onboarding Workflow Grid

BE Open Window Length — The number of days from the Effective Date during which an employee can enroll in benefits.

Min # of Overlap days — The Min # of Overlap days designates the limited amount of time an employee has to complete the open enrollment workflow when the workflow start date plus the Min # of Overlap days extends beyond the open enrollment window. This option is used only in an enrollment workflow overlap scenario.

AE Workflow — The Anytime Enrollment workflow.

OE Workflow — The OE Workflow to use for open enrollments. The list displays all active workflows in the Workflow Library.

OE Start Date and OE End Date — The start and end dates to use for open enrollments. The OE Start Date is the first day that employees can review and make elections during the Open Enrollment period. The OE End Date is the last day that the employees can submit elections. The OE Start Date and OE End Date must fall before the workflow Effective Date.

WF End Date — The last day that benefits are active and cover the employee. If the Effective Date for the benefits is 06/01/2018, the WF End Date is 05/31/2019, the End Effective Date. This option is required and cannot be earlier than the OE End Date.

Audit — Click to display audit logs. The system displays a message if no logs are available.

  1. Click Save. The record displays in the records table.

  2. If needed, assign additional workflows.

  3. Click Save.

Copying a Workflow Assignment

You use an existing workflow assignment as the basis of a new one by copying it and assigning a new effective date. For example, you can copy the information from a previous year to use for the current year.

To copy a workflow assignment:

  1. In the System Workflow grid, click Copy New. The Copy an Existing Record panel opens.

  2. Enter the Effective Date for the record to copy.

  3. Enter the New Effective Date for the record to create.

  4. Click Save. The record maintains the settings from the original record and displays the new effective date in the Records panel.

Updating the Employee Name and Address Case

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Update-Employee-Name-Address-Case.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Update-Employee-Name-Address-Case.htm

Updating the Employee Name and Address Case

This process converts employee names and/or addresses to either all upper case or proper case (the first letter of each word is in upper case). The data changed depends on the New Hire Proper Case Conversion setting in System Parameters. You can change the case for Employee Name, Employee Address, or both fields.

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

To update the case of the employee name and address:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Action, select Update Employee / Name Address Case.

  3. Click Update Case. The following occurs:

  • If set to Employee Name, the system converts all Employee Names to uppercase.

  • If set to Employee Address, the system converts all Employee Addresses to uppercase.

  • If set to Both Name and Address, the system converts all Employee Names and Addresses to uppercase.

  1. The system displays a job progress window and processes the required employees. It lists a running count of the number of employees processed. For example, Processing 1/500 employees.

  2. Click Close when complete.

Viewing Web Enrollment History

To view an employee’s enrollment activity during enrollments, use the Web Enrollments History form.

The information you can view includes:

  • The benefit plans the employee selected.

  • Whether the employee waived benefits individually or for a spouse.

Changing Start and End Dates

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Benefits%20Enrollment/Topics/Web_Enrollments_Date_Update.htm Path: OBE/Benefits Enrollment/Topics/Web_Enrollments_Date_Update.htm

Changing Start and End Dates

To modify the start and end dates on active enrollment workflows, use the Web Enrollments Date Update form. You can limit the update to one client or apply it to all clients. You can also limit the update to workflows with a specific start and end date, or select a specific workflow to update.

To change enrollment workflow start and end dates:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. Select System|Change > Web Enrollments Date Update. The Web Enrollments Date Update form opens.

For this option — Define this information

Client ID — The Client ID. If blank, the update applies to all clients.

Workflow — The Workflow to modify. The list includes all active workflows. If blank, the system applies the update to all active workflows for the client or, if the Client ID is blank, to all clients.

Start Date and End Date — The Start Date and End Date to further filter workflows if needed. The system limits the update to only the active workflows with the specified Start Date and End Date.

Replace matches with — The Start Date and End Date to use in place of the original values. If the updated workflow is for a benefits enrollment, employees whose effective dates are later than the replacement end date are not affected by the update. If the updated workflow is for an open enrollment, employees whose effective dates are earlier than the replacement end date are not affected by the update.

  1. Click Update. The system displays an alert notifying you of the number of employee records that will be affected by the change and prompts you to confirm

  2. Click Yes to confirm the change.

Configuring Electronic Pay Stubs

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Configuring_Electronic_Pay_Stubs.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Configuring_Electronic_Pay_Stubs.htm

Configuring Electronic Pay Stubs

Electronic pay stubs enable employees to receive their pay stubs electronically through Employee Portal (EP) instead of in a paper format. For employees setting up their direct deposit in Onboarding, the pay stub format options are dependent on PrismHR configuration.

The state rules regarding electronic pay stubs are not pre‑configured and must be set up in PrismHR on the State Rules form.

To open State Rules:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change select States Rules. The State Rules form opens.

  3. In the State Rules panel, see Electronic Pay Stubs Allowed.

Option — Description

Allowed, Employee may not un‑enroll — The state allows electronic pay stubs. You can configure direct deposit to disable employees from un‑enrolling.

Allowed, Employee may un‑enroll — The state allows electronic pay stubs. You can configure direct deposit to enable employees to un‑enroll.

Not allowed by the State — The state does not allow electronic pay stubs.

Allowed, Employee must Opt‑In — The state allows electronic pay stubs, but employees must select electronic pay stub when setting up their direct deposit. Employee defaults to paper pay stubs.

State rules control the pay stub format options shown to employees during onboarding and their default values. You can override this configuration at the client level found on the Client Details form Other tab.

To open Client Details:

  1. Select the Client menu.

  2. From Client|Change select Client Details. The Client Details form opens.

  3. Select the Other tab. The Client Other form opens.

  4. In the Other Options panel, see Electronic Pay Stubs.

Option — Description

Not Used — Electronic pay stubs are not used. All pay stubs are paper.

Employee may not un‑enroll — Employees are enrolled in electronic pay stubs by default and cannot un‑enroll.

Employee may un‑enroll — Employees are enrolled in electronic pay stubs by default, but can un‑enroll when setting up their direct deposit.

The pay stub format options shown to employees during onboarding and their default values depend on the configuration between the State Rules Electronic Pay Stubs Allowed option and the Client Other Electronic Pay Stubs option. These values also affect the Un‑Enroll in Electronic Pay Stub option on the Employee Details form Other tab. The following table details the relationship between the various options.

State Rules Client Details - Other tab Onboarding - Direct Deposit Form Onboarding - Direct Deposit Form Employee Details - Other tab
Electronic Pay Stubs Allowed Electronic Pay Stubs Choose your Pay Stub format dialog box Default Pay Stub Format Un‑Enroll in Electronic Pay Stub
Allowed, Employee may not un‑enroll Employee may not un‑enroll Hide N/A Disabled
Allowed, Employee may not un‑enroll Employee may un‑enroll Show Electronic Disabled
Allowed, Employee may un‑enroll N/A Show Electronic Disabled
Not allowed by the State N/A Hide N/A Enabled
Allowed, Employee must Opt‑In N/A Show Paper Enabled
N/A Not Used Hide N/A Enabled
N/A N/A Hide N/A Enabled
