Cross Hiring Employees

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Cross_Hiring_EEs.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Cross_Hiring_EEs.htm

Cross Hiring Employees

A cross hire employee works for multiple clients under the same service provider. If the system identifies a new hire employee as a cross hire and their employee information does not match the existing employee information, the system generates a conflict. Cross hire conflicts prevent the system from launching new hire onboarding workflows. The following outlines how the system identifies cross hire employees, checks for cross hire conflicts, and enables you to review or resolve those conflicts:

  1. When entering a new hire, if the Social Security number (SSN) matches the SSN of an existing employee, the system identifies the employee as a cross hire.

  2. After identifying the employee as a cross hire, the system checks for conflicts to determine if the new hire is the same person as the existing employee. However, system does not check for cross hire conflicts when a service provider enters a new hire on the New Hire form. For more information on these exceptions, see Service Providers and Worksite Managers.

  3. If cross hire conflicts exist, they display on the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form.

The following details how the system checks for cross hire conflicts.

Checking for Cross Hire Conflicts

To identify cross hire conflicts, the system verifies that the new hire is the same person as the existing employee with three key fields - First Name, Last Name, and Date of Birth. If any of these values do not match, the onboarding workflow does not launch, the system creates a conflict, and an email sends to the service provider assigned to the client Human Resources and Payroll accounts (Client Details > Accounts tab). A message also displays in My Messages. Service providers must resolve the conflict on the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form.

The system identifies cross hire conflicts when launching their onboarding workflow in the following scenarios:

  • Entering a new hire on the New Hire form. See Manually Entering a Cross Hire New Hire.

  • Importing a new hire with the Expanded Employee Import. See Importing Cross Hires with the Expanded Employee Import.

  • Importing a new hire with the API Import. See Importing Cross Hire Employees with the API Import.

  • Launching a new workflow for a new hire with the Launch New Workflow action on the Workflow Maintenance form. Launching a New Workflow for a Cross Hire New Hire.

In some cases, when onboarding a new hire to more than one client at the same time, the system may not identify a cross hire until a workflow launches and the new hire is completing their onboarding forms. In this scenario, the system performs a cross hire check on the Form I-9 Section 1 and the Employee Information form. See Employee Submits Forms in Onboarding Workflow.

Manually Entering a Cross Hire New Hire

When you enter a new hire on the New Hire form, the system identifies whether the new hire is also a cross hire. The system supports entering cross hires for the following user types:

  • Service providers

  • Worksite managers

  • Worksite trusted advisors

Service Providers

For service providers, the following table details the results of saving a new hire employee that is also a cross hire. The results differ when the employee is set up for Onboarding.

Onboarding? Result
Yes - Employee fields populate. - The system does not check for a cross hire conflict. - Onboarding workflow launches.
  • Employee fields populate.
  • The system does not check for a cross hire conflict.
  • Onboarding workflow launches. | No | - Employee fields populate. - The system does not check for a cross hire conflict. |
  • Employee fields populate.
  • The system does not check for a cross hire conflict. When a service provider enters a cross hire, the system does not check for a cross hire conflict because the service provider can view employee data on the New Hire form. They can see if there is a conflict with the data entered and correct it if needed.

Worksite Managers

For worksite managers, the following tables detail the results of saving a new hire employee that is also a cross hire.

Onboarding? Result
Yes - Employee fields do not populate. - The system checks for a cross hire conflict. - If a conflict does not exist, the onboarding workflow launches. - If a conflict exists, the conflict displays in the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form and the onboarding workflow does not launch.
  • Employee fields do not populate.
  • The system checks for a cross hire conflict.
  • If a conflict does not exist, the onboarding workflow launches.
  • If a conflict exists, the conflict displays in the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form and the onboarding workflow does not launch. | No | The system does not support cross hires. | When a worksite manager enters a cross hire, the system checks for a cross hire conflict because the worksite manager cannot view employee data on the New Hire form. They cannot see if there is a conflict with the data entered and correct it if needed.

Worksite Trusted Advisors

For worksite trusted advisors, the following table details the results of saving a new hire employee that is also a cross hire. The results differ when the employee is set up for Onboarding.

Onboarding? Result
Yes - Employee fields do not populate. - The system checks for a cross hire conflict. - If a conflict does not exist, the onboarding workflow launches. - If a conflict exists, the conflict displays in the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form, and the onboarding workflow does not launch.
  • Employee fields do not populate.
  • The system checks for a cross hire conflict.
  • If a conflict does not exist, the onboarding workflow launches.
  • If a conflict exists, the conflict displays in the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form, and the onboarding workflow does not launch. | No | The system does not support cross hires. | When a worksite trusted advisor enters a cross hire, the system checks for a cross hire conflict because the worksite trusted advisor cannot view employee data on the New Hire form. They cannot see if there is a conflict with the data entered and correct it if needed.

Importing Cross Hires with the Expanded Employee Import

The Expanded Employee Import in HRPyramid System Administration > Process > Conversion Processing > Expanded Employee Import) supports importing and converting cross hire employees set up for Onboarding into the backend system of PrismHR. First, you must import the employees (Expanded Employee Import > Import Employees).

Note: Note: To import an employee as a cross hire, you must include the employee ID in column GQ (field 199). For more information, see Expanded Employee Import File Specifications on the Customer Resource Center.

The import process loads an external file and creates IMPORT.EMPLOYEE records you can import into the Employee Master file. During the import, the system checks for cross hire conflicts. If a conflict exists, a warning displays in the import report. You can fix it by correcting the data in the external file. After importing, you must convert the employees (Expanded Employee Import > Convert Employees).

The conversion process moves valid employees from the IMPORT.EMPLOYEES file into the Employee Master file. If a conflict exists, the conflict displays on the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form, and their onboarding workflow does not launch. If a conflict does not exist, the system launches their onboarding workflow.

Importing Cross Hire Employees with the API Import

The API Employee Import supports importing cross hire employees set up for Onboarding into the backend system of PrismHR. First, you must import the employees.

Note: Note: To import an employee as a cross hire, you must include the employee ID.

The import process checks the data and creates a Data Import Errors Report to identify any data issues. If a cross hire conflict exists, a warning displays in the import report. You can fix the conflict by correcting the data. After importing, you must commit the employees.

The commit process updates the data in PrismHR. If a conflict exists, the conflict displays on the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form, and their onboarding workflow does not launch. If a conflict does not exist, the system launches their onboarding workflows.

Launching a New Workflow for a Cross Hire New Hire

When you launch a new workflow on the Workflow Maintenance form, the system identifies whether the new hire is also a cross hire. If a cross hire conflict does not exist, the onboarding workflow launches. If there is a cross hire conflict, it displays on the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form, and the onboarding workflow does not launch.

Employee Submits Forms in Onboarding Workflow

When an employee submits forms in their onboarding workflow, the system checks for cross hire conflicts at three points. When the employee:

  • Clicks Back or Next on the Form I‑9 Section 1, Employee Information page.

  • Clicks Back or Next on the Employee Information form, Personal Information page.

  • Clicks Save and Continue on the Employee Information form, Emergency Contacts page.

If a conflict exists, the system informs the employee they cannot proceed because either the First Name, Last Name, or Date of Birth does not match what is already in the system. The employee can resolve the conflict by entering the correct values. If the employee resolves the conflict, the system removes it from the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form and sends an email indicating that the conflict is resolved to the service provider. A message also displays in My Messages. If the employee cannot resolve the conflict, the service provider can correct it on the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form.

Launching a New Hire

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Launching_New_Hire.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Launching_New_Hire.htm

Launching a New Hire

To enter new hire employee information into PrismHR, use the New Hire form (located in the HR menu under HR|Action). After entering the employee information, the system displays the prehire ID when you click Save. The new hire can then register and log in to Onboarding to complete the onboarding process.

If the new hire is a cross hire employee and the First Name, Last Name, Birth Date, and Social Security Number values do not match values on the employee record from the original client, the system creates a conflict. The system also creates a prehire record for the new hire without an Onboarding workflow. To review and manage cross hire conflicts, see Managing Cross Hire Conflicts.

For more information about hiring a new employee, see the Human Resources User Guide.

Setting Up Notifications (Onboarding)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Setting_up_Chapter_Notifications.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Setting_up_Chapter_Notifications.htm

Setting Up Notifications (Onboarding)

The Notification Templates and Notification Recipients forms enable you to set up notifications you want to send and to specify recipients for notifications.

For notifications to work, you must first set up Email Services. For more information, see the System Administration Guide.

  • You can set up content for notifications in the Notification Templates form. For more information, see Setting Up Notification Templates (Onboarding).

  • You can designate recipients for notifications in the Notification Recipients form. For more information, see Setting Up Notification Recipients (Onboarding).

  • Notifications are available in the Workflow Library where you can add them to workflows. For more information, see Creating a Workflow for Onboarding.

Managing Notifications

To specify how often the system should send the notifications, to resend time-based notifications if needed, and to stop or start the notifications engine, use the Notification Control Panel. For more information, see Managing the Notification Control Panel (Onboarding).

Handling Bounced Notifications

There are two possible circumstances that can result in notifications not reaching the recipient:

  • The email address is not entered correctly (for example, it is entered as @​example.com​ instead of ​jsmith@example.com​). PrismHR logs this error but it is not accessible to the user. Users should contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team.

  • The email address is entered correctly, but is otherwise not deliverable (for example, the email address is no longer active). In this case, PrismHR simply sends the email. There is no error log, and in most cases, there is no way to know that it was not received. Many email servers are configured not to send notifications of undeliverable emails.

Tracking Notifications

You can track notifications by setting up the Notification Recipients form to send copies of all notifications to an administrator in your organization. This also enables you to forward notifications.

========================================================================

Setting Up Forms and Workflows (Onboarding)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Setting_Up_Forms_Workflows.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Setting_Up_Forms_Workflows.htm

Setting Up Forms and Workflows (Onboarding)

A workflow is essentially a wizard—a script that presents employees with a series of forms to guide them through well-defined and highly customizable steps during onboarding and benefit enrollments.

If you plan to include any media items in a workflow, such as documents or images, in forms or workflows, upload them to Document Management before you create the forms in the Form Library.

In the Form Library, you can create custom versions of the forms or use the prebuilt versions provided with their default language and settings. Prebuilt forms display in bold.

After you create the forms, you can use them as components to create workflows in the Workflow Library.

Managing Documents, Forms, and Workflows

Use the following libraries to manage documents, forms, and workflows for onboarding.

Library — Description

Document Management — Upload and maintain documents to use when creating forms (graphic files for logos, PDF files containing onboarding information, and so on).

Form Library (Onboarding) — Configure forms to include in a workflow. PrismHR provides prebuilt forms that you can use as templates to create new forms. You can also build your own custom forms.

Workflow Library (Onboarding) — Create workflows using forms from the Forms Library as components.

Working with Data Issues

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Working_with_Data_Issues.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Working_with_Data_Issues.htm

Working with Data Issues

During the onboarding process, data‑related problems automatically generate emails and messages sent to service providers describing the issues, and what must be done to correct them.

You can identify the set of recipients in PrismHR.

To identify the set of recipients:

  1. Click Client.

  2. From Client|Change select Client Details. The Client Details form opens.

  3. Click the Account tab.

  4. In the Account Assignments panel, the set of recipients includes those assigned as the Payroll and Human Resources employee representative.

If… — The system sends the email and message to…

The representative for each role is different — Both representatives receive an email and message.

The same representative is assigned to both roles — Only one email and message are sent to that individual.

A Payroll and Human Resources representative does not exist in Account Assignments — The email and message are delivered to the service provider assigned as the Human Resources Representative and Payroll Manager Account Management Role on the Users form.

Emails are sent directly to their email address and messages appear in My Messages. These scenarios include:

Issue — Solution

On the Form I-9 Section 1 or the Employee Information form, if an employee enters a key field (First Name, Last Name, DOB, or SSN) that does not match the value stored in PrismHR, an error message displays and the employee is unable to save changes. — The service provider must review the message to identify the mismatched values. If the employee entered the incorrect value, the service provider must inform the employee to enter the correct value. If the incorrect value is in PrismHR, the value must be corrected in Workflow Maintenance using the Edit New Hire action.

On the Form I‑9 Section 1, if an employee attempts to submit the form with no I‑9 Section 2 approver in the system, an error message displays and the employee is unable to submit the form. Employees must contact their employer. — The service provider must assign an I‑9 Section 2 approver on the Approval Policies form in PrismHR. For more information on approvals, see the Human Resources User Guide. Once an I-9 Section 2 approver exists, the employee can submit the Form I‑9 Section 1 and continue with the onboarding process.

On the Employee Information form, if an employee enters their resident address manually, a GeoCode is not stored. Without a GeoCode, employees cannot receive pay. This must be corrected for a payroll to be run for this employee. — The service provider must go to the Employee Details Address tab to enter the employeeGEO Code under Resident Address.

Working with the Onboarding Dashboard

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Working_with_OB_Dashboard.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Working_with_OB_Dashboard.htm

Working with the Onboarding Dashboard

The PrismHR Dashboard provides quick access to company information and relevant features. The information shown in the Chart and List sections of the Perspective panel reflects the client selected.

To view onboarding tasks within Onboarding, in the Chart section of the My Perspective panel, select Onboarding Tasks by Status from the list.

[Image: screenshot]

Using the Chart and Filters

The Chart displays onboarding tasks and their statuses (Not Due, Due Today, Past Due, and Completed) for a date range. The Chart includes the following filters:

  • The Show list enables you to select the date range you want to view (Active, Last 7 Days, Last 30 Days). The Chart and List display tasks in the date range.

  • The Tasks list enables you to select which type of task you want to view in the Dashboard (I-9 Section 1, Employee Information, I-9 Section 2, All Other Forms, or Approve Paycard). The Chart and List display the task selected.

Using the List

The List section displays information about new hire tasks. The number of tasks that display is determined by the filters selected in the Chart section of the My Perspective panel.

Working with Pay Card Direct Deposit Accounts

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Working_with_Pay_Card_Dir_Dep_Accts.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Front_Matter/Working_with_Pay_Card_Dir_Dep_Accts.htm

Working with Pay Card Direct Deposit Accounts

Onboarding supports the use of pay cards for direct deposit. Pay cards offer employees without a bank account an alternative to paper paychecks that can be lost or stolen.

Configuration

To use pay card direct deposit accounts, you must configure system‑level and client‑level settings in PrismHR.

Configuring Bank Routing for Pay Cards

To configure the financial institution used for pay cards:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change select Bank Routing. The Bank Routing form opens.

  3. Enter the Routing Code for the financial institution (also known as the transit number). The remaining fields populate automatically.

  4. To indicate that your organization uses the account for pay cards, select PayCard (Automove).

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

Adding Pay Card Accounts in System Parameters

To define the default pay card for all clients at the global level, use the System Parameters form.

To add pay card accounts in System Parameters:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change select System Parameters. The System Parameters form opens.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. In Pay Card Account, enter a valid pay card account number, or select the link to choose from a list. Values in the list are defined on the Bank Routing form (see Bank Routing for Pay Cards).

  2. Click Save.

Adding Pay Card Accounts in Client Details

To establish the pay card account used by the client, use the Client Details form Account tab.

To add pay card accounts in Client Details:

  1. From Client|Change select Client Details. The Client Details form opens.

  2. Select the Account tab.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. In Pay Card Account, enter a valid pay card account number, or select the link to choose from a list. Values in the list are defined on the Bank Routing form (see Bank Routing for Pay Cards).

  2. Select Save.

Approval Policies

When an employee selects Pay Card as a direct deposit account in Onboarding, the account number is assigned to the employee via a pay card account approval in PrismHR.

Pay card approval policies can be set up at the system level using the Global Approval Policies form or the client level using the Approval Policies form.

For more information about setting up approval policies at the system level, see the System Administration Guide or Approvals Guide. For more information about setting up approval policies at the client level, see the Human Resources User Guide or Approvals Guide.

Assigning System-level Pay Card Account Approvers

To define pay card account approvals by service type at the global level, use the Global Approval Policies form.

To assign system-level pay card account approvers:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change select Global Approval Policies. The Global Approval Policies form opens.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. In Service Type, select Professional Employer.

  2. In Approval Type, select Pay Card Account.

  3. In HR Role or A/M Role, select a User ID and designate the user as a Pay Card Account Approver. For more information about user role types, see the System Administration User Guide.

  4. Click Save.

Assigning Client-level Pay Card Account Approvers

To define pay card account approvals by service type at the client level, use the Approval Policies form.

To assign client-level pay card account approvers:

  1. From HR|Change select Approval Policies. The Approval Policies form opens.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. In Approval Type, select Pay Card Account.

  2. In HR Role or A/M Role, select a User ID, and permit them as a Pay Card Account Approver. For more information about user role types, see User Roles in the System Administration User Guide.

  3. Click Save.

Pending Pay Card Account Approvals

After users create their direct deposit pay card account, the system creates an approval and assigns it to designated approvers in PrismHR. The approval is used to assign the pay card account number. Use the Approvals Pending form to select a pending pay card account approval.

To open the form, from Client|Action select Approvals Pending.

[Image: screenshot]

Approvals Pending (Available to You)

Approvals in the Approvals Pending (Available to You) panel are approvals available to multiple designated approvers.

  1. Select an approval from the Approval Type column. The approval dialog box displays.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. To move the approval to the Approvals Pending (Assigned to You) panel, click Grab.

Approvals Pending (Assigned to You)

To select a pay card account approval, use the Approvals Pending (Assigned to You) panel. The approval is used to assign the pay card account number.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. In Pay Card Account, enter the employee pay card account number. This is the account number on the pay card you are providing to the employee.

  2. To save the pay card account number, click Apply.

Note: Note: If an employee removes the pay card account from their direct deposit during the approval process, an alert message displays informing the designated approvers that their approval is no longer necessary.

Some field types are editable. For example, Checkbox is an editable field type since the user can enable or disable the checkbox on the form.

Additional Options: - Source URL:The URL for the website to link from the Field Label. Employees can access the website by clicking the link during enrollments. |
  • Source URL:The URL for the website to link from the Field Label. Employees can access the website by clicking the link during enrollments. | Markdown | Creates text that includes markdown or HTML formatting (for example, a bold heading) to display in the form. You can select Paragraph if you do not need to use markdown or HTML formatting. Additional Options: - Field Default Value: The default value to display in the field. |
  • Field Default Value: The default value to display in the field. | PDF Frame | Creates a window to display the uploaded PDF on the custom form. The PDF Frame field type is available only when you upload a fillable PDF to a custom form. For more information, see PDF Integration. | ========================================================================

Approving Forms I-9

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Approving_Forms_I-9.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Approving_Forms_I-9.htm

Approving Forms I-9

When an onboarding workflow contains the Form I-9, there must be a Form I-9 approval.

For Onboarding, you must set up a user as an I-9 Approver. Employees are unable to submit their Form I-9 Section 1 without an I-9 Section 2 approver. The I-9 Section 2 approval is not created unless an approver exists. For more information, see the System Administration Guide.

Note: Note: If E-Verification is enabled for an employee, it is flagged after Form I-9 Section 2 is completed.

The user set up with this role must approve the I-9 Forms for new hires to complete the onboarding process. For more information about processing approvals, see the Human Resources User Guide.

After the new hire submits the I-9 Section 1, an I-9 Section 2 approval is created, and the approval displays in the approval queue of the Approvals Pending form in PrismHR. The approver can select one of the following options:

  • Deny the Form I-9 Section 1

  • Complete and submit the Form I-9 Section 2

  • Return the approval to the queue for another approver to complete

For more information about pending approvals, see the Human Resources User Guide or Approvals Guide.

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Assigning_Workflows_Client_Level_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Assigning_Workflows_Client_Level_OB.htm

Assigning Workflows at the Client Level (Onboarding)

Use the Client Onboarding Workflow Grid form to assign a workflow to Onboarding at the client level.

To access this form:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Client Onboarding Workflow Grid.

Viewing and Modifying Workflow Assignments

Existing onboarding workflows display in the records table. You can:

  • View workflows for a specific client by entering the Client ID or the Client Name, then clicking Search. You can also select Search without entering client information to view workflows for all clients.

  • Select a different workflow to use in a record from multiple Workflow drop-downs (New Hire, Rehire, Take On).

  • Modify record settings for the Window Length by clicking the link.

  • Copy a record to a new Effective Date by clicking Copy.

Assigning a New Onboarding Workflow

To enter a new onboarding workflow assignment:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Client Onboarding Workflow Grid. The Client Onboarding Workflow Grid form opens.

  3. Click Add. The Add Workflow Effective Date form opens.

  4. Complete the following fields:

Field Action
Client Id Enter the client ID. The Client Name displays. This field is required to save the workflow.
Workflow Effective Date Enter the date when onboarding begins. This field is required to save the workflow.
New Hire Workflow Select the workflow you want to use for a New Hire from the drop-down. The drop-down displays all active Onboarding workflows in the Workflow Library. This field is required to save the workflow. For New Hire workflows, the Client Start Date and the Employer Start Date are the same.
Rehire Workflow Select the workflow you want to use for a Rehire from the drop-down. The drop-down displays all active Onboarding workflows in the Workflow Library. For Rehire workflows, the Last Hire Date is greater than the Original Hire Date.
Take On Workflow Select the workflow you want to use for a Take On from the drop-down. The drop-down displays all active Onboarding workflows in the Workflow Library. For Take-On workflows, the Client Start Date / Original Hire Date is before the Employer Start Date.
Window Length Enter the number of days from the Workflow Effective Date during which an employee can log in and complete their onboarding workflow. The Window Length defines the window for all forms except the Form I-9. The Form I-9 window is as follows: - The Form I-9 Section I is due on the employee hire date. - The Form I-9 Section II is due three (3) days from the employee hire date.
  • The Form I-9 Section I is due on the employee hire date.
  • The Form I-9 Section II is due three (3) days from the employee hire date. | Audit | Click to display audit logs. The system displays a message if no logs are available. | | Send Notification Icon | A letter icon displays next to the line item to send the same-day notification when the following criteria are met: - The line item has an effective date. - An open enrollment workflow is present. - The OBE Start Date is set to today’s date or changed to today’s date. Click the icon to display a confirmation window, where the user can select to send notifications for the open enrollment workflow. |
  1. Click Save. The record displays in the records table.

Conditional Notifications

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Conditional_Notifications.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Conditional_Notifications.htm

Conditional Notifications

Conditional notifications are event-based notifications triggered by content in a form field or a business rule. This feature helps clients with custom forms ask questions of an employee, whose Yes response triggers a notification to previously specified recipients. For example, a custom form might ask if employees have received a COVID-19 vaccine.

In the form library, fields need to be identified where these notifications apply. For instance, a new radio button is added with Yes or No options for response.

Additional attributes are needed to trigger a notification for a Form Field Response. Notification triggers are event-based, such as when a workflow is completed. When an employee submits a workflow, conditional notifications are triggered.

A single notification template is allowed for each field type. The user is unable to choose a different notification template per response. There is one chosen template that is sent based on the responses selected. A user can choose multiple responses for sending the notification.

The conditional notifications workflow operates like this:

  1. Notification logic is updated to account for the Send Notification configuration on the field type of a form.

  2. When a trigger is enabled. employees submit a workflow and the system examines the notifications attached to a workflow to determine whether this form has field types that require notification.

  3. If yes, the system then determines if an employee’s response is selected to send a notification.

  4. If yes, the system sends the notification.

========================================================================

Creating a Workflow (Onboarding)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Creating_a_Workflow_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Creating_a_Workflow_OB.htm

Creating a Workflow (Onboarding)

Use the Workflow Library form to create workflows for onboarding. You can also use this form to add notifications to send out to employees or groups during onboarding.

  • You can create a workflow using prebuilt forms. Enter the workflow ID and Name (these are required), select the workflow type Onboarding, and save the workflow.

  • You can also customize settings, select different process forms, and add additional forms to the workflow. The forms you select display in the navigation menu during onboarding in the same order in which they are arranged.

To create a new workflow:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Workflow Library. The Workflow Library form opens.

  3. Click Create New Workflow. The Create New Workflow form opens.

  4. On the General tab, complete the following:

Field — Action

ID — Enter a unique workflow ID.

Name — Enter a unique workflow name.

Workflow Type — Select Onboarding. The Workflow Type Enrollment is used only to create workflows for Benefits Enrollment.

The Start Date and End Date fields are read only. These dates are set when you submit the workflow.

  1. Click Save. The Process, Forms and Documents, and Notifications tabs are enabled.

  2. Select the Process tab. You can save, change, and retrieve data. Autosave occurs when the drop-down selector is changed.

The following forms are required for all onboarding workflows. If additional languages are configured on the system, these form fields will appear in multiple languages.

  • Initial Visit

  • Return Visit

  • Employee Information

  • Summary

  • Submit

The following forms are optional.

  • Electronic Consent:

  • When creating a new workflow, the form is enabled by default. You can disable it to not include the form in the workflow.

  • When editing a preexisting workflow, the form is disabled by default and must be enabled to include in the workflow.

For information on including the Electronic Consent form at the beginning of a workflow, see Including the Electronic Consent Form.

  • I‑9 Section 1 and 2: Enabled by default, but can disable to not include in workflow.

  1. Use the lists to select forms. To edit a form label, click [Image: screenshot] .

  2. Select the Forms and Documents tab.

Add forms and form sets to the workflow by dragging and dropping them from the Available Items panel to the Selected Items panel. Forms in bold are prebuilt forms. If additional languages are configured on your system, both the form title and category title display additional language fields.

  • To change the name of a form, click [Image: screenshot] . To remove a form from the Selected Items panel, click [Image: screenshot] . You can also view forms included in form sets by selecting [Image: screenshot] .

Note: Note: The forms included in the workflow display in the navigation menu of Onboarding in sequential order.

  1. Select the Notifications tab.

You must create notifications and set up recipients to assign them to workflows. For more information about creating notification templates, seeSetting Up Notification Templates (Onboarding). For more information assigning notification recipients, see Setting Up Notification Recipients (Onboarding).

  1. For each notification to attach to the workflow, click Enabled. When a notification is enabled, the Object field is enabled.

  2. Select the Object to apply to the enabled notification. The options available depend upon the notification’s Type and Condition. For more information about these options, see Working with Objects and Tasks for Onboarding Notifications.

  3. Click Save. The Submit Workflow dialog box opens.

When does the change need to go into effect? — Definition

Immediately — The system sets the workflow status to Active and sets the current date as the Start Date. Since there can only be one active version of the workflow, the system changes the status of any prior active version to Inactive and sets the current date as its End Date.

Future Date — The form prompts you to enter a Start Date. The system sets the workflow status to Inactive and changes it to Active on the specified start date.

Save as Draft — The workflow status is set to Draft. The system does not set a Start Date, version, or revision number for the workflow.

  1. Click Submit Workflow when the workflow is complete.

Managing the Workflow Library (Onboarding)

Creating a Business Rule

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Creating_Business_Rule_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Creating_Business_Rule_OB.htm

Creating a Business Rule

To define the name and type of a business rule, use the Create Business Rule form.

To create a business rule:

  1. Navigate- to the Business Rules Library form.

The Business Rules Library opens.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Create Rule. The Create Business Rule form opens.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Complete the following fields:
Field Action
Business Rule ID Enter a unique ID.
Business Rule Name Enter a title.
Description Enter a brief description.
Workflow Type Select Onboarding.
  1. In the Rule Type field, select:
  • Global: A global rule type can contain only global variables. For example, gender is a global variable since its values are not specific to a client.

The following global variables are available:

  • Client Category Code

  • Employee 1099 Contractor

  • Employee FLSA Exempt

  • Employee Gender

  • Employee Preferred Language

  • Employee Pay Method

  • Employee Resident Address State

  • Employee Employment Type

  • Employee Employer ID

  • Employee Worksite Location State

Note: Note: Global-level values are the same across all business rules.

  • Client: A client rule type can contain both client variables and global variables. For example, position code is a client-level variable since positions of employment differ from client to client—a cleaning service and an insurance company will differ in the positions the company has to offer.

In addition to the global variables, the following client-level variables are available for use:

  • Client Category Code

  • Employee 1099 Contractor

  • Employee Benefit Group

  • Employee Position Code

  • Employee Worksite Location Code

  • Employee Work Department Code

  • Employee Work Division Code

Note: Note: Client-level values vary for business rule as they depend on the clients selected in the business rule.

  1. To save and continue to the Edit Business Rule form, click Next. The Edit Business Rule form opens.

  • You can edit the Business Rule Name. This field is required, and must be unique.

  • You can edit the Rule Type. If you change the business rule type, a confirmation message informs you that the current expression will be overwritten.

  • You can edit the rule Description. This field populates with the value defined on the Create Business Rule form.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. The Rule field is an incomplete expression. Use variables, operators, and values to assemble your business rule expression.

  • In the Choose a variable list, select a variable. This applies only to the GlobalRule Type. If using a ClientRule Type, the variable list is read only.

  • In the Choose Operator list, select an operator.

  • Equal To: The variable or client ID is equal to the value.

  • Not Equal To: The variable or client ID is not equal to the value.

  • To select one or more values, click Choose Value.

You can flag certain global and client level variables as obsolete when used in a business rule expression to prevent further use. Values assigned to these variables do not display in the business rule drop-downs when in use.

  • For more information on Employee Worksite Location, Employee Work Division Code, and Employee Work Department Code, see the Client Administration User Guide.

  • For more information on Employee Position Code and Employee Employment Type, see the Human Resources User Guide.

  • For more information on Employee Benefit Group, see the Benefit Administration User Guide.

  1. When you are finished configuring the rule, click Save.

Parent Topic

See Managing Business Rules (Onboarding)

Creating Custom Forms (Onboarding)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Creating_Custom_Forms_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Creating_Custom_Forms_OB.htm

Creating Custom Forms (Onboarding)

Use the Form Library to create a custom form. When creating a custom form, the fields to include on the form must be added and configured manually.

To create a custom form:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change select Form Library. The Form Library displays.

  3. Click Create New Form. The Create New Custom Form form displays.

  4. Complete the following:

Field — Action

Form ID — Enter a unique ID for the form. Required.

Form Name — Enter a title for the form. Required. PrismHR recommends using spaces, not hyphens. Hyphenated words do not wrap correctly in Onboarding. If additional languages are configured on the system, clicking this field causes a Form Name window to display, where a form name for each language can be entered.

Description — Enter a brief description of the form.

Workflow Type — Select Onboarding. Required.

  1. Click Continue. The Edit Custom Form form displays.

On the General tab, entries are made automatically in the Form Name field and the Description field based on the information entered on the Create New Custom form. These entries can be edited if necessary.

  1. Complete the following:

Field — Action

Form Symbol — Enter a symbol for the form (any two characters A-Z, 0-9). Required.

Business Rules — Click this field to display a list of business rules that can be applied to the form. For more information, see Business Rules Library (Onboarding) . > Note: Note: If a business rule is applied to Form I-9 Section 1, the same business rule must be applied to Form I-9 Section 2.

Form Set — Click this field to display a list of form sets that can be assigned to the form. For more information, see Form Sets (Onboarding).

PDF Integration — Upload PDFs with the capability of mapping them to the custom form. For more information, see PDF Integration.

  1. Click Save. Alternatively, access the Layout tab to modify the layout of the form.

To modify the form layout:

  1. Select the Layout tab.

  2. From Field Type, drag field types to the layout on the right. Field types are customizable and vary depending on the type of form.

Some field types, such as Textfield, Text Area, Checkbox, Select List, Radio Button, and E-signature, can be edited. Others are read-only. Editable field types allow employees to enter unique data.

Note: Note: If a field type is configured for additional languages, the languages for which the field is available displays in the Languages column.

If PDF integration is used, the PDF Fields list displays. From here, the editable fields extracted from the PDF can be selected for inclusion on the custom form. For more information, see PDF Integration.

Note: Note: When PDF files are uploaded in an additional language, the file’s mappable fields are parsed and compared to the field map of the English PDF file. If there is a mismatch of fields and field IDs with the English field map, it is rejected by the system.

  1. To customize field types, click Configure. Field Type parameters vary. For more information about setting up field parameters and settings, see Form Library Fields.
  • To preview the form before saving, click Preview Form. If additional languages are enabled on the system, a form preview includes an additional language, such as Spanish.

When previewing a form in a different language, text that is not translated displays in red. This can help identify missing translation strings.

When previewing a form with conditional form fields, these conditions should correctly be displayed or hidden when the parent field conditions are changed.

  • To return to the Form Library without saving changes, click Return to Forms Library.
  1. Click Save. The Submit Form dialog box opens, prompting you to specify when the form should be made available.

Option — Description

Immediately — The system sets the form status to Active, and sets the current date as the Start Date. Since there can be only one active version of the form, the system changes the status of any prior active version to Inactive and sets the current date as its End Date.

Future Date — You are prompted to enter a Start Date. The system sets the form status to Inactive and changes it to Active on the specified start date.

Save as Draft — The system sets the form status to Draft. The system does not set a Start Date, version, or revision number for the form.

  1. Click Submit Form when the form is complete.

  1. Click the Layout tab.

Immediately — The system sets the form status to Active, and sets the current date as the Start Date. Since there can be only one active version of the form, the system changes the status of any prior active version to Inactive and sets the current date as its End Date.

Future Date — You are prompted to enter a Start Date. The system sets the form status to Inactive and changes it to Active on the specified start date.

Save as Draft — The system sets the form status to Draft. The system does not set a Start Date, version, or revision number for the form.

  1. Click Submit Form when the form is complete.

Deactivating Workflows

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Deactivating_Workflow.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Deactivating_Workflow.htm

Deactivating Workflows

On the Workflow Library form, you can deactivate unused workflows giving them an inactive status. You can only deactivate active workflows.

To deactivate workflows:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Workflow Library. The Workflow Library opens.

  3. Using the checkmarks next to workflows in the list, select one or more workflows to deactivate. The system enables the Deactivate button.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Deactivating a workflow deactivates all versions of the workflow. To deactivate a workflow, click Deactivate.

If the workflow is in use, a dialog box displays indicating why you cannot deactivate the workflow. For example, the workflow is in a draft state, it is in use on the Client Workflow Grid, it is a prebuilt or default workflow, and so on.

If the workflow is in use on one or more of the following grids, you must remove the workflow from the grid before deactivating:

  • System Workflow Grid

  • Client Workflow Grid

  • Systen Onboarding Workflow Grid

  • Client Onboarding Workflow Grid

After deactivating a workflow, it does not show in the system as an available option for configuration.

  1. Click Yes, deactivate.

  2. Click Save.

Deleting a Workflow

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Deleting_Workflow_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Deleting_Workflow_OB.htm

Deleting a Workflow

The following details how to launch a workflow with the Launch New Workflow action.

To delete a workflow:

  1. In the Filters panel, you can filter the search results by client or by new hire information. To filter by client, enter a client ID number in the Client ID field or click the Client ID link to select a client ID number from a list. To filter by new hire information, enter a new hire’s last name, first name, Social Security Number, or employee ID in the New Hire field.

  2. Click Search. The Search Results panel displays the following information:

Field — Description

New Hire — The employee’s name.

SSN — The employee’s Social Security Number. The value shown may be masked or hidden depending on settings at the system or client level.

Employee ID — The employee’s ID number.

Employer Start Date — The date the employee started with the employer.

Client Start Date — The date the employee started with the client.

Workflow — The workflow name and version number.

Launch Date — The date the prehire record was created.

Action — Delete: Enables you to delete an onboarding workflow.

  1. In the Action column, click Delete.

If the employee entered data on one or more of the forms in the workflow, a confirmation dialog box warns that the system deletes and cannot recover employee data.

  1. To continue deleting the workflow, click Yes.

Document Set - Workflow Library

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Document%20Set%20Workflow%20Library.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Document Set Workflow Library.htm

Document Set - Workflow Library

Instead of creating a form for each document set (and needing to build a new field type), you can add the document set directly to the workflow.

To add a document set to a workflow:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Workflow Library. The Workflow Library form opens.

  3. Create a new workflow or edit an existing workflow.See Creating a Workflow (Onboarding)

  4. Select the Forms and Document tab.

  5. (Optional) Select a filter from the Filter Type drop-down to filter the available forms and sets by type in the Available Items section.

  6. (Optional) Enter a keyword in the Search field to filter the available forms and sets by keyword in the Available Items section.

  7. Review the items in the Available Items section. You can click the column header to sort the items by name or by type.

  8. Drag and drop one or more item from the Available Items section to the Selected Items section.

  9. (Optional) Edit the field label of the item.

a. Click the edit button for an item in the Selected items section.
b. Update the field label in the English field or the Spanish field.
— —
c. Click Save to complete the label change.
— —

  1. (Optional) Click the Remove button for one or more items in the Selected items section to remove the item(s) from the list.

  2. Click Save to save changes to the Forms and Documents tab.

  3. Indicate when the change needs to take effect by selecting one of the following:

  • Immediately

  • Future Date and enter a date in the Start Date field

  • Save as Draft

  1. Click Submit Workflow.

Editing a New Hire

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Editing_New_Hire_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Editing_New_Hire_OB.htm

Editing a New Hire

The following details how to edit new hire information using the Edit New Hire action.

To edit new hire information:

  1. In the Filters panel, you can filter the search results by client or by new hire information. To filter by client, enter a client ID number in the Client ID field or click the Client ID link to select a client ID number from a list. To filter by new hire information, enter a new hire’s last name, first name, Social Security Number, or employee ID in the New Hire field.

  2. Click Search. The Search Results panel displays the following information:

Field — Description

New Hire — The employee’s name.

SSN — The employee’s Social Security Number. The value shown may be masked or hidden depending on settings at the system or client level.

Employee ID — The employee’s ID number.

Employer Start Date — The date the employee started with the employer.

Client Start Date — The date the employee started with the client.

Personal Email — The employee’s personal email address.

Work Email — The employee’s email address with the client.

Action — Edit: Enables you to edit new hire information.

  1. In the Action column, click Edit. The Edit New Hire, Personal Information, Employment Details, Pay Details, and User panels display.

  2. Editing new hire information without an employee record:

If the employee has not submitted their Employee Information form (the employee does not have an employee record in the system), you can edit the following fields:

Field Description
Work Location The location where the employee works. When selecting a new location, the New‑Hire Questions form opens. Required.
Social Security Number The employee’s Social Security number. Required.
First Name, Last Name, Middle Name The employee’s first and last name. Required. The employee’s middle name. Configure as required or optional on the Global New Hire Fields and New Hire Optional Fields forms.
Date of Birth The employee’s date of birth.
Personal Email, Work Email The employee’s personal and work email addresses. To send an email indicating that the workflow is launched, click Send “Workflow Launched” Email. The link is disabled if: - The notification is not set up for the Employee recipient - The notification is not enabled on the workflow - The Personal Email and Work Email fields are blank
  • The notification is not set up for the Employee recipient
  • The notification is not enabled on the workflow
  • The Personal Email and Work Email fields are blank | Employer Start Date, Client Start Date | You can edit the Employer Start Date and Client Start Date if the employee has not submitted the Employee Information form. Edits to these dates automatically update the workflow due date and the employee hire date on page two of Form I‑9. You cannot edit the Employer Start Date or the Client Start Date after the employee submits their Employee Information form. | | Position | A unique code that briefly describes the position. For example, OPER for vehicle operator. Codes cannot contain spaces or special characters. Required. | | Employment Type | A unique code that briefly describes the type of employment. For example, FT for full‑time. Codes cannot contain spaces or special characters. | | Department | A unique code that briefly describes the department. For example, SLS for sales. Codes cannot contain spaces or special characters. | | Work Group | A unique code that briefly describes the work group. For example, 1 for level‑one employees. Codes cannot contain spaces or special characters. | | PTO Approver | The person who would approve the employee’s PTO requests. | | Pay Group | A unique code that briefly describes the pay group. For example, BW for biweekly. Codes cannot contain spaces or special characters. | | Pay Method | The employee’s method of pay (required). Note: The Variable Salary option only displays if the VARSALMETHOD custom feature code is enabled on the System Parameters form. You can use the Variable Salary option to accurately track the hours that salary-exempt employees actually work. For example, non-profit companies could use this option to track the work hours of their employees who are paid a standard salaried amount, so they can use these hours and their allocations for grant purposes. | | Pay Rate | The rate the employee is paid based on the pay method. | | Pay Rate Basis | Whether the employee is paid Hourly, Weekly, Bi‑Weekly, Semi‑Monthly, Monthly, or Yearly. | The User panel shows the new hire’s User ID and Last changed on date indicating the date of the most recent change to the user account.

Enabling Onboarding

To enable Onboarding, use the Global Configuration form in PrismHR.

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Global Configuration. The Global Configuration form opens.

  3. Select Enable Onboarding.

  4. Click Save.

Enabling Spanish for Onboarding

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Enabling_Spanish_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Enabling_Spanish_OB.htm

Enabling Spanish

To help onboard your Spanish-speaking employees, refer to the following:

  1. Viewing Default Forms Translations

  2. Enabling Spanish for the Onboarding Process

  3. Configuring the Custom Forms in the Form Library

  4. Creating a Workflow in Spanish

  5. Working with the Custom Form Library in Spanish

  6. Working with PDFs in Spanish and English

  7. Changing the Language While Reviewing a Form

Viewing Default Forms Translations

PrismHR includes default employee-facing forms in Spanish for the onboarding process.

Custom forms are translated by the user (see Configuring the Custom Forms in the Form Library).

[Image: screenshot]

Enabling Spanish for the Onboarding Process

To enable Spanish:

  1. To enable Spanish, go to the Global Configuration form, and select the Enable Additional Languages field.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

  2. In the New Hire form, enter the required information and select Spanish in the Preferred Language field.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

Configuring the Custom Forms in the Form Library

Use the Form Library to build custom forms in Spanish.

To use the Form Library:

  1. From the Form Library, click Create New Form. The Create New Custom Form displays.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter the Form ID.

  2. When you click in the Form Name field, the Form Name form displays, Enter Form Name in English and then in Spanish.

  3. Click Save.

  4. Enter the Description and Workflow Type.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Continue.

  2. Enter the Form Symbol in the Form Symbolfield.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

  2. Select when the change needs to go into effect.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Select Submit Form.

  2. Click Done.

Creating a Workflow in Spanish

Use the Workflow Library form to manage the workflow.

To use the workflow library:

  1. Go to the Workflow Library form.

  2. Click Create New Workflow.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. The Create New Workflow form displays. Enter the ID, Name, and Workflow type.

  2. Click Save.

  3. Select the Process tab.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Select the pencil icons to overwrite the form in Spanish. Enter a field label in Spanish.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

  2. Select the Forms and Documents tab.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Select the forms to include in the workflow by moving them from available items to selected items.

Note: Form names in the workflow can be in English and Spanish.

  1. Click Save.

Working with the Custom Form Library in Spanish

  1. Go to the Form Library. Click Create New Form.

  2. Enter Form ID, Form Name, Description, and Workflow Type.

  3. Click Continue.

  4. Select the Layout tab. The Edit Custom Form displays.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. To edit a form, drag the form from the Field Type left column to the Field Label section on the right.

  2. Click Update. The Configure checkbox form displays.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter the Field label in English and Spanish.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Done.

  2. Configure the existing form by selecting the pencil icon.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. The Configure checkbox form displays. You can customize the Field Labels in both English and Spanish.

  2. Click Done.

Working with PDFs in Spanish and English

You can configure custom forms in Spanish (including PDFs) in the Form Library.

  1. In the PDF Integration section in the Form Library, you can upload English or Spanish PDFs.

  • English: Select Upload English PDF.

  • Spanish: With Spanish selected, select Upload PDF.

Note: You must select Upload English PDF first and then select Spanish Upload PDF.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

  2. If there is a mismatch of fields, an error message displays when the form uploads. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Changing the Language While Reviewing a Form

While reviewing forms in the Form Library, you can change languages by selecting a language from the Profile drop-down field.

[Image: screenshot]

Employee Process for Completing Form I-9 Section 1

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Form_I-9_Section1-Employee-Process.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Form_I-9_Section1-Employee-Process.htm

Employee Process for Completing Form I-9 Section 1

To complete Section 1 of Form I-9:

If necessary, the employee can click View Instructions to review instructions for completing Form I-9 directly on the USCIS website.

  1. The employee logs in to Onboarding and selects I-9 in the navigation menu on the left. The Form I-9 Instructions Overview displays. [Image: screenshot]
  2. After reviewing the information in each section, the employee clicks Next to proceed to the Preparer and Translator Certification. [Image: screenshot] If the employee was assisted by a preparer or translator in completing the Form I-9, the employee selects Yes and an additional field displays to allow the employee to upload the Form I-9 provided by their preparer or translator. In addition, the employee can select the I have more than one preparer or translator option to indicate multiple preparers or translators were used and upload an I-9 Supplement form provided by their second preparer or translator. Otherwise, the employee selects No.
  3. Once a selection is made and documents have been uploaded as applicable, the employee clicks Next. The Attestation of Citizenship or Immigration Status displays. [Image: screenshot] The employee selects the appropriate option for their citizenship or immigration status. Depending on the option selected, additional fields display to allow the employee to enter additional information such as their Alien Registration Number/USCIS Number.
  4. After making entries as necessary, the employee clicks Next. The Supporting Documents screen displays. > Note: Note: This screen only displays if the option for uploading supporting documents is enabled for the client. If uploading supporting documents is disabled, the Employee Information screen displays instead. Proceed to step 5. For more information about enabling this setting at the client level, refer to Setting Client Control Parameters . For more information about enabling this setting at the system level, refer to Maintaining Global Onboarding Options . [Image: screenshot] If the employee chooses to upload supporting documents at a later time, the employee selects No. If the employee chooses to upload supporting documents now, the employee selects Yes. Additional fields display to allow the employee to select the type of supporting documents to upload and then upload the documents.
  5. Once a selection is made and documents have been uploaded as applicable, the employee clicks Next. The Employee Information screen displays. [Image: screenshot] The employee enters information in each field as appropriate. Once entries are made, the employee selects the checkbox in the Signature of Employee section to indicate that they consent to electronically signing the form. The employee’s name and the current date are automatically entered in this section when the checkbox is selected. The employee’s address is validated automatically to ensure accuracy.
  6. After selecting the checkbox in the Signature of Employee section, the employee clicks Next. The Review screen displays. [Image: screenshot] The employee can review a PDF copy of their completed Form I-9 and download a copy for their own records if necessary. If information on the form needs to be corrected or otherwise modified, the employee clicks Back to navigate back to the appropriate screen and correct the information.
  7. After reviewing the information on the form, the employee clicks Submit. A message window displays to confirm the form should be submitted. The employee clicks Yes, and the completed section of the Form I-9 is submitted for approval. The onboarding process then continues to the next form in the workflow.

========================================================================

Generating PDF Files

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Generating_PDF_Files_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Generating_PDF_Files_OB.htm

Generating PDF Files

When a new hire completes and submits a form in their onboarding workflow, a copy of the PDF file stores in the Document Repository and Employee Documents forms in PrismHR.

Note: Note: PrismHR runs a daily process to generate any missing custom form PDF files. This is important for clients that started using Onboarding after the introduction of the custom form PDF generation feature in March of 2019.

Viewing Employee Documents

To upload and manage documents, use the Document Repository. Documents can include logos and other images, PDFs, spreadsheets, and so on. You can also view PDF copies of submitted forms by employees during their onboarding workflow.

To view employee forms in the Document Repository:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change select Document Repository. The Document Repository opens.

  3. In Classification, select Onboarding. A list of onboarding‑related documents displays.

  4. In Select documents to display, select Employee for Client ID. Then use the search function to select a client. A list of employee documents for the selected client displays.

Viewing Submitted Forms

To view PDF copies of employee submitted forms during their onboarding workflow, use the Employee Documents form.

To view employee forms in Employee Documents:

  1. In the HR menu under HR|Change, select Employee Details. The Employee Details formopens.

  2. In Employee, enter an employee ID or select Employee to choose from a list.

  3. In the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] , select Documents. The Documents form opens.

For more information on the Employee Documents form, see the Human Resources User Guide.

========================================================================

Including the Electronic Consent Form

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Including_Electronic_Consent.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Including_Electronic_Consent.htm

Including the Electronic Consent Form

The Form Library and Workflow Library enable you to configure and include an electronic consent form at the beginning of a workflow. In the Form Library, the prebuilt Electronic Consent form provides a template for you to configure. In the Workflow Library, you can include the form in a workflow. The Electronic Consent form displays on the Process tab in the Start Process section.

  • When creating a new workflow, the form is enabled by default. You can disable it to not include the form in the workflow.

  • When editing a preexisting workflow, the form is disabled by default and must be enabled to include in the workflow.

When the form is in a workflow, an employee can accept or decline electronic consent during the onboarding process.

When an employee accepts the Electronic Consent form… When an employee declines the Electronic Consent form…
- The employee can continue to complete additional forms electronically. - A PDF generates and stores in the Document Repository in PrismHR. The PDF is also available in Employee Documents. During onboarding, the employee can download a copy. - The employee cannot complete onboarding electronically. They must complete onboarding using paper forms. - The employee can edit their electronic consent and select Accept. - A PDF generates and stores in the Document Repository in PrismHR. During onboarding, the employee can download a copy. - With the OBCONSENTDECLINED message template, an email is sent to the service provider of the client with instructions about how to complete the employee onboarding process using paper forms. The set of recipients include those with the Account Management Roles of Human Resource and Payroll Representative in PrismHR.
  • The employee can continue to complete additional forms electronically.
  • A PDF generates and stores in the Document Repository in PrismHR. The PDF is also available in Employee Documents. During onboarding, the employee can download a copy.
  • The employee cannot complete onboarding electronically. They must complete onboarding using paper forms.
  • The employee can edit their electronic consent and select Accept.
  • A PDF generates and stores in the Document Repository in PrismHR. During onboarding, the employee can download a copy.

  • With the OBCONSENTDECLINED message template, an email is sent to the service provider of the client with instructions about how to complete the employee onboarding process using paper forms. The set of recipients include those with the Account Management Roles of Human Resource and Payroll Representative in PrismHR.

Including State‑Required Forms

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Including_State-Required_Forms.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Including_State-Required_Forms.htm

Including State‑Required Forms

When including state‑required forms in a workflow (such as the South Carolina Terms of Employment Notice or the Washington D.C. Notice of Hire), you must:

  • Create the form business rule

  • Create the form

  • Enter the state‑required questions

To access form PDFs, see State‑Required Forms for Onboarding on the Customer Resource Center.

The following explains creating the form business rule in the system.

To create the form business rule:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Business Rules Library. The Business Rules Library opens.

  3. Click Create Rule. The Create Business Rule form opens.

  4. Complete the following:

Launching a New Workflow

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Launching_New_Workflow_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Launching_New_Workflow_OB.htm

Launching a New Workflow

The following details how to launch new workflows using the Launch New Workflow action.

To launch a new workflow:

  1. In the Filters panel, you can filter the search results by client or by new hire information. To filter by client, enter a client ID number in the Client ID field or click the Client ID link to select a client ID number from a list. To filter by new hire information, enter a new hire’s last name, first name, Social Security Number, or employee ID in the New Hire field.

  2. Click Search. The Search Results panel displays the following information:

Field — Description

New Hire — The employee’s name.

SSN — The employee’s Social Security Number. The value shown may be masked or hidden depending on settings at the system or client level.

Employee ID — The employee’s ID number.

Client ID — The client ID number.

Employer Start Date — The date the employee started with the employer.

Client Start Date — The date the employee started with the client.

Closed Workflow — The name and version of the closed workflow.

Closed Date — The date the prehire record was created.

New Workflow — The name and version of the new workflow to be launched.

Action — Launch: Enables you to launch a new workflow.

  1. In the Action column, click Launch.

  2. Launching a new workflow without an employee record:

At the start of the onboarding process, new hires do not have an employee record (they have only a prehire record). In the Launch Options panel, you can specify the following information for employees with a prehire record:

Select — To

Workflow that will be launched — Allows you to select any of the active onboarding workflows from a drop-down.

Launch new workflow immediately — Enables you to launch a new workflow immediately.

Go to New Hire page to enter employee data and launch new workflow — Opens the New Hire form where you must enter the new hire information again to launch a new onboarding process.

  1. Click Continue. The system returns you to the Workflow Maintenance form.

Launching a new workflow with an employee record:

In the Launch Options panel, you can specify the following information for employees with an employee record:

Select — To

Workflow that will be launched — Allows you to select any of the active onboarding workflows from a drop-down.

Launch new workflow immediately — Launches a new workflow immediately. If the new workflow has a cross hire conflict, a confirmation dialog box displays. The message indicates that the Social Security Number matches an employee in a different company, but the First Name, Last Name, or Date of Birth do not match. If you continue, the conflict will be saved. However, the workflow will not be launched until you resolve the conflict on the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form.

Go to Employee Details to make changes and launch new workflow — Opens Employee Details where you can enter employee information.

[Image: screenshot]

On the Employee Details form, you can:

  • Click Save & Onboard Now after entering the employee’s data to save their changes and launch the new workflow. The Employee Details form refreshes.

  • Click Remove Onboarding to cancel the launch of the new workflow. The Employee Details refreshes to its original state prior to launching a new workflow.

Onboarding-related information appears on every tab of the Employee Details form in the upper right‑hand corner.

Managing the Workflow Library (Onboarding)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Managing%20the%20Workflow%20Library%20(OB).htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Managing the Workflow Library (OB).htm

Managing the Workflow Library (Onboarding)

To configure onboarding workflows, use the Workflow Library.

To create and configure onboarding workflows:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Workflow Library. The Workflow Library opens.

  3. Filter these fields as required:

Field Description
Name The workflow name.
Id The workflow ID assigned on the Create New Workflow form.
Version The workflow version number.
Type Onboarding.
Status - Active: The workflow is in use. - Draft: The workflow is saved in Draft mode. - Inactive: The workflow is not in use.
  • Active: The workflow is in use.
  • Draft: The workflow is saved in Draft mode.
  • Inactive: The workflow is not in use. | Date of Creation | The date the workflow was created. | | Start Date | The date the workflow was designated to become available for use. | Prebuilt forms are shown in the build.

See the following to create new workflows:

  • To create a new workflow to use in Onboarding, see Creating a Workflow (Onboarding).

You can also add notifications that you want to send to employees or groups to workflows. To do this, you must first set up notification templates and recipients. When created, notifications are available in the Notifications tab in the Workflow Library where you can add them to workflows as needed.

  • To set up the content for the notifications you want to send out to employees or groups during enrollments, and specify when to send the notifications, see Setting Up Notification Templates (Onboarding).

  • To set up recipients for the notifications. Notification recipients can be specified by group (for example, Internal Employees, Managers), by user role (for example, Client Benefit Representative), or both, see Setting Up Notification Recipients (Onboarding).

The Workflow Library also maintains multiple versions and revisions of workflows:

  • For information about viewing and managing workflow versions and revisions in the Workflow Library, see Managing Versions and Revisions.
