Managing Notification Objects and Tasks (Onboarding)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Managing_Notification_Objects_Tasks_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Managing_Notification_Objects_Tasks_OB.htm

Managing Notification Objects and Tasks (Onboarding)

You can specify sending Onboarding notifications based on the object specified in the Notification tab when setting up a workflow in the Workflow Library. For event based notifications an object can be a workflow, specific form, or the I-9 Section 2 form.

The objects that display in the Object column differ depending on the notification Trigger Condition. For time based notifications, an object can be a task.

A task is an action that an employee or administrator must completed by the employee (or an administrator) by a fixed due date. Notifications that have the time‑based Trigger Type display tasks in the Object drop-down.

The following tables lists all of the notification conditions and their associated objects or tasks you can select when attaching a notification to an onboarding workflow.

Trigger Type Trigger Condition Object or Task Options Available
Event Based Workflow is launched Workflow (no additional objects or task list displays)
Event Based Workflow is completed Workflow (no additional objects; no drop-down displays)
Event Based Employee submits form - I-9 section 1 - Employee Information - Selected Forms - Submit
  • I-9 section 1
  • Employee Information
  • Selected Forms
  • Submit | Event Based | Approver confirms | I-9 Section 2 (no additional approval policies or list displays) | | Event Based | Approver rejects | I-9 Section 2 (no additional approval policies or list displays) | | Time Based | X Days before due date | Tasks available: - I-9 section 1 - Employee Information - I-9 Section 2 - All Other Forms |
  • I-9 section 1
  • Employee Information
  • I-9 Section 2
  • All Other Forms | Time Based | X Days after due date | Tasks available: - I-9 section 1 - Employee Information - I-9 Section 2 - All Other Forms |
  • I-9 section 1
  • Employee Information
  • I-9 Section 2
  • All Other Forms Onboarding includes the following tasks:
Task Due Date
I-9 Section 1 Employee’s date of hire
Employee Information Employee’s date of hire
I-9 Section 2 Approval due 3 days after date of hire
All Other Forms Onboarding end date

For example, if the task I-9 Section 1 is set up with the time based Trigger Condition X Days before due date, and the number Days are defined as 5, the notification is sent 5 days before the employee’s date of hire.

Note: Note: The date of hire is defined as the later of Client Start Date and Employer Start Date. It is the day the new hire begins employment.

========================================================================

Managing Versions and Revisions

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Managing_Versions_and_Revisions_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Managing_Versions_and_Revisions_OB.htm

Managing Versions and Revisions

The Form Library and Workflow Library both maintain multiple versions and revisions for forms and workflows. The system sets revisions automatically when you submit a new form or workflow or modify an existing one.

  • When you create a new form or workflow, the system automatically sets the Version to 1.0.

  • If you change the form or workflow and select the option to have them go into effect immediately, the system adds a Revision number after the decimal point in the Version number (1.1, 1.2, and so on).

The system also immediately sets the Status to Active, and sets the current date as the Start Date. Since there can be only one active version of the form, the system changes the Status of any prior active version to Inactive and sets the current date as its End Date.

  • If you change the form or workflow and select the option to have the changes go into effect at a future date, the system automatically sets the Version number to the next number in the sequence, and resets the revision number to zero (2.0, 3.0, and so on).

The system sets the new version’s Status to Active on the Start Date, which you assign when you submit the form or workflow. Since there can be only one active version, the system changes the Status of any prior active version to Inactive and sets the current date as its End Date.

You can view and compare different versions and revisions in both libraries by clicking the form or workflow ID in the table. The Versions form opens, displaying all versions and revisions that exist.

  • Click the ID if you want to modify a form or workflow version or revision.

  • To compare two versions or revisions of a form or workflow, select the two you want to compare and click Compare. The Comparison panel opens below the main table in the form (you must scroll down to view data) and displays settings for both.

Maintaining Message Templates

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Message_Templates.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Message_Templates.htm

Maintaining Message Templates

[Image: screenshot]

To configure message templates:

  1. Complete these fields as required.

Field — Description

Message ID — Enter or select an ID for the template. For example, Password Reset could be identified as PWDRESET. When you enter or select a Message ID, additional language fields display.

Description — Enter the description of the template. For example, HR Action Approval Email Template.

Additional languages can be selected from the drop-down in the box on the right that displays after you select the Message ID. For example, you can configure a Spanish message template, which will display a notification in Spanish for employees whose preferred language is set to Spanish in the Personal tab on the Employee Details form.

Subject — Enter the subject line for the message template.

Body — Enter the body text for the message template. (You can use HTML in the message body if the Content Type is set to HTML.)

Note: Note: You can use substitution variables for Subject and Body to customize the message appearance. (See Using Substitution Variables.)

  1. Click Save.

Using the Notification Control Panel (Onboarding)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Notification_Control_Panel_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Notification_Control_Panel_OB.htm

Using the Notification Control Panel (Onboarding)

Use the OB Notify section in the Notification Control Panel to:

  • Start and stop the notification engine during onboarding

  • Determine the frequency at which the notifications engine sends notifications

During onboarding, the system sends event and time-based notifications attached to the workflows to notification recipients. The system:

  • Sends time-based notifications daily, typically shortly after midnight. This applies only to incomplete forms in the onboarding workflow.

  • Sends event-based notifications when the event specified by the trigger condition occurs (for example, when the employee completes the workflow). The notification engine runs in the background and periodically checks to see whether there are notifications to send. If so, it sends event-based notifications when they turn up during the check.

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note: The options in the BE Notify section are only used in Benefits Enrollment and do not affect Onboarding.

To check for and send available event-based notifications:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From Operations|Action select Notification Control Panel. The Notification Control Panel form opens.

  3. In OB Notify Interval (mins), enter the number of minutes. You can enter values from 5 to 1440 minutes.

Note: The state of the process, ENABLED or DISABLED, displays next to the OB Notify Interval (mins) field.

OB Notify Date populates automatically with the date the system last sent time-based notifications.

If you need to manually resend a notification (for example, if employees did not receive the notification, or if there is a change to the notification template), you can delete the displayed OB Notify Date, and click Save. The system resends time-based notifications for that day with the current date. When the notifications are resent, the current date displays.

  1. To submit your settings and start the notifications engine, click Save.

Click this in the OB Notify section — To do this

Stop — Stop the system from sending notifications — Stop changes to Start, and the status changes to NOT RUNNING. The request to stop notifications takes effect immediately. However, it can take a few minutes for the updated status to display correctly. Note: The status displays next to the Stop button.

Start — Start the notifications engine without changing settings — Start changes to Stop, and the status changes to RUNNING. If you stop and then restart the notifications engine, the process ID (PID) number resets and a new PID displays. Note: The status displays next to the Start button.

Cancel — Close without saving changes.

========================================================================

Setting Up Notification Recipients (Onboarding)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Notification_Recipients_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Notification_Recipients_OB.htm

Setting Up Notification Recipients (Onboarding)

To set up recipients for onboarding notifications created using the Notification Templates form, use the Notification Recipients form.

This form can also be used to modify or delete existing notification recipient records. However, recipient records that are currently associated with a notification template cannot be deleted.

[Image: screenshot]

To set up notification recipients:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Notification Recipients. The Notification Recipients form opens.

  3. Complete the following fields:

Field — Action

Notification ID — Enter an existing notification ID, or click Setup a new Notificationcreate a new notification.

Name — Enter a name for the notification recipient group. The name displays on the Workflow Library form in the list of notification users you can add to workflows.

Description — (Optional) Enter a description for the notification.

  1. Select recipients for the notification using the following panels as appropriate:

Panel — Description

To Group Recipients — Primary recipients for the notification, organized by group (for example, Internal Employees, Managers, and so on). To set up groups, use the Message Center Groups form. > Note: Note: Message groups that include client records cannot be used in notification recipient records.

To Generic — Primary recipients for the notification, organized by role: Employee, Client Benefit Rep, Human Resources, Client Manager, I-9 Section 2 Approver. The Client Benefit Rep, Human Resources, and Client Manager roles can be assigned to users using the Users form. Before selecting I-9 Section 2 Approver as a generic recipient, I-9 Section 2 approval policies must be set up at the system level using the Global Approval Policies form and at the client level using the Approval Policies form. An administrator must also be assigned to the I-9 Approval role using the Users form. For more information, see Configuring System Settings.

CC Group Recipients — Additional recipients to copy publicly, organized by group.

CC Generic — Additional recipients to copy publicly, organized by role.

BCC Group Recipients — Additional recipients to copy privately, organized by group.

BCC Generic — Additional recipients to copy privately, organized by role.

  1. In the Notification Template panel, enter the ID number of the template to use in the Template ID field. The name and description associated with the template are automatically entered in the appropriate fields, and the appropriate check boxes are selected for the associated workflow type and message delivery method.

  2. Click Save.

Setting Up Notification Templates (Onboarding)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Notification_Templates_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Notification_Templates_OB.htm

Setting Up Notification Templates (Onboarding)

The Notification Templates form is used to create templates for the notifications that are sent to employees or groups during onboarding. Each template specifies the content that is included in the notification, such as the subject and message body, as well as the criteria for sending the notification and the communication method used to send the notification.

When defining notification content, substitution variables can be used to replace generic information with specific information from PrismHR.

Once the template has been created, the Notification Recipients form can be used to define the recipients who receive the notification. Refer to Setting Up Notification Recipients (Onboarding) for more information. In addition, the notifications can be added to a workflow using the Workflow Library. Refer to Creating a Workflow (Onboarding) for more information.

Note: Note: Notification templates that have been attached to a workflow or a notification recipient record cannot be deleted.

To set up a notification template:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Notification Templates. The Notification Templates form displays.

  3. In the Notification Templates panel, complete the following fields:

Field — Action

Template Id — To modify an existing template, enter a template ID or click Template Id to display a list of existing templates. To create a new template, click Create a New Template. An available template ID is entered in the Template Id field.

Name — Enter a name for the template. This name identifies the template and displays in the list of available templates when assigning notification recipients.

Description — Enter a description of the template.

Language Default — For future development. Does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR.

Subject — Enter a subject for notifications sent using the template. > Note: Note: Do not use square brackets ([ or ]) in this field as doing so will prevent recipients from receiving notifications sent using this template.

Message Body — Enter the body text for the notifications sent using the template.

SMS Body — For future development. Does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR.

  1. In the Notification Triggers panel, complete the following fields:
Field Action
Trigger Type Select a notification trigger type. - Time Based: Notifications are sent a specific number of days before or after the workflow’s due date. If the notification engine is set up to check in the morning, the notifications are sent in the morning. If the notification engine is set up to check every other day, the notifications are sent every other day. Notification engine settings are defined using the Notification Control Panel. Refer to Using the Notification Control Panel (Onboarding) for more information. - Event Based: Notifications are sent when a specific event occurs, such as when an onboarding workflow is completed. The notification engine runs in the background and periodically checks to see whether there are notifications to send and sends event-based notifications when they turn up during the check.
  • Time Based: Notifications are sent a specific number of days before or after the workflow’s due date.
  • Event Based: Notifications are sent when a specific event occurs, such as when an onboarding workflow is completed. | Trigger Condition | If Time Based is selected in the Trigger Type drop-down, the following options are available: - X Days before start date - X Days before due date - X Days before hire date - X Days after hire date If Event Based is selected in the Trigger Type drop-down, the following options are available: - Workflow is launched - Workflow is completed - Employee submits form - Approver confirms - Approver rejects - Employee submits workflow with form field response |
  • X Days before start date
  • X Days before due date
  • X Days before hire date
  • X Days after hire date
  • Workflow is launched
  • Workflow is completed
  • Employee submits form
  • Approver confirms
  • Approver rejects
  • Employee submits workflow with form field response | Number of Days | Enter the number of days before or after the workflow’s due date at which to send the notification. > Note: Note: This field is only available if Time Based is selected in the Trigger Type drop-down. |
  1. In the Message Delivery Method panel, select Email to send the notification using email.

Note: Note: The Send SMS option is for future development and does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR.

  1. Click Save.

Onboarding Dashboard for Worksite Managers

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/OB_Dashboard_WSM.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/OB_Dashboard_WSM.htm

Onboarding Dashboard for Worksite Managers

The following procedure details the steps required to add the Onboarding dashboard to the worksite manager role in PrismHR.

  1. Log in to PrismHR as a service provider.

  2. Open Users (found in the Back Office menu under System|Change).

  3. In the User Roles panel, enter the worksite manager’s Role ID with a brief Role Description.

  4. Open User Roles (found in the Back Office menu under System|Change).

  5. Enter the user Role ID (from Step 3), and select Add. The User Role Maintenance from displays.

  6. Select the Process ID HRPY.WORK*DASH, and select Accept.

  7. Select Save on the User Roles form.

  8. Log out of PrismHR, and log back in as the worksite manager.

  9. Open My Home (found in the Client menu under Client|Dashboard).

  10. Select Onboarding Tasks by Status from the My Persepctive drop-down to view information relevant to Onboarding.

Note: Note: Save the My Home form as a favorite for convenience.

Maintaining Global Onboarding Options

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Onboarding_Options.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Onboarding_Options.htm

Maintaining Global Onboarding Options

Maintain global onboarding parameters with the Onboarding Options form.

Use this option… — To do this…

Complete All Forms to Activate Payroll — Define the system default option for allowing employees to be added to payroll before all onboarding‑related forms in their workflow are completed. When the Complete All Forms to Activate Payroll field on the Control tab on the Client Details form is set to System Default, the system uses this option to determine whether the client’s employees can be added to payroll before all onboarding-related forms in their workflow are completed. When the Complete All Forms to Activate Payroll field on the Control tab on the Client Details form is set to Enabled or Disabled, the system uses the setting in that field to determine whether the client’s employees can be added to payroll before all onboarding-related forms in their workflow are completed.

Enable Benefit Enrollment After Final Submit — Define the system default option for allowing employees to access their benefit enrollment workflow through Onboarding after submitting all onboarding‑related forms. When the Enable Benefit Enrollment After Final Submit field on the Control tab on the Client Details form is set to System Default, the system uses this option to determine whether the client’s employees can access their benefit enrollment workflow through Onboarding after submitting all onboarding‑related forms. When the Enable Benefit Enrollment After Final Submit field on the Control tab on the Client Details form is set to Enabled or Disabled, the system uses the setting in that field to determine whether the client’s employees can access their benefit enrollment workflow through Onboarding after submitting all onboarding‑related forms.

Disable Pay Rate Change Approval for Rehire — Disable approval requests for rehire pay rate changes on the Pay tab on the Employee Details form.

Suspend Workflow When “Onboarding in Progress” Manually Unchecked — Enable this option to suspend employee onboarding workflows when the Onboarding in Progress option on the Other tab on the Employee Details form is manually disabled. If this option is disabled and a manager also disables the Onboarding in Progress option, the employee onboarding workflow stays in progress.

Allow Upload of Supporting Documents (I-9) — Define the system default option for allowing employees to upload supporting I-9 documents during the I-9 workflow. When the Allow Upload of Supporting Documents (I-9) field on the Control tab on the Client Details form is set to System Default, the system uses this option to determine whether the client’s employees are allowed to upload supporting I-9 documents. When the Allow Upload of Supporting Documents (I-9) field on the Control tab on the Client Details form is set to Enabled or Disabled, the system uses the setting in that field to determine the client’s employees are allowed to upload supporting I-9 documents.

Archive Prehire Workflows for Terminated Employees Older Than — Select the number of months after which prehire records and onboarding workflows for terminated employees are archived. By default, 12 Months is selected. > Note: Note: A number of months that is shorter or longer than the onboarding window length can be selected using this option. To also archive onboarding workflows for active employees and employees on leave of absence, in addition to terminated employees, select the Include Non-Terminated Employees check box. When this check box is selected, a message displays to indicate that existing workflows will be archived and that electronic onboarding will be unable to continue for the affected employees. However, completed onboarding documents will remain available as normal. After a prehire record or onboarding workflow has been archived, the record can be restored using the Workflow Maintenance form. See Maintaining Workflow Actions.

Enable Logging in Rules Engine — Enable this option to turn on logging. When you select this option and the system creates a prehire record, a trace logging file is also created for the prehire record if the employee for whom the prehire record was created is assigned an Onboarding workflow that includes forms to which business rules are applied. (Disable this field to prevent the system from creating the trace logging file for the prehire record, which can increase performance.)

OB background PDF generator — Enable a background process that locates and generates missing PDF documents for completed Onboarding workflows.

Employee Information Form Fields — Define which fields on the Employee Information form are required in onboarding workflows.

Registering by Proxy

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Registering_by_Proxy_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Registering_by_Proxy_OB.htm

Registering by Proxy

Cross hire employees can have multiple employee IDs in the system. For example, this occurs when an employee works for both a PEO and ASO. It also occurs when an employee works for both an ASO (Employer 1) and another ASO (Employer 2).

Cross hire employee onboarding with a newly generated employee ID for Employer 1 have only a prehire record. Since the Users form in PrismHR does not display employee IDs from prehire records, the service provider cannot create a new user record for Employer 2. To create a user record, you can use the Proxy Registration action.

The following details how to register by proxy with the Proxy Registration action.

To create a user record by proxy:

  1. In the Filters panel, you can filter the search results by client or by new hire information. To filter by client, enter a client ID number in the Client ID field or click the Client ID link to select a client ID number from a list. To filter by new hire information, enter a new hire’s last name, first name, Social Security Number, or employee ID in the New Hire field.

  2. Click Search. The Search Results panel displays the following information:

Field — Description

New Hire — The employee’s name.

SSN — The employee’s Social Security number. The system may hide the value shown depending on settings at the system or client level.

Employee ID — The employee’s ID number.

Employer Start Date — The date the employee started with the employer.

Client Start Date — The date the employee started with the client.

Personal Email — The employee’s personal email address.

Action — Register: Lets you register the employee in the system by proxy.

  1. In the Action column, click Register. The Proxy Registration panel displays.

  2. Complete the following required fields:

Use this option To do this
Email Address Enter the employee’s email address.
Username Enter the employee’s username.
Password Enter the employee’s password.
Confirm Password Reenter the employee’s password.
  1. Click Register. The system creates the user record and the employee no longer displays in the proxy registration search results list.

Reopening a Workflow

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Reopening_Workflow_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Reopening_Workflow_OB.htm

Reopening a Workflow

The following details how to reopen a workflow using the Reopen Workflow action.

To reopen a workflow:

  1. In the Filters panel, you can filter the search results by client or by new hire information. To filter by client, enter a client ID number in the Client ID field or click the Client ID link to select a client ID number from a list. To filter by new hire information, enter a new hire’s last name, first name, Social Security Number, or employee ID in the New Hire field.

  2. Click Search. The Search Results panel displays the following information:

Field Description
New Hire The employee’s name.
SSN The employee’s Social Security Number. The value shown may be masked or hidden depending on settings at the system or client level.
Employee ID The employee’s ID number.
Employer Start Date The date the employee started with the employer.
Client Start Date The date the employee started with the client.
Workflow The workflow name and version number.
Submit Date The date the new hire submitted the onboarding workflow. If the workflow includes the Form I-9, the Submit Date is the later of the following: - The date the onboarding workflow is submitted - The approval date for the I-9 Section 2 If the workflow does not include the Form I-9 Section 2, the Submit Date is the date the onboarding workflow is submitted.
  • The date the onboarding workflow is submitted
  • The approval date for the I-9 Section 2 | Action | Reopen: Enables you to reopen the new hire workflow. |

  1. In the Action column, click Reopen. The Reopen Workflow and Reopen Options panels display.

  2. Complete the following:

Use this option — To do this

Unlock I‑9 Section 1 for employee (Reopens workflow and I‑9 Section 2) — Unlock the Form I‑9 Section 1 and reopen the workflow and Form I‑9 Section 2 Approval. This option is available if the I‑9 Section 2 Approval is in progress or was submitted by the approver.

Reopen workflow for employee — Reopen the workflow. This options is available if the employee submitted the workflow.

Reopen I‑9 Section 2 for approver — Reopen the Form I‑9 Section 2 Approval. This option is available if the approver submitted the I‑9 Section 2 Approval.

  1. Click Continue.

Reviewing Cross Hire Conflicts

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Reviewing_Cross_Hire_Conflicts.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Reviewing_Cross_Hire_Conflicts.htm

Reviewing Cross Hire Conflicts

Note: Note: On the User Roles form, you can only grant full access (Full) or deny access (Deny) to the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form. Currently, the Inquiry option functions the same as granting full access.

On the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form, you must review the conflict shown to decide whether the new hire is the same person as the existing employee. This may involve speaking with the manager who entered the new hire, the new hire, or the existing employee. To help you communicate with these people, the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form shows the person who entered the conflict, as well as contact information for the manager who entered the new hire, the new hire, and the existing employee.

If the new hire is the same person as the existing employee, you can correct the mismatched fields so the system can automatically launch the workflow. If the data correction involves an active onboarding workflow, that workflow resets so the employee can resign all forms with the correct information. If the new hire is not the same person as the existing employee, you can delete the new hire.

To open the Review Cross Hire Conflicts form:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Action, select Review Cross Hire Conflicts. The Review Cross Hire Conflicts form opens.

Column Description
New Hire Name The new hire’s first and last name (last name, first name).
SSN The new hire’s Social Security number.
Client ID The client’s unique identification number.
Client Name The client’s name.
Conflict Date The date the conflict was entered into the system.
Data Entered By The person that entered the conflict in the system.
User Type The user type of the person that entered the conflict. - Service Provider - PEO employee or other users at your organization - Worksite Manager - Manager for a client - Worksite Employee - Employee for a client - Worksite Trusted Advisor - User who works for one or more clients and is not necessarily an employee
  • Service Provider - PEO employee or other users at your organization
  • Worksite Manager - Manager for a client
  • Worksite Employee - Employee for a client
  • Worksite Trusted Advisor - User who works for one or more clients and is not necessarily an employee To review cross hire conflicts:

Select a new hire from the list. The Review Conflict tab displays.

Section Description
New Hire Company The client with the conflict. - Client ID - The client’s unique identification number. - Client Company - The client’s name.
  • Client ID - The client’s unique identification number.
  • Client Company - The client’s name. | Conflicts Detected | Highlights conflicts between the Existing Employee Record and the New Hire Record. | | Choose a Resolution to the Conflict | There are two options for resolving the conflict: - Fix Conflict - If you determine that the new hire and the existing employee are the same person, you can update the data so the existing employee record and the new hire record match. For more information, see Fixing the Conflict. - Delete New Hire - If you determine that the new hire and the existing employee are not the same person, you can delete the new hire. For more information, see Deleting the New Hire. |
  • Fix Conflict - If you determine that the new hire and the existing employee are the same person, you can update the data so the existing employee record and the new hire record match. For more information, see Fixing the Conflict.
  • Delete New Hire - If you determine that the new hire and the existing employee are not the same person, you can delete the new hire. For more information, see Deleting the New Hire. ### Fixing the Conflict

After identifying that the new hire is the same person, you can fix the conflict to make the existing employee and new hire records match.

To fix the conflict:

  1. Click Fix Conflict. The Fix Conflicts dialog box displays.

  2. In Update data for both records, correct the conflicts and click Update.

  3. In the confirmation dialog box, click Yes, Update. The Review Cross Hire Conflicts form displays. Both records update to match correctly, and the conflict is removed from the list.

Deleting the Conflict

After identifying that the new hires are not the same person, you can delete the new hire record.

To delete the new hire:

  1. Click Delete New Hire.

  2. In the confirmation dialog box, click Yes, Delete. The Review Cross Hire Conflicts form displays. The record deletes and the conflict is removed from the list.

Viewing Who Entered the Conflict

To view the person that entered the conflict in the system, use the Conflict Entered By tab.

Viewing Contact Information

To view contact information for the manager who entered the new hire, new hire, and existing employee in the system related to the conflict, use the Contact Information tab.

Setting Up Approval Policies

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Setting_Up_Approval_Policies_(Global_and_Client_Level).htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Setting_Up_Approval_Policies_(Global_and_Client_Level).htm

Setting Up Approval Policies

Set up the I-9 Section 2 approval policies for your organization at the system or client level in the Global Approval Policies and the Approval Policies forms.

An I-9 Approver role is available in both forms. You can use that role, or any other role, for I-9 Section 2 approvals.

For more information about setting up approval policies at the system level, see the System Administration Guide or Approvals Guide. For more information about setting up approval policies at the client level, see the Human Resources User Guide or Approvals Guide.

Setting Up Form I-9 Approvers

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Setting_Up_Form_I-9_Approvers.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Setting_Up_Form_I-9_Approvers.htm

Setting Up Form I-9 Approvers

For Onboarding, you must set up an administrator as an I-9 Approver.

To define an administrator as an I‑9 Approver:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Users.

  3. In User ID, enter the administrator ID.

  4. In User Type:

  • For a service provider, in the Account Management Roles panel, select I‑9 Approver.

  • For a worksite manager, in the Human Resource Roles panel, select I‑9 Approver.

Suspending a Workflow

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Suspending_Workflow_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Suspending_Workflow_OB.htm

Suspending a Workflow

The following details how to suspend a workflow with the Suspend Workflow action.

To suspend a workflow:

  1. In the Filters panel, you can filter the search results by client or by new hire information. To filter by client, enter a client ID number in the Client ID field or click the Client ID link to select a client ID number from a list. To filter by new hire information, enter a new hire’s last name, first name, Social Security Number, or employee ID in the New Hire field.

  2. Click Search. The Search Results panel displays the following information:

Field — Description

New Hire — The employee’s name.

SSN — The employee’s Social Security Number. The value shown may be masked or hidden depending on settings at the system or client level.

Employee ID — The employee’s ID number.

Employer Start Date — The date the employee started with the employer.

Client Start Date — The date the employee started with the client.

Workflow — The workflow name and version number.

Launch Date — The date the onboarding workflow was initially launched.

Action — Suspend: Enables you to suspend onboarding workflows.

  1. In the Action column, click Suspend.

  2. Suspending a workflow without an employee record:

At the start of the onboarding process, new hires do not have an employee record (they have only a prehire record). For employees with only a prehire record, clicking Suspend displays a confirmation dialog box indicating that you are suspending the workflow, and a message shows when the employee logs in that their onboarding process is unavailable. To suspend the workflow, click Yes. The system returns you to the Workflow Maintenance form.

Suspending a workflow with an employee record:

For employees with only an employee record, clicking Suspend displays the Suspend Workflow and Suspend Options panels. In the Suspend Options panel, you can specify the following options for employees with an employee record:

Select — To

Display Onboarding unavailable message to the new hire. Use when you plan to launch a new onboarding workflow. — Show a message when an employee logs in that their onboarding process is unavailable.

Allow the new hire to access Employee Portal (EP) with Payroll enabled. Use when you do not plan to electronically onboard this person. — Enable the employee to access EP with payroll functionality enabled. If this option is selected, the employee cannot be electronically onboarded.

Click Continue. The system returns you to the Workflow Maintenance form.

Unarchiving a Workflow

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Unarchiving%20a%20Workflow.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Unarchiving a Workflow.htm

Unarchiving a Workflow

The following details how to restore prehire records and onboarding workflows using the Unarchive Workflow action.

To restore a prehire record and the associated workflow:

  1. In the Filters panel, you can filter the search results by client or by new hire information. To filter by client, enter a client ID number in the Client ID field or click the Client ID link to select a client ID number from a list. To filter by new hire information, enter a new hire’s last name, first name, Social Security Number, or employee ID in the New Hire field.

  2. Click Search. The Search Results panel displays the following information:

Field — Description

New Hire — The employee’s name.

SSN — The employee’s Social Security Number. The value shown may be masked or hidden depending on settings at the system or client level.

Employee ID — The employee’s ID number.

Client ID — The client ID number.

Employer Start Date — The date the employee started with the employer.

Client Start Date — The date the employee started with the client.

Workflow — The workflow name and version number.

Action — Unarchive: Allows you to restore the corresponding prehire record and associated workflow.

Note: Note: This feature can be used to view archived records and workflows based on the archiving time period defined on the Onboarding Options form plus an additional 180 days. For example, if 12 Months is entered in the Archive Prehire Workflows for Terminated Employees Older Than option on the Onboarding Options form, you can view archived records and workflows from the previous 12 months and an additional 180 days, for a total of 18 months.

  1. In the Action column, click Unarchive. The corresponding prehire record and the associated onboarding workflow are restored. Restored workflows retain the status and completion progress from the time of archiving, allowing the employee to resume onboarding from the point at which they left off.

Uploading PDF Files

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Uploading_PDF_Files.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Uploading_PDF_Files.htm

Uploading PDF Files

To upload and map PDF files to custom forms, use the PDF Integration option. Upload state‑required or custom PDF files to include in employee onboarding workflows. To download the following PDFs, see State‑Required Forms for Onboarding on the Customer Resource Center.

The following state‑required PDF files are supported for mapping:

  • California: Theft Notice to Employee (CA Wage)

  • New York: Notice for Hourly Rate Employees (NY Wage Hourly)

  • New York: Notice for Exempt Employees (NY Wage Salaried)

  • South Carolina: Terms of Employment Notice (SC Wage)

  • Washington D.C. Notice of Hire — Employment Status and Acknowledgment of Wage Rate(s) (DC Wage)

  • Minnesota Employee notice (MN Wage)

To upload a PDF file for mapping:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Form Library. The Form Library opens.

  3. Select the custom form from the list to open to the Edit Custom Form.

  4. On the General tab, click Please upload a compatible PDF for mapping. Then, select a document to upload.

The Confirm PDF Upload message displays if the layout of the form currently contains editable field types that will be removed. The editable field types are removed because all editable fields must be mapped to a PDF field.

  1. To confirm and continue, click Yes. When the PDF uploads, the PDF Fields Map tab displays.

Working with the PDF Fields Map

On the PDF Fields Map tab, you can view a complete list of the fields extracted from the uploaded file. If the uploaded file is a state-required PDF, the PDF field is automatically assigned the map. This value displays in the PDF Mapping field.

[Image: screenshot]

Field Description
PDF Field The name of the field on the PDF file.
PDF Mapping The value assigned to the PDF file field in the system.
Action Edit to assign a different map (source) to the PDF file field. The default value is None.

To configure the PDF file field mapping:

  1. Click Edit. The Edit PDF Fields Map opens.

[Image: screenshot]

Field Description
PDF Field (Target) The name of the field on the PDF file.
PDF Field Type The type of field on the PDF file. PrismHR supports the two most common field types found on PDF forms: - FIELD_TYPE_TEXT: Text field to enter data. - FIELD_TYPE_RADIOBUTTON: Option to select one of two items.
  • FIELD_TYPE_TEXT: Text field to enter data.
  • FIELD_TYPE_RADIOBUTTON: Option to select one of two items. | Map (Source) | The source location of the value to display in the PDF Field. | | Description | A description of the map. |
  1. In the Map (Source) field, select a map from the list you want to use to populate the PDF field.

In the example, the PDF Field (Employee Name) is populated with the Map (Source) Employee Full Name. On the PDF, the employee’s full name displays in the Employee Name field.

The Map (Source) list currently contains the set of maps needed for the CA and NY Wage state‑required forms. If the system recognizes a field on the uploaded PDF, it automatically assigns the map from the list to the PDF field. If the system does not recognize a field on the uploaded PDF, no map is assigned.

  1. After you select a map from the list, click Apply Changes.

Constructing the Layout

On the Layout tab, you can construct the form using the Field Type and PDF Fields lists. When a PDF uploads, the PDF Frame option displays in the Field Type list, and the PDF Fields list displays. The PDF Fields list contains the PDF fields mapped to custom form field types. You can view these PDF fields on the PDF Fields Map tab.

[Image: screenshot]

To construct the layout:

  1. In the Field Type list, drag and drop the PDF Frame to the layout on the right.

  2. In the PDF Fields list, drag and drop each entry list to the layout on the right.

The PDF fields available vary depending on the uploaded PDF.

  1. To view the form output after configuring the layout, click Preview Form.

  2. Click Save.

Viewing a Prehire Audit Log

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Viewing_Prehire_Audit_Log_OB.htm Path: OBE/Onboarding/Topics/Viewing_Prehire_Audit_Log_OB.htm

Viewing a Prehire Audit Log

The Prehire Audit action lets you see audit log information for new hires. For example, the user ID and date the employee was entered in the system. The search results display all prehires in the system for the client and employee IDs selected.

Note: Note: In some cases, employees can have multiple employee IDs in the system. For example, this occurs when an employee works for both a PEO (Professional Employer Organization) and ASO (Administrative Services Organization). It also occurs when an employee works for both an ASO (Employer 1) and another ASO (Employer 2). Therefore, there may be prehires in the list for multiple clients.

The following details how to view a prehire audit log with the Prehire Audit action.

To view a prehire audit log:

  1. In the Filters panel, you can filter the search results by client or by new hire information. To filter by client, enter a client ID number in the Client ID field or click the Client ID link to select a client ID number from a list. To filter by new hire information, enter a new hire’s last name, first name, Social Security Number, or employee ID in the New Hire field.

  2. Click Search. The Search Results panel displays the following information:

Field — Description

New Hire — The employee’s name.

SSN — The employee’s Social Security number. The system may hide the value shown depending on settings at the system or client level.

Employee ID — The employee’s ID number.

Prehire Create Date‑Time — The date and time the system created the prehire employee.

Prehire ID — The ID assigned to the prehire.

Status — The employment status.

Action — Audit: Lets you view the prehire audit log.

  1. In the Action column, click Audit. The File Audit form displays.

  2. To view log details, click the Log ID link.

  3. To close the audit log, click Close.
